Well, White didn’t care much about being left in the dewy grass, bewilderedly shaking first one paw, then another. But presently Eepersip came out of her burrow with Chippy. Seeing the patch of white, Eepersip thought the kitten was an exceptionally late bit of snow left on the grass. But no, it certainly had not been there the last time she had looked. And then she realized that it was altogether too late for any snow. Darting up to it, she found the little kitten, so snow-white, with the blue-grey eyes like little moonstones, gazing pitifully up at her. Because she thought the kitten had been a patch of snow, Eepersip named her Snowflake.

She took her, shivering with the wetness of the dew, into her burrow, and found, much to her surprise, that Chippy recognized her and sprang at her in great delight. The truth of the matter was that Chippy had often seen the kitten during his captivity, and had played many a happy game with her in his cage.

Caireen and Flitterveen had thought when they let the kitten go that Eepersip, seeing a new animal, would give them back Chippy. Not so! Eepersip would rather have two animals than one. Besides, she saw how dearly the two loved each other, and would not have parted them for anything now. The Brunios had been disappointed enough at losing Chippy—and now they had lost the kitten too!

===Chinese===
伊佩西普机警地四下一望，踮着脚溜出去，打开笼门，一把抓起奇皮，飞奔而去。它就这样获救了；伊佩西普真是快活极了！至于布鲁尼奥一家，发现花栗鼠没了，懊恼得很。他们暗中窥探，摸清了伊佩西普的一些古怪习性。于是一天清晨，他们抱着自家的小猫——双胞胎曾傻里傻气地按毛色给它起名叫“小白”——上到伊佩西普安家的那片原野，把它放在离伊佩西普的洞穴很近的地方，然后趁小猫还没找着他们，飞快跑开了。唉，小白可不大乐意被丢在露水淋淋的草里，晕头转向地先抖抖这只爪子，又抖抖那只。可不多时，伊佩西普就带着奇皮出了洞。望见那一小团白色，伊佩西普还当是草上一块散得格外晚的积雪。不对呀，上回看的时候它分明不在。她随即醒悟，眼下这时节，说什么也下不了雪了。她飞跑到跟前，发现了那只小猫，那样雪白，一双蓝灰色的眼睛像两粒小小的月亮石，正可怜巴巴地仰望着她。因为先入为主把它当成了雪，伊佩西普给它起名叫“雪花”。

她带着它——它被露水浸得直打颤——进了洞，又惊又喜地发现，奇皮认得它，欢天喜地朝它扑过去。实情是，奇皮被囚的那些日子常常见到这只小猫，还在笼子里同它玩过许多快活的游戏。

凯琳和弗利特文放走小猫时想，伊佩西普见了新的动物，总该把奇皮还给他们了吧。才不呢！伊佩西普宁可要两只动物，不要一只。何况，她看得出这一对彼此爱得有多深，如今说什么也不肯把它们拆散。布鲁尼奥一家丢了奇皮已经够失望——如今连小猫也丢了！

===Original===
It was now summer, and the Brunios—Mr. Brunio, Caireen, and Flitterveen—decided to climb Eiki-ennern Peak and try to get back their kitten. They wandered around a little while and at last came to a sunny place on the edge of the woods. It was a very grassy spot, all surrounded with blackberry-bushes just blossoming. Near its edge was Chippy asleep, and in the middle was Snowflake washing herself and playing with the dry oak-leaves that swirled about in the breeze. Snowflake started back a little at the sound of whispering voices, and then looked up. Eepersip was not there, for she had gone off to find some sweet bulbs and roots.

“Come on, White, dear!” called Caireen. But Snowflake did not know her old name since Eepersip had changed it.

“Here, Sugar-bowl!” said Mr. Brunio. (He had got rather used to calling her that, because she loved sugar and had a habit of pawing into the sugar-bowl to get it). This was a trifle more familiar, and she took a step forward.

“That’s the way!” said Flitterveen, encouraging her. “Come on!”

Snowflake took back the step.

===Chinese===
这时已是夏天，布鲁尼奥一家——布鲁尼奥先生、凯琳和弗利特文——决定登上艾基恩嫩峰，设法要回他们的小猫。他们转悠了一阵，终于来到林边一块向阳的地方。那是一片丰草之地，四周围着刚开花的黑莓丛。近处边上睡着奇皮，当中是雪花，正洗着脸，玩着微风中打着旋的干橡叶。雪花听见悄声说话，微微一惊，随后抬起头来。伊佩西普不在，她找甜球茎和草根去了。

“过来，小白，亲爱的！”凯琳喊道。可雪花不认得自己的旧名字了，自从伊佩西普给它改了名。

“喂，糖罐儿！”布鲁尼奥先生说。（他已经叫顺了口，因为它爱吃糖，惯于把爪子伸进糖罐里去掏。）这一声多少熟些，它向前挪了一步。

“就是这样！”弗利特文给它打气，“过来！”

雪花又把那一步收了回去。

===Original===
They grew impatient; yet some of their calls were encouraging that Snowflake could hardly resist creeping through the briers to their feet. Pretty soon she touched Chippy with her nose. He awoke, yawned two or three times, and, when he saw the people there, instantly pattered off into the woods. Now Snowflake sat very still, in spite of all their coaxings, for she knew that help was coming. And she didn’t have long to wait, for in a moment Eepersip came running up with Chippy in her arms. With a beautiful leap she cleared the briers and, taking up Snowflake, cleared them again and vanished into the woods.

Life for Chippy and Snowflake was great fun during the time when Mr. Brunio, sometimes with the twins, was coming up to the brier-patch and trying to entice Snowflake away. In the afternoons, though the two little animals were glad to stay in the brier-patch, Eepersip generally took them along with her when she went anywhere, for she thought that possibly Mr. Brunio might come up with an ax to chop down the briers. In this Eepersip proved wise, for, about the third day that Mr. Brunio and the children had been coming up Eiki-ennern Peak to rescue Snowflake, Mr. Brunio did bring an ax. But this time Eepersip had taken the two little animals out with her; they had gone exploring, finding sweet roots and brilliant berries.

===Chinese===
他们不耐烦起来；可他们的一声声呼唤那么诱人，雪花简直忍不住要钻过荆棘，蹭到他们脚边。不多时，它的鼻子碰到了奇皮。奇皮醒了，打了两三个哈欠，一见跟前有人，登时噔噔噔跑进树林。此刻雪花端坐不动，任他们百般哄诱，因为它知道救兵来了。它也没等多久，转眼间伊佩西普便抱着奇皮飞奔而来。她一个漂亮的腾跃越过荆棘，抱起雪花，又一跃而出，消失在树林里。

在布鲁尼奥先生——有时带着双胞胎——一趟趟上荆棘丛、想诱走雪花的那段日子，奇皮和雪花的日子快活极了。不过一到下午，尽管两个小家伙乐意待在荆棘丛里，伊佩西普出门时多半还是把它们带在身边，因为她想，布鲁尼奥先生说不定会抡着斧头来砍荆棘。事实证明她想得周到，因为布鲁尼奥先生和孩子们上艾基恩嫩峰来救雪花大约到第三天，他果真带了斧头来。可这一回，伊佩西普把两个小家伙带出门去了；他们一路探察，寻甜草根和亮晶晶的浆果。

===Original===
Eepersip spent that summer in continual fits of dancing, laughing, and merriment. She had never before been so happy. Every day she felt as though she loved the animals, birds, and butterflies—everything of Nature—more than the day before. She loved to see the same birds coming back this year. Above all she loved the delicate butterflies with wings of all colours. She would lie in the meadow for hours and watch what was happening. She could imagine miniature cities in the air, and saw little butterflies and birds constantly going and coming from them. There were cities on the ground too, where orchestras of grasshoppers and crickets played in the grass.

She sometimes made up words for her melodies—little songs of Nature. She would sing them over and over, sometimes ringingly, sometimes in a murmur.

> Buttercups are smiling
 

 To see the butterflies
 

 Feathering so softly,
 

 Rainbowing the skies.  …

> The wind is snowing butterflies,
 

 Fairy golden showers;
 

 Misty the air with dancing wings;
 

 The sun is raining flowers.

She told the deer that *she* felt like a butterfly, and that the wind was snowing *her* when she danced. And then she gave them handfuls of lush grass.

===Chinese===
那个夏天，伊佩西普是在一阵接一阵的舞蹈、欢笑和嬉闹里度过的。她从未这样快活过。每一天，她都觉得比起前一天，自己更爱那些走兽、飞鸟和蝴蝶——更爱大自然的一切。她爱看同一批鸟儿今年又飞回来。她最爱的是那些翅膀色彩缤纷的娇美蝴蝶。她会在草地上一躺几个钟头，看眼前发生的一切。她能想象空中的微型城市，看见小蝴蝶和小鸟从城里不停地进进出出。地上也有城市，蚱蜢和蟋蟀的乐队就在草丛里演奏。

她有时为自己的曲子编词——一支支大自然的小歌。她一遍遍地唱，有时清亮，有时低吟。

> 毛茛花儿笑眯眯，
 

 瞧那小蝴蝶，
 

 翅羽扑得轻轻，
 

 把天空飞成彩虹。……

> 风把蝴蝶当雪片撒下，
 

 恍若仙子的金色骤雨；
 

 舞翅翩翩，空中一片迷蒙，
 

 太阳洒落的尽是花。

她告诉鹿群，*她*觉得自己就是一只蝴蝶，她起舞的时候，风里飘落的雪片就是*她*。说完，她捧给它们一把把鲜嫩的青草。

---

===Original===
At the end of that first winter Mrs. Eigleen began to feel ill. No one knew what was the matter with her. She spent the spring in continual weeping and hysterics. Towards the summer she began to feel seriously ill. They had had several different doctors in to see her, but none of them could find out exactly what the matter was, for she refused to tell anyone anything, even though she said she herself knew. One afternoon she called her friends round her and bade them take her over to the meadow, where they would surely see Eepersip. They took her out, but never a trace of Eepersip did they see. And Mrs. Eigleen kept on having her fits of weeping all through the summer, even more frequently than before.

Now, by this time Mrs. Eigleen, her husband, and all her neighbours had found out that Eepersip had taken White away from the Brunios; for once they had been out in the field and seen Eepersip. She was crowned with a wreath on which butterflies were clustering in bunches, like grapes; and Chippy and Snowflake were frolicking about her. The Eigleens, the Ikkisfields, and the Wraspanes went down the meadow and to Mr. Brunio’s house (for some of them knew the Brunios and recognized the kitten), and he related his adventures. That very afternoon they went back to the meadow and chased Eepersip, but they couldn’t catch her, for she took up Snowflake and Chippy and mounted a doe, who bore them off like the wind.

The next day they tried again: It was dawn, and Eepersip was lying in the centre of the meadow with Snowflake and Chippy by her side. She had had her breakfast, but she lay on the grass watching the sun rise and send away the shadows to right and left, flushing the sky with delicate pink and yellow. The deer were still laying down. Eepersip heard a sound of voices talking, followed by a roar of laughter; and instantly, of course, she grew suspicious. She heard: “Mr. Wraspane, will you come with me?” “Certainly, Mr. Eigleen.” “Mrs. Ikkisfield, you come with me. We are the ones that are not so skilled in slyness. We will go up on the hill and guard there.” “All right, Mrs. Eigleen.”

===Chinese===
第一个冬天将尽时，伊格琳太太觉得身子不适起来。谁也不知道她得了什么病。整个春天，她都在不断的哭泣和歇斯底里里度过。入夏之后，她病得更重了。他们请了好几位不同的医生来看，可谁也查不出究竟，因为她什么都不肯对人说，尽管她讲自己心里明白。一天下午，她把朋友们唤到身边，请他们带她去草地上，说在那儿准能见到伊佩西普。他们带她去了，可伊佩西普的影子也没见着。整个夏天，伊格琳太太的哭泣一阵接着一阵，发作得比先前更勤了。

到这时，伊格琳太太、她丈夫和所有邻居都已查明，伊佩西普把小白从布鲁尼奥家带走了；因为他们有一回在原野上见过伊佩西普。她头上戴着花冠，蝴蝶一簇簇聚在上面，像成串的葡萄；奇皮和雪花在她四周嬉闹。伊格琳夫妇、伊基斯菲尔德夫妇和拉斯潘夫妇下了草地，来到布鲁尼奥先生家（他们中间有几位认得布鲁尼奥家，认出了那只小猫），他把自己的种种遭遇讲了一遍。就在那天下午，他们回到草地追伊佩西普，可没能抓住她，因为她抱起雪花和奇皮，跨上一头母鹿，那鹿载着他们乘风而去。

第二天他们又试了一回。那是黎明时分，伊佩西普躺在草地中央，雪花和奇皮守在身旁。她已用过早饭，正躺在草上看太阳升起，把影子向左右两边驱散，给天空染上淡淡的粉与黄。鹿还卧着。伊佩西普听见一阵说话声，跟着是一阵哄笑；她自然立刻起了疑心。她听见：“拉斯潘先生，你跟我来吗？”“当然，伊格琳先生。”“伊基斯菲尔德太太，你跟我来。我们是不擅长耍滑的。我们上小山上去把守。”“好的，伊格琳太太。”

===Original===
As Eepersip lay there in the field, two men broke out of the woods right near where she was lying. She sprang to her feet, caught Chippy and Snowflake in her arms, and ran. Before she could snatch up the two little animals, Mr. Eigleen was just able to seize her dress as she darted by him. But, of course, the fern that he caught hold of came out in his hand, and she ran along toward the hill—a foolish thing to do, for she had just heard that there were two people guarding it. Still, that was just about the only thing that she could do, for the other two, Mr. Ikkisfield and Mrs. Wraspane, had also come out of the woods and blocked her path.

Eepersip fled up on the hill and nearly ran into Mrs. Eigleen and Mrs. Ikkisfield. Mrs. Eigleen caught hold of Snowflake’s tail, and Mrs. Ikkisfield stepped in front of Eepersip, who dodged desperately to one side, releasing her hold on the kitten to prevent its being injured.

But Eepersip was not going to give up her pet just yet. She sped down the hill, knowing that the others would soon be going to give the kitten back to the Brunios. Through the big field she ran, with Chippy clinging to her hair—down the other side of the slope to the river, along its banks, across the small bridge ’way down, and back to Mr. Brunio’s house on the other side.

===Chinese===
伊佩西普正躺在原野上，两个男人从近旁的树林里冲了出来。她一跃而起，把奇皮和雪花揽进怀里，撒腿便跑。她还没来得及抓起两个小家伙，伊格琳先生堪堪在她掠过身侧时抓住了她的裙子。可他抓住的那片蕨叶自然留在了他手里；她径直朝小山跑去——这可犯傻了，因为她刚听见有两个人在那儿把守。不过那也差不多是她唯一能走的路，因为另外两位，伊基斯菲尔德先生和拉斯潘太太，也已出了树林，堵住了她的道。

伊佩西普逃上山坡，险些撞进伊格琳太太和伊基斯菲尔德太太怀里。伊格琳太太一把抓住雪花的尾巴，伊基斯菲尔德太太挡在伊佩西普面前；伊佩西普拼命向一旁一闪，松开小猫，免得它受伤。

可伊佩西普还不打算就这么交出自己的爱宠。她飞奔下山，知道他们很快就要把小猫还给布鲁尼奥家了。她带着紧攀住她头发的奇皮，穿过大原野，下了坡的另一侧到河边，沿着河岸一路跑下去，过了远远的那座小桥，绕回河对岸布鲁尼奥先生家。

===Original===
Eepersip looked all about her for some place of concealment. No one was in sight. Along the side of the house there was a forest of blackberry-bushes, which extended several yards and was unusually dense and towering. The luscious black-and-purple fruit was ripening, making it look even more sheltering and mysterious. This patch was in such a position that it had to be passed to the front door, which was really on the side of the house. Eepersip was pleased to find such a convenient place. She sampled the berries with satisfaction, always taking pains to see that no one was coming. Then she wriggled inside and waited.

Presently they all came along, Mrs. Ikkisfield holding Snowflake. Eepersip had ready in her hand a little sharp-pointed stick. She pushed it through a tiny hole in Mrs. Ikkisfield’s stocking. It hurt! Mrs. Ikkisfield gave a little shriek of pain and dropped Snowflake, who instantly put her paw on a small fern which she saw—she couldn’t see Eepersip, but the fern was very familiar!—and was pulled by Eepersip into the bush. When the people saw that, they knew, of course, who was near.

Eepersip started to crawl through the bush and out the other side; but she heard Mr. Eigleen whispering to the others to go around and stop her. While they were watching, Mr. Brunio, who had joined them, went back into the house and fetched a net. It was woven of coarse, thick ropes, but the meshes were quite small enough to hold the kitten, and almost Chippy himself. (Mr. Brunio had once been a fisherman; he had retired, but he still had many nets with meshes of various sizes.) They put this net over the bush and pegged it down firmly, driving the pegs with the head of an ax which Mr. Brunio brought out. Then they retreated to a distance and watched.

===Chinese===
伊佩西普环顾四周，想找个藏身的地方。一个人影也不见。房子一侧有一片黑莓丛的森林，绵延好几码，长得密不透风、高耸过人。黑紫色的甜美果实正成熟，让这片荆棘愈发显得荫蔽而神秘。这丛荆棘的位置正卡在去前门的必经之路上——那扇前门其实开在房子侧面。伊佩西普为找到这样一处便当的地方而高兴。她满意地尝了尝莓子，时刻留神不叫人撞见。随后她扭着身子钻了进去，静候。

不多时他们一行人到了，伊基斯菲尔德太太抱着雪花。伊佩西普手里早握好一根尖尖的小棍。她把棍子从伊基斯菲尔德太太长袜上的一个小孔里戳过去。好疼！伊基斯菲尔德太太痛得轻叫一声，松手丢了雪花；雪花立刻把爪子搭上一小株蕨草——它望不见伊佩西普，可这株蕨草熟悉得很！——被伊佩西普一把拉进了荆棘丛。人们一见这情形，自然知道附近是谁了。

伊佩西普开始从荆棘丛里往外爬，想从另一侧钻出去；可她听见伊格琳先生低声吩咐众人绕过去堵她。他们监视着，这时赶上来的布鲁尼奥先生回屋取来一张网。网是用粗厚的绳编的，可网眼小得很，足以兜住小猫，几乎连奇皮自己也钻不过。（布鲁尼奥先生从前当过渔夫；如今歇了业，可家里还存着许多网眼大小不一的网。）他们把网罩在荆棘丛上，用木桩钉牢，拿布鲁尼奥先生搬出来的斧头背把桩砸实。随后他们退到远处守着。

===Original===
Eepersip began working at the pegs; and the chipmunk and the kitten to dig at the base of each so that she might be able to pull them up more easily. The pegs were really too big for Eepersip’s little hands to manage comfortably, but she didn’t think of comfort in such danger as this, and she worked boldly at the pegs with her nimble fingers. After she had got two or three up, the Eigleens and their friends came forward, took those pegs, and put them down more firmly than before, so that Eepersip had to begin all over.

Although the people who were trying to capture Eepersip and the kitten were naturally becoming very hungry, they didn’t like to leave the bush unguarded. But Mr. Brunio (who was exceptionally hungry) said that he had many more such nets, and that they could spread them all over the bush and hurriedly get luncheon. If they put them down very firmly, and ate rather fast, there wouldn’t be much chance of Eepersip’s escaping before they could get out again. So they spread four more nets over the bush and went in.

Now was Eepersip’s chance, and she worked harder than ever. At last, with the aid of Chippy and Snowflake, who helped a lot by digging around the pegs, Eepersip got out of the first net and began tugging at the second. She didn’t try to dig up the pegs of this one: instead, all three tried to dig under it, and at last they had made a hole large enough for Eepersip to crawl through. The fibres of the third net were rather rotten, so that Eepersip tore it easily. Each peg of the fourth and fifth nets came up at one mighty yank; Mr. Brunio and his helpers had put the last nets down in a great hurry, in order to get their luncheon. Then Eepersip, with the two little animals, fled from that dread place, across the bridge and back to the meadow, where she found a sheltered spot and slept.

===Chinese===
伊佩西普动手拔桩；花栗鼠和小猫则在每个桩脚下刨土，好让她拔得省力些。那些桩对伊佩西普的小手实在太大，捏不舒服，可在这样的险境里她哪还想什么舒服，只管用灵巧的手指大胆地干。她拔起两三根之后，伊格琳夫妇和他们的朋友们走上前来，把桩重新钉得比先前更牢，害得伊佩西普只得从头再来。

这些一心想捉住伊佩西普和小猫的人自然渐渐饿极了，可谁也不愿让荆棘丛没人看守。倒是布鲁尼奥先生（他饿得尤其厉害）说，他这样的网还多着呢，不妨把荆棘丛全罩上，赶紧回去吃午饭。只要钉得牢牢的、吃得快些，趁他们再出来之前，伊佩西普逃掉的余地不大。于是他们又罩上四张网，进屋去了。

这下轮到伊佩西普了，她干得比先前更卖力。终于，靠奇皮和雪花帮忙——它们在桩脚刨土，帮了大忙——伊佩西普钻出了第一张网，开始拉扯第二张。这一张她不去拔桩：三个一齐从网下刨，终于刨出一个刚够伊佩西普爬过的洞。第三张网的绳索颇朽，伊佩西普一撕就开。第四第五张网的桩，一使劲便拔了起来；布鲁尼奥先生和他的帮手们为了赶午饭，最后几张钉得慌慌张张。随后伊佩西普带着两个小家伙逃出那可怕的所在，过了桥，回到草地，寻了个避风的角落睡了。

---

===Original===
The three families were much disgusted with themselves for not catching Eepersip and the kitten; but they promised Mr. Brunio that they would try again. There followed a week of rainy weather, during which they made no attempts, but laid plans. As soon as the weather cleared, they tried one of these plans; and Snowflake had a narrow escape.

It was in the middle of the night. The families found Eepersip, with the two little animals, asleep on the meadow. They took the kitten from her arms. But as they did so, Eepersip herself woke up, screamed loudly, and rushed after them. Then they turned and came after her; and Eepersip was bold enough and angry enough not to run until she had rescued Snowflake. She came right up to her parents and seized Snowflake from the arms of the horrified Mrs. Eigleen. Then Eepersip ran—and fast, too!

When she came to the edge of the woods she made straight for a great pine. This tree she had climbed about in often, and she knew its every limb. In pitch dark she could find all its branches, and sometimes she trusted to her memory rather recklessly. She was as sure of this tree as of the ground, even at night. She loved it—she called it *her* tree. A tree was, perhaps, not the easiest place of concealment, but Eepersip thought that in this way she would not have to run all over the meadow in the dark—and she was very tired after her usual all-day playing.

===Chinese===
三家人为没抓住伊佩西普和小猫懊恼不已；可他们向布鲁尼奥先生保证还要再试。随后下了一个星期的雨，其间他们没有动手，只是定计。天一放晴，他们便试了其中一计；雪花险些遭殃。

那是半夜。几家人发现伊佩西普搂着两个小家伙睡在草地上。他们把小猫从她怀里抱走。可这一抱，伊佩西普自己醒了，放声尖叫，追了上来。他们转身对付她；伊佩西普又勇又怒，不救回雪花绝不肯跑。她径直冲到父母跟前，从吓呆了的伊格琳太太怀里夺回雪花。然后她跑了——还跑得飞快！

跑到林边，她直奔一棵大松树。这树她常爬，每一条枝干都认得。漆黑里她也摸得着所有的枝桠，有时简直全凭记忆，胆子大得可以。对这棵树，她熟得像熟脚下的地，哪怕在夜里。她爱它——她叫它*她的*树。藏在一棵树上，或许不是最稳妥的去处，可伊佩西普觉得，这样便用不着摸黑满草地乱跑——照例玩了一整天，她也实在累了。

===Original===
There were no branches lower than seven feet up. Eepersip made one leap, caught hold of a branch, and swung herself up on to it. From that branch she mounted higher and higher until she reached the very top. It swayed gloriously, even under her light weight, but it didn’t creak as if about to break. She sat up in the high crotch and looked at the people so far below, through a mass of black needles and a mighty thickness of strong limbs. It was a wonderful night. The sky was spangled with stars of vivid silver. Not a cloud was to be seen except on the western horizon, where a bank was piling up rapidly, silhouetted against the deep ultramarine of the sky, across which the Milky Way made a path of radiance. Eepersip, looking down among the powerful limbs, felt as if she were part of the familiar tree.

Poor Chippy and Snowflake were tired and sleepy after what they had just been through. Eepersip murmured kind words to them, while she thought of all that she had been through herself. She was not in the least dizzy, but she was tired, and she knew that she must not go to sleep up there.

Then she saw that Mr. Eigleen had started to climb the great tree. He got about halfway up and then stopped. She remembered the place: it had been difficult for her, too. There was not a limb where he could put out a foot and step on it; the next one was at the level of his neck. The question was, how could he get on to this limb? He didn’t have the strength to pull himself up to it the way Eepersip had done. He tried for a long time; but his caution proved too much for him. At last, in despair, he descended; and the people went away, leaving Eepersip in peace.

As soon as they were well out of sight and hearing, Eepersip came down in a series of leaps from limb to limb. At length she got to the bottom, where the last limb was seven feet from the ground. She braced herself as firmly as she could on this, and then she jumped. It was a marvellous jump in the dark, and she landed on the ground unhurt, though very tired and covered with bits of bark. “My, that was a dreary adventure!” she said sleepily, as she crawled off to find a place to sleep.

===Chinese===
七英尺以下没有枝桠。伊佩西普纵身一跃，抓住一根枝干，一荡翻了上去。从那根枝干她又越攀越高，直到树梢。树梢悠悠地摇曳，即便承着她轻轻的分量也摇得壮美，却不咯吱作响，不像要断的样子。她端坐在高高的枝桠间，透过黑压压的针叶和层层粗壮的枝干，望着下面远得小小的人们。那是个奇妙的夜晚。天空缀满鲜明银色的星。除了西天边上——那里正迅速堆起一带云，衬在深群青色的夜空上——四下没有一丝云，银河横贯，铺成一条光的路。伊佩西普俯望坚实的枝干之间，觉得自己已成了这棵亲爱大树的一部分。

可怜的奇皮和雪花刚经历了那一切，又累又困。伊佩西普对它们柔声低语，心里想着自己经历过的种种。她一点儿也不头晕，只是累，而且她知道，在上面万万睡不得。

这时她看见伊格琳先生开始爬这棵大树。他爬到大约一半便停住了。她记得那个地方：当初对她也难。那里没有一条能落脚的枝干；下一条已齐到他脖子那么高。问题是，他怎么上到那条枝上去？他可没有伊佩西普那样的力气把自己拽上去。他试了许久；可他的谨慎终究压倒了他。他终于绝望地退了下去；人们走了，把安宁留给了伊佩西普。

等他们走得望不见、听不见了，伊佩西普便从枝到枝一连串跳跃着下来了。最后她到了底下，末一根枝干离地七英尺。她在枝上尽力撑稳身子，纵身一跳。黑地里这一跳妙极了，她安然落在地上，虽又累又沾了一身碎树皮，却毫发无伤。“哎呀，这一场冒险可真闷人！”她睡意朦胧地说着，爬开去找睡觉的地方了。

---

===Original===
Eepersip saw no more of the Brunios or her parents, and she decided that they had given up chasing her and Snowflake for the winter—a winter which she and her two friends spent undisturbed, playing with the leaves and shadows.

It was spring—spring before the third summer that Eepersip was to spend wild—and the golden sun melted away the last patches of snow from off the bare rocks and from round the pool, where it had lodged between them. It was warm, although a wind was blowing—the delicious wind of spring. This marvellous spring air made her blood course quickly. She felt extremely happy and dancy. Her body seemed to her lighter than ever, in spite of its strength. Her spirit was so joyous that she could not express it in action; she had to let part of it out in song. But song, however light and happy, could not quite express Eepersip’s feeling. She danced, and she sang, and she leaped aloft for joy.

As the season advanced, she crowned herself with sweet-smelling flowers, and the butterflies came and lit on them. She went up the pool wearing her fluttering crown, and there she saw the flowers that had come to bloom. There was iris, purple and gold—huge blossoms which reminded Eepersip of the ocean as she had seen it, so far away, on the first day of her wandering. In a soft bed of green moss she found a little pink-and-white flower that she didn’t know, bell-shaped and very fragrant. There were wild-rose-buds there, too, and never had Eepersip seen so many butterflies as were on those roses. They bordered the tiny beach, mingled with the tenderly uncurling green ferns. The delicate red leaf-buds on the maple-trees were now developing into tiny emerald leaves. And there were ever so many other treasures of Nature there.

===Chinese===
伊佩西普再没见过布鲁尼奥一家，也没见过父母，她想他们已经放弃在冬天里追她和雪花了——这个冬天，她和两个朋友过得安然无扰，玩着落叶和影子。

春天来了——伊佩西普野居的第三个夏天之前的那个春天——金色的太阳把光秃岩石上和池塘四周最后几片残雪化尽了，雪就嵌在石缝之间。天暖了，虽然吹着风——那是春天可人的风。这奇妙的春气叫她的血涌得飞快。她快活得直想跳舞。她觉得身子前所未有地轻盈，却又不失力气。她的心欢畅得行动都盛不下，只好放出一半来化作歌。可歌，无论多轻快，也唱不尽伊佩西普的心意。她舞着，唱着，欢畅得向空中高高跃起。

时序推进，她给自己戴上香花编的花冠，蝴蝶飞来停在上面。她戴着颤巍巍的花冠走上池塘，看见那里新开的花。有鸢尾，紫的、金的——硕大的花朵让伊佩西普想起漫游第一天她远远望见的大海。在一床柔软的绿苔里，她发现一朵不认识的粉白小花，钟形，极香。那里还有野玫瑰蕾，伊佩西普从没见过哪里的玫瑰上有这么多蝴蝶。它们镶着小沙滩的边，同柔柔舒展的绿蕨相间。枫树上绛红的嫩叶芽，如今正长成小小的翡翠叶。那里还有数不尽的大自然的其他珍宝。

===Original===
Eepersip played little happy games with all the creatures of the field. One game she played with the crickets. A cricket would be hiding in a certain place, and when Eepersip danced by he would buzz out of the grass into her face; she would pretend to be startled and would run from the spot. She played another game with the grasshoppers. One would be hiding, and Eepersip would come dancing by with her eyes shut. Then the grasshopper would whirr out of the grass and alight on her hand. When she opened her eyes she would shake her hand and try to get rid of him, all in fun, of course. Then she played two lovely games with the happy butterflies. She would let a butterfly alight on her hand, to which she would then give a violent jerk, so that the butterfly was sent sailing into the air; then, without a motion of the wings, he would come sailing back to Eepersip’s hand. This they would do again and again. When she tired of this game, Eepersip would crown herself with the sweetest flowers she could find, and then flocks of butterflies would try to alight on her wreath as she danced. There were never enough flowers for all of them; some were always fluttering around Eepersip’s head, trying to find a nestling-place, and others were safely folded in the blossoms.

One of the thrilling hours of Eepersip’s happy life that summer was when she lay in the meadow watching the sky and all the swallows circling. Snowflake and Chippy were frolicking gayly in the short, dry grass, chasing leaves. Now Chippy snuggled up to Eepersip. Snowflake kept on playing; she was crouched on her little white belly, playing with a dry brown leaf, and when it drifted beyond her reach she would spring after it. Eepersip watched her in a dreamy way. Now Snowflake cast the dead leaf away, having torn it to shreds, and played with other things. Sometimes she would rear herself up into the air; at other times she would run with little tripping steps over to Eepersip, as if something had frightened her; again she would rush round and round Eepersip in a wide circle, and finally she would settle down to play with another dead leaf. It made Eepersip glad to hear the kitten’s little pattering feet on the grass; she knew how madly Snowflake was frolicking, but she did not share in the play—instead, with a dreamy happiness, she watched the sky.

===Chinese===
伊佩西普同原野上所有的生灵玩着快活的小游戏。有一种是同蟋蟀玩的。一只蟋蟀藏在某处，伊佩西普舞过时，它便嗡地从草里扑到她脸上；她就假装吓着了，从那儿逃开。另一种同蚱蜢玩。一只蚱蜢藏着，伊佩西普闭着眼舞过去。蚱蜢便呼地从草里飞起，落在她手上。她一睁眼，便甩甩手要摆脱它——当然全是玩闹。她还同快活的蝴蝶玩两种可爱的游戏。她让一只蝴蝶落在手上，猛地把手一抖，蝴蝶便被送上半空；随后它不扇一下翅膀，滑翔着回到伊佩西普手上。他们这样玩了一遍又一遍。这个游戏玩腻了，伊佩西普就给自己戴上寻得到的最香的花，她起舞时，一群群蝴蝶便争着往她的花冠上落。花从来不够它们全落上去；总有几只绕着伊佩西普的头顶翻飞，想找个安身处，另一些则稳稳收拢在花朵里。

那年夏天伊佩西普快活日子里最叫人心醉的时辰之一，是她躺在草地上望着天空和满天盘旋的燕子。雪花和奇皮在短而干的草里欢腾，追着叶子跑。这时奇皮偎到伊佩西普身边。雪花还在玩；它伏在雪白的小肚子上，玩一片棕色的枯叶，叶子飘出它的爪距，它便扑过去。伊佩西普梦一般望着它。后来雪花把撕成碎片的枯叶抛开，玩别的去了。它时而向空中直直立起；时而迈着碎步跑到伊佩西普跟前，像被什么吓着了；时而又围着伊佩西普兜一个大圈，跑了一圈又一圈；末了又伏下来玩另一片枯叶。听见小猫的爪子在草上嗒嗒轻响，伊佩西普心里欢喜；她知道雪花玩得多疯，可她不掺和进去——只带着梦一样的幸福，望着天。

===Original===
Another day in this summer was even happier. It was in July, and Eepersip was lying in a part of the meadow where there weren’t many deer, so that the grass was long, soft, and green instead of stiff and short. Snowflake and Chippy were frolicking around in it, but again Eepersip was not thinking of them. She was thinking of the swallows that flew over her, and the way the sun shone on their breasts, making them glitter like silver. The crickets were chirping, and the grasshoppers were accompanying them, and they were both very happy. The frogs croaked bass songs from the pool—the cool, green frogs! The birds were singing merrily, and the butterflies passed over Eepersip’s head in flocks—butterflies of white and purple and blue and yellow, little ones of copper-green, and big ones of orange and red. Some of them flew with short, quick flirts of the wings, others with long strokes which swept them through the air. The gauzy dragonflies, too, flew over her. Everything thrilled Eepersip’s happy, tireless eyes.

The bees hummed their way low over the long green grass, and Chippy and Snowflake leaped high in the air when they passed. Eepersip had taught the two little animals not to catch the creatures of the field, and before long all the birds loved Snowflake—something that few kittens have yet attained. But Snowflake and Chippy liked to pretend to catch the bees, and sometimes they went so far as to hold them: on the ground with their paws, very gently, not hurting them at all. Snowflake and Chippy lay in the grass, reaching and touching anything that took their fancy. When the wind blew they would leap up at the clover-blossoms that nodded. They played hide-and-seek, leaping over the grasses and chasing each other in and out of their hiding-places. The long grass offered a splendid place of concealment. Chippy would scurry behind a big sheltering tuft, seeming to Snowflake to have vanished in midair. Snowflake would poke about and run in such bewildering circles that it tired them looking at each other. Soon they would get so mixed up that they wouldn’t know which of them was supposed to be hiding, and it often happened that they were both hiding at the same time, or both searching.

This was for Eepersip the happiest of that summer’s hours in the field. Something fresh and fragrant in the air made breathing a delight; it almost lifted her off the ground, and she let forth a glorious burst of song.

===Chinese===
这年夏天还有一天更快活。那是七月里，伊佩西普躺在草地上一处鹿不多的地方，所以草长得又长又软又绿，不似别处又硬又短。雪花和奇皮在草里嬉闹，可伊佩西普又没想着它们。她想的是头顶飞过的燕子，想阳光照在它们胸脯上，把它们照得像银子一样闪亮。蟋蟀在叫，蚱蜢伴着奏，它们都快活。青蛙在池塘里唱着低音的歌——清凉的绿青蛙！鸟儿欢唱，一群群蝴蝶从伊佩西普头顶掠过——白的、紫的、蓝的、黄的蝴蝶，铜绿色的小蝶，橙红的大蝶。有的振翅短短急急地飞，有的长长一掠，扫过天空。轻纱似的蜻蜓也从她头顶飞过。一切都让伊佩西普那双快活不知疲倦的眼睛激动不已。

蜜蜂嗡嗡地低低飞过长长的绿草，飞过时奇皮和雪花便高高跃起。伊佩西普教过两个小家伙不许捉原野上的生灵，不久所有的鸟儿都爱上了雪花——这份荣耀，没几只小猫得到过。可雪花和奇皮爱假装捉蜜蜂，有时索性真的按住它们：用爪子把它们按在地上，轻轻的，一点儿也不伤着。雪花和奇皮躺在草里，够着、碰着一切入眼的东西。风吹来，它们便朝点头的车轴草花蹦起来。它们玩捉迷藏，跃过草丛，在各自的藏身处间追来追去。长草提供了绝妙的藏身之所。奇皮嗖地窜到一大丛蔽身的草后，在雪花眼里就像凭空消失了。雪花扒来扒去，兜着叫人眼晕的圈子，瞧得彼此都累了。不一会儿它们就闹糊涂了，闹不清该谁藏谁找，常常两个一齐藏，或者两个一齐找。

对伊佩西普来说，这是那年夏天在原野上最最快活的时辰。空气里有什么清新芬芳的东西，叫呼吸都成了享受；它几乎把她托离了地面，她放声唱出嘹亮的一串歌。

===Original===
It was a cold, frisky day in October, and Eepersip, even in her warm coat, had to keep moving. Snowflake and Chippy were frolicking and playing games with each other. Eepersip had taught them how to shake hands, and this they were practising. A leaf had settled on Snowflake’s head like a brown crown, and she was trying vigorously to get it off. But no, the leaf was curled firmly around her small, dainty ears. She got wilder and wilder in her amusing efforts. She dashed round and round. She reached after it with her forepaws. All in vain! She could not get rid of that persistent leaf. But there! a gust of cold wind sent it flying from her head, to be instantly lost in a whirl of others which the wind had started up. Snowflake dashed among them madly, and played with them, trying to catch them all at the same time. But at last she stopped her foolish efforts and came quietly back to rest.

In November the first snow of the winter fell. The flakes came thick and fast, like white and silver butterflies dancing, flying. Eepersip took them in her hands and noticed how each flake had its own shape, which was never found again. During that day in October Snowflake had worn a brown crown of a dead leaf, but now she wore a white one. The snow didn’t show much on the white fur of the kitten, but Chippy’s autumn brown was soon covered with a mossy blanket of it. The flakes whirled down thicker and faster than ever, and Chippy tried to jump at them all. The playmates could hide in the snow now, for if they got far enough apart they couldn’t see each other. They tried to capture the snowflakes, but they found that this made their paws even wetter.

In spite of all this merriment, Eepersip had a slightly sad feeling in her heart. The night before, she had seen the sea; and it had looked *so* glorious that she felt as if—as if she would like to go to it. She loved the meadow so much that this would be almost impossible for her. Yet she knew that, in spite of her love for the meadow, her longing for the sea would grow, and that one day she must leave her present home. All this made her rather sad. But she tried to be happy—to share the joy of her two little friends, and the joy of having the little fairy things come whirling down upon her. She played all day in the meadow with her friends, and when the evening fell they went back to the burrow and slept in peace till morning.

===Chinese===
那是十月里一个寒冽而活泼的日子，伊佩西普即便裹着暖和的大衣，也得不停地动。雪花和奇皮相互嬉闹做游戏。伊佩西普教过它们握手，它们正练着呢。一片叶子落在雪花头上，像一顶棕色的王冠，它使劲想甩掉。可不行，叶子牢牢卷在它小巧玲珑的耳朵上。它那些滑稽的努力越发张狂。它一圈圈狂转。它伸前爪去够。全白费！它甩不掉那片固执的叶子。可是瞧！一阵冷风把它从它头上刮跑，转眼卷进风刮起来的漫天落叶里。雪花疯了似的冲进落叶堆，同它们玩，想一下子全抓住。可最后它歇了这傻劲，安安静静回来歇着了。

十一月里，这年冬天的头一场雪落了下来。雪片又密又急，像白色和银色的蝴蝶在舞蹈、在飞。伊佩西普把它们接在手心，留意到每一片都有自己的形状，而这形状再不会重现。十月里那一天，雪花戴过枯叶的棕色王冠，如今它戴上了一顶白的。雪落在小猫的白毛上显不出什么，可奇皮一身秋棕不久便盖上了苔藓似的雪毯。雪片越旋越密、越落越急，奇皮想一下子扑住所有的雪片。两个玩伴如今可以藏进雪里了，只要隔得够远，彼此就望不见。它们想捉住雪花，却发现这样爪子湿得更厉害。

尽管有这么些欢喜，伊佩西普心里却有一丝淡淡的忧伤。前一晚，她望见了大海；它看上去*那样*壮丽，她觉得——觉得恨不得奔它而去。她太爱这片草地，这于她几乎不可能。可她知道，尽管爱着草地，她对海的渴望会长起来，总有一天她必须离开眼下的家。这一切让她有些忧伤。可她努力快活——去分享两个小朋友的欢喜，分享那些小仙子似的东西打着旋落到她身上的欢喜。她在草地上同朋友们玩了一整天，暮色四合时，他们回到洞穴，安然一觉睡到天明。

===Original===
In this way the winter passed. Every evening at sunset Eepersip would go over to the edge of the meadow and gaze long upon the sea, with the brilliant sunset colours reflected in it. And each time she looked it seemed so beautiful, so beautiful! and each time she tried to go to it, it seemed as if the ground of the meadow was a great magnet to her feet.

The spring came, and with it the flowers and leaves.

One night Eepersip woke up to find the full moon as if hanging in the sky. A few faint stars could be seen. She tried to go to sleep, but could not. At last she got up from her bed of moss. The dew lay thick on the grass, which slushed deliciously against her bare feet. All entranced with the beauty of the night, she ran lightly over to the spot where she often had a view of the sea. And she beheld it with the full moon reflected in it—a globe of soft silver, shimmering and quivering in the unstill waters. This time it was too much for Eepersip. She could stand it no longer—her heart gave way. She decided that the next morning she would satisfy her longing.

And so, just after dawn, she left her beautiful home in the field and journeyed toward the ocean. She went to the edge of the meadow with a herd of deer daintily tripping after her. She turned and cried: “Goodbye, O deer! for probably I shall never see you again.” She kissed the ground of the meadow, and she wept to think that she was leaving it; but she knew that her love for the sea had become greater than her love for the meadow. And then she went away—sadly, yet happy at the prospect of a new and beautiful life by the foaming, churning rocks and the white-capped waves.

===Chinese===
冬天就这样过去了。每天傍晚日落时分，伊佩西普都会走到草地边上，久久凝望那映着绚烂落日色彩的大海。每回望去，它都显得那样美，那样美！每回她想朝它走去，脚下的草地却仿佛成了一块巨大的磁石，吸住她的双脚。

春天来了，花与叶一同来了。

一天夜里，伊佩西普醒来，只见一轮满月仿佛悬在天上。几颗淡淡的星依稀可见。她想再睡，却睡不着。终于，她从苔藓床上起身。露水浓浓地压在草上，蹭着她赤裸的双脚，凉丝丝的好不舒服。她完全醉心于这夜的美丽，轻快地跑到那处常能望见大海的地方。她看见了它——满月映在海中——一团柔柔的银球，在荡漾不定的水面里闪烁、颤动。这一次伊佩西普受不住了。她再也支撑不住——她的心屈服了。她决定，第二天早晨就去满足自己的渴望。

就这样，天刚亮，她离开了原野上美丽的家，向大海进发。她走到草地边上，一群鹿踩着轻盈的碎步，斯斯文文地跟在她身后。她回过身喊道：“再见了，鹿儿们！我怕是再也见不着你们了。”她亲吻草地的土地，想到要离开它，她哭了；可她知道，她对海的爱已经胜过了对草地的爱。随后她走了——怀着忧伤，也怀着欢喜，盼望着泡沫翻涌的礁石与白帽似的浪涛边，那崭新而美丽的生活。

## III The Mountains / 三 群山

===Original===
> The droning wind

 Entwined about the peaks

 A golden trail of music…

 Far off, the snow-topped mountains

 Were sea-waves

 Capped with foam.

===Chinese===
> 低吟的风

缠绕着峰顶

一道金色的乐声……

远处，雪顶的群山

原是海浪

顶着白沫的冠。

===Original===
Eepersip had begun to wonder whether it would be so easy to take Fleuriss away. She might consent to play, like Toby; but to run away, like Eepersip?—it was a great problem. Eepersip must use some other means than simply appearing and asking her sister to go with her. Perhaps she would entice her on with the assurance that there was something wonderful waiting. Or maybe she could show Fleuriss wonder after wonder—point out the beautiful sea from far away, then lead her on to the little cottage which she had prepared. And if Fleuriss was cold, or hungry, what should she do then? Perhaps she would not like roots to eat. Then, suddenly, an idea: she would dress herself up in wonderful flowers interwoven with the ferns, she would lure butterflies about her wreaths, she would bear armfuls of roses and apple-blossoms and lilacs and scatter them over Fleuriss, she would make her a fern dress, and, thus fascinating her, draw her away.

Eepersip wondered where she could sleep, near the house, and yet concealed. She thought of returning to the meadow, but that would be too far for convenient communication with Fleuriss. And then she saw a lilac-bush on the eastern side of the cottage—a great tall lilac-bush, thick and with great branches. It looked as though she could go into it. And when she tried, she found, to her great delight, that she could squeeze in, curl up in comfort, and be absolutely invisible from the outside.

Then she began to make her fairy array, weaving more ferns into her skirt, and more and more, until it was thick and flouncy—maidenhair ferns and Christmas ferns, evergreen ferns and hay-scented ferns. She tucked flowers all over her dress—late daffodils, cosmos, wild geraniums, primroses. She made a girdle of yellow daisies, a crown of golden buttercups; she plucked a bunch of roses, lilacs, and ferns, binding them with daisies woven together. A great bouquet of violets decorated her dress—violets and little white Pyrolas. With a huge hollyhock for her wand and her arms full of lilacs and roses, she danced in the woods, thinking how her little sister would wonder—and follow.

===Chinese===
伊佩西普（Eepersip）已经开始怀疑，把弗勒里丝带走是不是真有那么容易。她也许肯像托比那样陪她玩；可要她像伊佩西普一样离家出走？——这可是个大难题。伊佩西普不能只是现身出来，请妹妹跟她走，得另想办法。也许她可以先哄着她，叫她相信前头有美妙的东西在等着。或者，她可以领着弗勒里丝一景接一景地看那些奇观——远远地指给她看美丽的大海，再把她一路引到她备好的小屋去。要是弗勒里丝冷了、饿了，那可怎么办？她也许不爱吃树根。忽然，她有了主意：她要用奇花与蕨草交织的盛装打扮自己，引得蝴蝶绕着她的花环飞舞；她要抱着满怀的玫瑰、苹果花和丁香，撒遍弗勒里丝一身；她要给她也做一条蕨裙——就这样迷住她，把她引走。

伊佩西普琢磨着，在哪儿过夜才好——既要挨着房子，又要藏得住。她想过回草地去，可那样离弗勒里丝太远，来往不便。这时她看见小屋东侧有一丛丁香——高大的一丛，枝干粗壮，密密层层。看上去好像能钻进去似的。她一试，不由得大喜过望：果然挤得进去，还能舒舒服服蜷成一团，从外面绝对看不见。

于是她动手置办她的仙女装束：往裙子上编蕨草，编了又编，直到又厚又蓬——铁线蕨和圣诞蕨，常绿蕨和草香蕨。她在裙上四处插满花朵——迟开的黄水仙、秋英、野老鹳草、报春花。她用黄雏菊编了一条腰带，用金灿灿的毛茛编了一顶花冠；又采了一束玫瑰、丁香和蕨草，用编在一处的雏菊捆扎好。一大束紫罗兰缀在她的裙上——紫罗兰，还有小白鹿蹄草。她拿一朵大蜀葵当魔杖，怀里抱满丁香和玫瑰，在林间跳起舞来，一路想着：小妹妹见了该多么惊奇——然后跟上来。

===Original===
That evening early she climbed an oak which was beside the window of her former room, and peeped in. The moonlight shone on the face of a child lying in a little wooden crib. She had fluffy black curls and bright, snapping black eyes, and she was watching delightedly the shadows of the branches on her wall and softly humming.

"Oh," breathed Eepersip, "the little sister. I want her, I want her!" Entranced, Eepersip watched, sitting in a crotch just outside the window—watched her as she lay there, tracing with her finger the curving patterns on her wallpaper; as she played her hands in the moonlight and the waving shadows on her wall. And after a while the humming died away, the finger ceased to stroke the wall, her eyes closed, and in a moment she was gently sleeping. Before Eepersip went down she left a fair sprig of apple-blossoms on Fleuriss's bed—apple-blossoms that, with difficulty, she had brought up the tree. When she went back to her lilac-bush she imagined Fleuriss's surprise, when she should wake, to see them on her bed; imagined Fleuriss following her, all fascinated by butterflies and sweet flowers; imagined her little sister climbing mountains with her, eating berries and roots, swimming and diving and dancing; and—Her thoughts began to grow more and more fantastic—the smell of lilacs intoxicated her—and she went to sleep.

===Chinese===
那天傍晚天还早，她爬上一棵橡树——就立在她原先那间屋子的窗边——朝里窥望。月光照在一个孩子的脸上，她躺在小小的木床里，一头蓬松的黑鬈发，一双乌黑明亮、忽闪忽闪的眼睛，正快活地望着墙上摇曳的枝影，轻轻地哼着歌。

「啊，」伊佩西普屏息轻语，「小妹妹。我要她，我要她！」伊佩西普坐在窗外正合适的树杈上，看得入了迷——看她躺在那儿，用手指描着墙纸上弯弯曲曲的花样；看她在月光里、在墙上晃动的影子里摆弄小手。过了一会儿，哼唱声渐渐低下去，手指不再抚着墙，眼睛阖上了，转眼便安安稳稳地睡着了。伊佩西普下树之前，在弗勒里丝（Fleuriss）床上留下一枝鲜嫩的苹果花——那是她费了好大力气才带上树来的。回到丁香丛里，她想象着弗勒里丝醒来看见床上花枝时的惊奇；想象弗勒里丝被蝴蝶和甜香的花迷住，一路跟来；想象小妹妹同她一起爬山，吃浆果和树根，游泳、潜水、跳舞；还有——她的念头越来越奇幻——丁香的香气把她熏醉了——她睡着了。

===Original===
In the morning she climbed the tree again. Fleuriss was just waking. Her eyes were turned toward the lovely oak-tree, watching the sunlight playing on the emerald leaves. She caught a glimpse of Eepersip as she vanished around the trunk.

"Oh, Mother," she called softly. "I saw a nymph! She smiled at me, and went away."

"Hush, child," said Mrs. Eigleen, coming upstairs and stroking gently the silky black hair. "You were only dreaming."

"No, Mother," returned the child, "I was awake. I *saw* a nymph, really."

Mrs. Eigleen only smiled.

===Chinese===
早上她又爬上那棵树。弗勒里丝刚醒，眼睛望着那棵可爱的橡树，看阳光在翡翠般的叶子上嬉戏。伊佩西普绕过树干隐去的那一瞬，恰好被她瞥见了。

「啊，妈妈，」她轻轻地唤道，「我看见一位仙女！她朝我笑，然后就走了。」

「嘘，孩子，」伊格琳太太（Mrs. Eigleen）说着上了楼，轻抚那丝缎般的黑发，「你不过是做梦罢了。」

「不，妈妈，」孩子答道，「我醒着呢。我*真的*看见了一位仙女。」

伊格琳太太只是笑笑。

===Original===
And then Fleuriss saw the flowers. "O Mother," she cried, "did you bring those to me?" Mrs. Eigleen was wonderstruck.

"Why, no!" she answered.

"Maybe that nymph left them here."

Mrs. Eigleen was astounded enough not to contradict her. "Perhaps," she said.

Eepersip descended again and ran off to her safe hiding-place in the lilac-bush. "She is so, so lovely!" she thought. "I want her more and more."

===Chinese===
接着弗勒里丝看见了那些花。「啊，妈妈，」她叫起来，「是您带给我的吗？」伊格琳太太惊得说不出话。

「咦，不是！」她答道。

「兴许是那位仙女留下的吧。」

伊格琳太太惊愕得竟没反驳她。「也许吧。」她说。

伊佩西普又下了树，跑回丁香丛里安全的藏身处。「她真是、真是太可爱了！」她想，「我越来越想要她了。」

---
===Original===
In a short time little Fleuriss appeared with Mrs. Eigleen. "Fleuriss," said her mother, "you may play here in the garden, but don't go outside it, and don't climb the trees."

"All right, Mother."

"And don't run off and worry me as you did once before." She had *not* forgotten Eepersip. Perhaps she scented something in the air. Those flowers troubled her.

"No, I won't."

Mrs. Eigleen went in, and Fleuriss began to run about and play. Then Eepersip stepped out from under the bush, and the lovely butterflies, lured by her flowers, fluttered and hovered around her.

===Chinese===
不一会儿，小弗勒里丝跟着伊格琳太太出来了。「弗勒里丝，」妈妈说，「你可以在花园里玩，可不许跑到外面去，也不许爬树。」

「好的，妈妈。」

「也不许再像上回那样跑得没影儿，叫我操心。」她可*没有*忘记伊佩西普。也许她嗅出了空气里有什么蹊跷。那些花让她心神不安。

「不会的，我不跑。」

伊格琳太太进了屋，弗勒里丝便跑来跑去玩起来。这时伊佩西普从丛下现身出来，那些美丽的蝴蝶被她的花引得团团围着她翻飞盘旋。

===Original===
"Oh," said Fleuriss, "goodness, how you frightened me. Sit down on the grass, and talk with me. And *how* do you get those butterflies? They always fly away from me."

"Listen, Fleuriss," said Eepersip. "I am Eepersip, who ran away. The butterflies and birds all love me and come to me in great flocks when I call them. And I want you. I want to take you with me to live wild, and eat leaves and berries with the birds—sweet red berries. And if you come the butterflies will gather around you, too. They will not any other way. And look at all my flowers! Butterflies love my flowers."

"Oh, did *you* bring me those *bee‑yoo‑ti‑ful* flowers?"

"Yes, I did. Come!"

"Oh," answered Fleuriss, "and wouldn't it be funny if Mother came out and found me not here!"

===Chinese===
「哎呀，」弗勒里丝说，「天哪，吓了我一跳。坐到草地上来，跟我说说话吧。你*到底*是怎么让那些蝴蝶跟着你的？它们见了我总是飞走。」

「听着，弗勒里丝，」伊佩西普说，「我是伊佩西普，那个离家出走的伊佩西普。蝴蝶和鸟儿都爱我，我一唤，它们就成群成群地飞来找我。我想要你。我要带你一起去过野日子，同鸟儿一起吃叶子和浆果——甜甜的红浆果。你若来，蝴蝶也会围着你飞。不这样它们可不来。瞧瞧我这一身花！蝴蝶就爱我的花。」

「哦，那些*漂——亮——极——了*的花，是*你*带给我的？」

「是我。来吧！」

「哦，」弗勒里丝答道，「要是妈妈出来找不着我，那多好玩呀！」

===Original===
"And think—the birds, the butterflies, the flowers! Look, I'd dress you like this, with ferns and flowers and butterflies. And what fun we could have! We would dance and sing and chase each other amongst the fluttering leaves."

"Oo, I could never catch you."

"No, but I could catch you, and that would be as much fun."

"But Mother doesn't like me to eat leaves, and berries all the time make one sick."

"But we would not have berries all the time. We would dig up sweet white roots and wash them clean; and *m-m!* they are good, little sister Fleuriss. We would have honey. The bees gather honey from the flowers, which they would share with us."

===Chinese===
「你想想——鸟儿、蝴蝶、花儿！瞧，我可以把你打扮成我这样，缀满蕨草、鲜花和蝴蝶。咱们该多快活！咱们可以在飘舞的叶子间跳舞、唱歌、你追我赶。」

「噢，我可怎么也追不上你。」

「追不上我，可我能追上你呀，那一样好玩。」

「可妈妈不喜欢我吃叶子，浆果吃多了会闹病的。」

「咱们不会光吃浆果呀。咱们可以挖甜甜的白根，洗得干干净净——*唔——嗯！*可好吃啦，弗勒里丝小妹妹。咱们还有蜂蜜。蜜蜂从花里采蜜，会分给咱们吃的。」

===Original===
"Bees sting," said Fleuriss, shrinking away; "they sting, and they hurt, Eepersip."

"Oh, but the bees love us all so they don't sting us," answered Eepersip. "It's only the people that try to hurt them that they sting. We wouldn't hurt them."

"Oh, *Eepersip!* the leaves and butterflies, and—and honey—*m-m!* But I oughtn't, really," she said, backing off toward the house.

"Oh, come," said Eepersip, "come, don't go away. Your Mother wouldn't care; she would love to see how happy you were. *Please* come." And Eepersip's hands went out in supplication, scattering over Fleuriss wreaths of flowers, sprays of berries, crimson, gold, frosty white.

"Oh, how beautiful!" exclaimed the little girl. But when she looked up, Eepersip had vanished.

===Chinese===
「蜜蜂蜇人，」弗勒里丝说着往后缩，「它们蜇人，蜇得可疼呢，伊佩西普。」

「哦，可蜜蜂爱咱们，所以不蜇咱们，」伊佩西普答道，「它们只蜇那些想害它们的人。咱们不会伤害它们。」

「哦，*伊佩西普！*叶子呀，蝴蝶呀，还——还有蜂蜜——*唔——嗯！*可我不该去，真的，」她说着，一步步退向屋子。

「哦，来呀，」伊佩西普说，「来呀，别走开。你妈妈不会介意的；她看见你那么快活，只会高兴。*求你*来吧。」伊佩西普双手伸出，像在恳求，把一环环花圈、一串串浆果撒遍弗勒里丝周身——绯红的、金黄的、霜白的。

「哦，多美呀！」小姑娘叫道。可她一抬头，伊佩西普已经不见了。

---

===Original===
Suddenly the door opened and Mrs. Eigleen stepped out. Eepersip had darted under the welcome branches of an apple-tree, whose thick blossoms kept her from sight.

"How sweet it smells!" said Mrs. Eigleen—"just as if a fairy had been here. Where did those flowers come from, Fleuriss?"

"Oh," answered Fleuriss, "I saw the most beautiful girl. She brought me flowers and called me 'little *sister*' and wanted me to go away with her!"

Pale and weak from fright, Mrs. Eigleen took Fleuriss by the hand and dragged her roughly into the house.

Eepersip sat down under the apple-tree in ecstasy. "I saw her," she said softly, "I saw her and talked to her, and—oh, how dear she is! But I *do* wish she hadn't told about me." She waited there, and in a short time Fleuriss appeared again, running.

===Chinese===
忽然门开了，伊格琳太太走了出来。伊佩西普一闪身，钻进一棵苹果树欢迎她的枝叶下，繁密的花朵把她遮得严严实实。

「多香啊！」伊格琳太太说——「简直像有位小仙子来过。这些花是打哪儿来的，弗勒里丝？」

「哦，」弗勒里丝答道，「我看见一个顶顶美丽的姑娘。她带花给我，叫我『小*妹妹*』，还想带我一块儿走呢！」

伊格琳太太吓得脸色煞白、浑身发软，一把攥住弗勒里丝的手，硬生生把她拽进了屋。

伊佩西普喜滋滋地在苹果树下坐下来。「我看见她了，」她轻轻地说，「我看见她了，还跟她说了话，还有——啊，她多亲呀！不过她把我漏了出去，我*真*希望她没说。」她在那儿等着，不多时，弗勒里丝又跑着出来了。

===Original===
"Eepersip, Eepersip," she cried, "where are you?"

In a moment Eepersip had her arms around her waist, kissing her and hugging her.

"Are you coming?" she asked; "have you decided to come, Fleuriss?"

"Y-y-es," said Fleuriss, "I really have, Eepersip. I thought all dinner time, and couldn't eat, I was so 'xcited! But we must go quickly now, or they will run after us."

So they ran quickly into the woods—ran amid the trees and flowers until they were far from the house. Eepersip showed her little sister how to dance, and they danced together. She also showed her how to leap and run fast, and Fleuriss was delighted. When they grew tired, they sat down together and made fern dresses and flower wreaths. Fleuriss followed Eepersip's example, casting aside her dress, shoes, and stockings.

===Chinese===
「伊佩西普，伊佩西普，」她喊道，「你在哪儿呀？」

转眼间伊佩西普已搂住她的腰，又亲又抱。

「你来吗？」她问，「你拿定主意了吗，弗勒里丝？」

「是——是的，」弗勒里丝说，「我真的拿定主意了，伊佩西普。吃饭时我一直想啊想，激动得吃不下！可咱们得赶紧走，不然他们会追来的。」

于是她们飞快地跑进树林——在树木和花丛间一路跑去，直跑到离屋子远远的地方。伊佩西普教小妹妹跳舞，两人便一起跳。她还教她跳跃和快跑，弗勒里丝高兴极了。跑累了，她们一起坐下，编蕨裙、扎花环。弗勒里丝学伊佩西普的样子，把连衣裙、鞋子、袜子统统丢在一边。

===Original===
"Oh, how 'licious the grass feels on my bare toes!" she said, "and the soft moss. Eepersip, I feel just like a nymph." (A slight pause.) "When I saw the flowers I said: 'Mother, I think a *nymph* left them there,' and she said: 'Oh, no, there aren't any nymphs. You're only dreaming!' *Are* there nymphs, Eepersip?"

"Oh, yes, Fleuriss, and if we dance and run and dress just like them, we'll pretend *we're* nymphs, too."

"But why can't we *see* them, Eepersip?"

"Oh, we can, if we look very hard. They're all around in the trees, the flowers, and the woods. *Sometimes* we can't see them, and they turn into butterflies so we can. I can see them."

"Well, sometimes," said Fleuriss thoughtfully, "it seems as if they were everywhere—when it's windy, you know, and sunny, and there are shadows. In my garden it's so beautiful I think there must be nymphs. I can *feel* them, not exactly see."

===Chinese===
「哦，青草踩在光脚趾上多美——滋——滋呀！」她说，「还有那软软的苔藓。伊佩西普，我觉得自己就像个仙女。」（微微一顿。）「看见那些花的时候我就说：『妈妈，我想是一位*仙女*留在那儿的。』她说：『哎呀不，哪有什么仙女。你做梦呢！』到底*有没有*仙女呀，伊佩西普？」

「哦，有的，弗勒里丝。要是咱们跳舞、奔跑，穿得就跟她们一模一样，咱们就假装*自己*也是仙女。」

「可咱们怎么*看不见*她们呢，伊佩西普？」

「哦，看得见的，只要用心去看。她们就在四下的树里、花里、林子里。*有时候*咱们看不见，她们就变成蝴蝶，好让咱们看见。我就看得见她们。」

「嗯，有时候，」弗勒里丝若有所思地说，「好像她们哪儿都是——起风的时候，你晓得，又是太阳，又是影子。我的花园那么美，我想一定有仙女。我能*觉出*她们来，只是看不真切。」

===Original===
There was a pause.

Then—"Where we going now, Eepersip?" for Eepersip was gradually working off to a hill which was a peak of Mount Varcrobis, north of Eiki-ennern Peak.

"Fleuriss," said Eepersip, with a strange emotion in her voice, "have you ever seen the sea?"

"No, but I heard Mother talking about it once. She said maybe you had gone down there; and she told me it was lots of blue water, and there were boats there. Did you really go there?"

"Yes; it's *so* beautiful, Fleuriss. The sun makes the waves sparkle like gold, and the great white gulls with their long, narrow wings go gliding, circling over the water, sometimes plunging down and catching fish underneath. And there is white sand there, soft sand, and shells and pretty pebbles, and little fishes swimming. And when it's windy the waves come dashing up on the rocks, flinging spray high in the air. And there is seaweed, too, Fleuriss, green seaweed that goes floating up and down as the waves stir it. And corals, too. Oh, my little sister, it's so, so beautiful. I would show you how to leap into it from the rocks, and how to swim—to be a mermaid and play with the gulls and the fishes, dressed all in seaweeds!"

===Chinese===
两人静了一会儿。

接着——「这会儿咱们上哪儿去呀，伊佩西普？」原来伊佩西普正渐渐朝一座山丘挪过去——那是瓦克罗比斯山（Mount Varcrobis）的一座峰，在艾基恩嫩峰（Eiki-ennern Peak）以北。

「弗勒里丝，」伊佩西普开口了，声音里带着一种奇异的激动，「你见过大海吗？」

「没见过，可我听妈妈说起过一回。她说兴许你到那儿去了；她告诉我，那是好多好多的蓝水，那儿还有船。你真的去过吗？」

「见过；那真是*太*美了，弗勒里丝。太阳照得浪花像金子一样闪，白色的大海鸥张开细长的翅膀，在水面上滑翔、盘旋，有时一头扎下去，从水底叼起鱼来。那儿还有白色的沙滩，软软的沙，有贝壳和漂亮的小卵石，还有游来游去的小鱼。起风的时候，浪头哗哗地扑上礁石，把水花高高抛向空中。还有海草呢，弗勒里丝，绿色的海草，随波浪搅动，一浮一沉。还有珊瑚。啊，我的小妹妹，那儿真是、真是太美了。我可以教你怎么从礁石上跳进海里，怎么游泳——做一个美人鱼，浑身裹着海草，同海鸥和鱼儿嬉戏！」

===Original===
"Oh, Eepersip! let's go *now*!"

"And I have a little cottage down there for you to live in—a pretty little cottage just like your home."

"Oh, how nice!"

"And we shall go riding up and down on the great waves, Fleuriss, while the seagulls scream over our heads. We shall go 'way out of sight of land and find islands and rocks out there. And the waves are tremendous when it's windy—very windy."

"Oo—"

"Fleuriss!" And Eepersip caught her little sister in her arms—glad that she had succeeded in entrancing her with the sea.

===Chinese===
「哦，伊佩西普！咱们*现在*就去吧！」

「我在那儿还有一间小屋给你住——一间漂亮的小屋，就跟你的家一模一样。」

「哦，多好呀！」

「咱们还可以骑着大浪头颠上颠下，弗勒里丝，海鸥就在头顶上尖叫。咱们要远远漂到看不见陆地的地方，去找那些海岛和礁石。起风的时候——刮大风的时候——那浪头可大得吓人。」

「噢——」

「弗勒里丝！」伊佩西普一把将小妹妹搂进怀里——她用大海迷住了她，心里好不喜欢。

===Original===
"But, Eepersip," said Fleuriss, doubtfully, "where *are* we going now?"

"I thought, Fleuriss, that we'd go to that great hill over there—do you see?"

"Yes."

"Go over there so that you can see from 'way of how beautiful it is."

"Oh yes; I'm crazy to see it!"

Eepersip saw that this hill was wooded on one side, but on the far side it was like a pasture—she could see sunlight glinting on it. On they went, often stopping to pick flowers, to dig up roots, or to refresh themselves at some little tinkling brook or mossy spring. Once as they were pushing through a fence of low beech-branches they came to a spring all surrounded with green moss—oh! so soft. There were ferns nodding beside it, and one or two strange pink orchids gazed at themselves admiringly in its surface. At the bottom were white stones. A cool, green frog plopped into it as they arrived. And Fleuriss was fascinated. She sat there for a long time, watching him reappear for air, then bob down again when he saw that they were still watching him.

===Chinese===
「可是，伊佩西普，」弗勒里丝迟疑地说，「咱们这会儿*到底*往哪儿去呀？」

「我想呀，弗勒里丝，咱们先到那边那座大山丘去——你瞧见了吗？」

「瞧见了。」

「到那边去，好让你从老远就瞧见它有多美。」

「哦，好啊；我都等不及想看啦！」

伊佩西普瞧出这山丘一侧林木森森，可远的那一侧却像片牧场——她能望见阳光在上面闪烁。她们继续赶路，时时停下来采采花、挖挖根，或是在哪条叮咚作响的小溪、哪眼长满青苔的泉水边歇歇乏。有一回，她们正钻过一道矮山毛榉枝条编成的篱笆，遇见一眼泉水，四周全是绿苔——啊！那么软。泉边有蕨草摇曳，还有一两朵奇异的粉色兰花，正对着水面顾影自赏。泉底铺着白色的石子。她们刚到，一只凉沁沁的绿青蛙扑通一声跳了进去。弗勒里丝看迷了。她在那儿坐了好久，看青蛙浮上来换气，一见她们还在盯着他，又咕咚沉下去。

===Original===
Again they came into a great meadow dotted with flowers. Butterflies with soft wings stroked Eepersip's cheeks caressingly. Fleuriss danced through the flowers, looking, as Eepersip thought, like a little butterfly herself. The sky was a heavenly deep blue—a rich deep blue, yet filled and sparkling with all the gold of the sun and all the coolth of snow. She could see for miles into it, as if it had suddenly come nearer than usual. She reached up and could almost see her fingers touching it. What a strange sensation!

But Fleuriss had a stranger one. As Eepersip danced along, it seemed as though her feet barely touched the ground. The flowers and grasses swayed gently beneath her, but they were not crushed. And Fleuriss felt a bit of dread coming into her mind—dread of living and staying with this strange sister. What if she should grow tired of Fleuriss and run off? Suppose she should change into a tree—a leaf—a sprite? But Fleuriss fought with this feeling—because she wanted to live by the ocean, and to do the things that Eepersip had promised.

===Chinese===
她们又走进一片繁花点点的大草地。软翅的蝴蝶爱抚地轻拂伊佩西普的脸颊。弗勒里丝在花间舞过，在伊佩西普看来，她自己也像一只小蝴蝶了。天空是一种天堂般的深蓝——浓郁深湛的蓝，却又盛满了太阳的全部金色与积雪的全部清凉，闪闪发亮。她能望进它好几英里深，仿佛它忽然比平日离人近了。她伸手向上，几乎看得见自己的指尖就要碰到它。多么奇异的感受！

可弗勒里丝的感受还要更奇特。伊佩西普一路舞着，双脚仿佛只是轻轻点过地面。花与草在她脚下柔柔摇曳，却不见压折。弗勒里丝心里悄悄生出一丝惧意——怕同这位奇异的姐姐一起生活、一起厮守。万一她厌了弗勒里丝，跑掉了呢？万一她变成一棵树——一片叶——一个精灵呢？可弗勒里丝拼命压下这感觉——因为她想住在海边，想做伊佩西普许诺过的那些事。

---

===Original===
After a while they came to the foot of the great hill. They slept down there, near a tiny lakelet, in the soft grass and among the flowers, with the tinkle, tinkle of a little brook in their ears all night. The next morning they climbed the hill together, and it was very steep and rocky. Fleuriss had to be helped often, and grew tired before she reached the top. But Eepersip lured her on by the promise of seeing the ocean, and they struggled painfully up.

The sea stretched away to the horizon, blue and sparkling as it met the sky. Fleuriss was spellbound.

"Eepersip, is that the sea?" she asked.

"Yes, Fleuriss—the sea, the sea!"

===Chinese===
过了一阵，她们来到那座大山丘脚下。她们就在那儿睡下，傍着一汪小小的湖，躺在软草与花丛之间，耳边整夜响着小溪叮咚、叮咚。第二天早上，她们一起爬山，山又陡又多石。弗勒里丝得时时让人搀一把，还没到顶就累坏了。可伊佩西普拿看海来哄她往前走，两人一路艰难地爬了上去。

大海一直铺到天边，与天空相接的地方蓝莹莹、亮闪闪。弗勒里丝看呆了。

「伊佩西普，那就是海吗？」她问。

「是的，弗勒里丝——是海，是海呀！」

===Original===
Off to the north was a range of high blue-green hills, and off beyond them higher ones, and higher—billowing mountains—and beyond them was a range of snowy peaks, rising, sharply outlined, into the blue. The lakelet where they had slept was like an opal set with dark green pines. But those mountains—! never before had Eepersip seen anything like them. The sea was not nearly so beautiful. And again she felt that longing which she had felt when she saw the sea—but a more passionate longing.

And Fleuriss? How could she climb those great peaks—she, who had had great difficulty even with the little hill? Well, Fleuriss could grow more used to such things, and then they would go together. But Fleuriss—Fleuriss barefoot, dressed in ferns—on those snowy summits! No, it would be impossible for years and years. She would have to wait—or else go alone.

But the hill had other things than just the view. For there were the loveliest little winding lanes, and bright open places, and close spots where they could hardly push through the bushes; great patches of delicious soft grass, then again enormous smooth-topped rocks from where they had first found the long-sought vision of the sea. Such feasts as Nature laid before them! There were great beds of the most delicious wild strawberries, and nobody to share them with but the birds. And they and the birds gobbled them; and it seemed as if the more they ate the more there were to eat; they ripened all the time. And in this marvellous place there were such contrasts! They could have anything they wanted there. There were places where the sun always struck brilliantly, and cool, shady ones for the hot days—places where not much sun ever came. There was the loveliest of soft grass, and then again nothing but brambles and heaps of pointed rocks. There were lanes leading through the woods occasionally, and there were places where no one would ever suspect that there was any such thing as a lane. There were little fairy glades where they could dance together—glades bordered with ferns and carpeted with moss.

===Chinese===
北边远远横着一列蓝绿色的高丘，丘后更有高山，越望越高——山峦如波涛翻涌——再往外，是一列雪峰，轮廓峻峭，直插蓝天。她们过夜的那汪小湖，像一颗镶着墨绿松树的欧泊。可那些山哪——！伊佩西普从未见过那样的东西。大海远没有这样美。当初望见大海时的那种渴望，又涌上她心头——只是这一回，更炽烈了。

那么弗勒里丝呢？她连爬那座小山丘都费了老大劲，又怎么上得了那些大峰？唉，弗勒里丝慢慢习惯这些，总有一天能同去的。可是弗勒里丝——光着脚、穿着蕨裙的弗勒里丝——上那些雪顶！不，那得等上好多年好多年才行。她只好等——要不就一个人去。

可这山丘有的不只是那一眼风景。这儿有最可爱的小小弯巷，有明亮的空地，也有密得几乎钻不过树丛的角落；有一大片一大片美味的软草，又有巨大的圆顶岩石——她们正是在那上面第一次望见了寻访许久的大海。大自然在她们面前摆下了怎样的盛宴！有一床一床顶好吃的野草莓，除了鸟儿，没有谁来分。她们同鸟儿一起大嚼；仿佛越吃越多，怎么也吃不完——它们时时都在成熟。在这奇妙的地方，处处是这样强烈的对照！她们想要什么，这里就有什么。有太阳总是明晃晃照着的地方，也有大热天里阴凉背荫的去处——有些地方几乎终年不见多少日头。有最可爱的软草，也有一味荆棘和成堆尖石的荒地。偶尔有小巷穿林而过，也有些地方，谁也想不到会有巷子这回事。还有小小的仙林间空地，好让她们一起跳舞——空地四边镶着蕨草，铺着苔藓。

===Original===
Fleuriss and Eepersip lived there enchanted day after day, and although they often saw the sea, they did not wish to leave the hill. Fleuriss spoke about it several times, but Eepersip would hurriedly change the subject. That range of blue hills seemed to be calling her—she would forget the sea for a while, until the next year. After they had stayed where they were for some time, they would go on and on to the blue hills, and perhaps explore the great snowy mountains beyond. She could manage with Fleuriss somehow.

One day they went exploring farther than ever toward the east. They followed a narrow path, winding, winding through the bushes. And then it curved around toward the northeast and led through low laurel-trees, and here Eepersip stopped to make for Fleuriss a crown of the blossoms. And again the path turned and came on to a broader gravel road all bordered with gorgeous roses of red and white, and Fleuriss was very much surprised at their magnificent beauty. But Eepersip was distressed. So they had come to a place where there were roads, houses, and people! But as yet they had seen no house. Eepersip hoped that there would be none, for she was as entranced as Fleuriss with the beauty of it all. And then they switched off on another little path, leading southeast on to a wide lawn all bordered with marvellous roses. Here they danced together a long time. Next they turned into another gravelled path which led eastward, through clumps of roses and laurel, downhill and uphill, for a long way; and then they saw a garden brilliant with colour. Fleuriss was dazed, there were so many flowering bushes—rhododendron, laurel, honeysuckle, azalea, quince, and fire-blossom. Hummingbirds, bright emerald and ruby with moonlight wings, were darting and sparkling about, sipping honey, resting and quivering on the air.

But soon after they had discovered the garden, Eepersip said that she was going on a short journey, coming back in two or three days. "Will you be all right here alone, little sister?" she said anxiously.

"Oh yes, Eepersip, and I'm going to find lots of things to show you when you come back. But where are you going?"

"I'm going—going—to a beautiful place—and take you there sometime."

===Chinese===
弗勒里丝和伊佩西普在那儿一天天过着着了魔的日子，虽然常常望见大海，却不想离开山丘。弗勒里丝几次提起海，伊佩西普总是急忙岔开话头。那列蓝色山峦仿佛在唤她——她要把海暂且忘一忘，直到明年。等在原地住上一阵，她们就继续往前，走到蓝色的山丘去，也许还要探访山外那座大雪岭。弗勒里丝嘛，她总归有法子安顿。

一天，她们朝东边探得比哪天都远。她们顺着一条窄径走，在灌丛里绕呀、绕呀。随后小径拐向东北，穿过一片低矮的山月桂树，伊佩西普在这儿停下，给弗勒里丝编了一顶花冠。小径又是一转，接上一条宽些的砾石路，路边尽是红红白白的绚烂玫瑰，弗勒里丝叫那华美惊得合不拢嘴。伊佩西普却发了愁。原来她们竟走到了有路、有屋子、有人的地方！好在她们还没见着一所房子。伊佩西普但愿一所也没有，因为这满眼的美，她也同弗勒里丝一样看迷了。接着她们拐上另一条小径，往东南通到一片宽阔的草坪，四边围满奇妙的玫瑰。她们在这儿跳了好一阵舞。随后又转上一条砾石小路，一路向东，穿过一丛丛玫瑰与山月桂，下坡又上坡，走了好远；然后，她们看见一座色彩灼灼的花园。弗勒里丝眼都花了——开花的灌木那么多——杜鹃、山月桂、忍冬、映山红、榅桲，还有火焰花。蜂鸟亮得像翡翠和红宝石，生着月光似的翅膀，飞箭似的穿来穿去、闪闪烁烁，吸着花蜜，悬在空中歇息、颤动。

可发现花园后不久，伊佩西普便说她要出一趟短门，两三天就回来。「你一个人在这儿不要紧吧，小妹妹？」她不放心地问。

「不要紧的，伊佩西普，我还要找好多好多东西，等你回来给你看呢。可你要上哪儿去呀？」

「我要去——去——一个美丽的地方——往后也带你去。」

===Original===
"Oh—I see. Can't I go now?"

"No—because—it would be too hard now. Wait till I go and find the easiest way for you."

"All right—goodbye!"

And, with a rustle of ferns, Eepersip vanished around a great rhododendron-bush.

---

===Chinese===
「哦——我明白了。现在不能去吗？」

「不能——因为——眼下太难走了。等我先去替你找出一条最好走的路。」

「那好吧——再见！」

说罢，蕨裙窸窣一响，伊佩西普绕过一大丛杜鹃，不见了。

---

===Original===
Fleuriss continued her explorations alone. She saw a gorgeous butterfly come sailing toward her, of yellow streaked with black. Others followed, and they covered her with soft wing-caresses, crowning her head with their wings. Fleuriss thought (as Eepersip had told her) that they were the fairies turning themselves into butterflies so that she could see them.

Not a mouse stirred when she wormed her way through the bushes, taking care not to step on leaves or dry twigs so as to make a noise. And then the sun started to set and turned the whole sky golden and rose. Fleuriss crept in among a vine with golden flowers (there was no rich purple fruit yet, only the lovely flowers) and watched. And, lo! each leaf was quivering, and on their smooth surfaces was represented another miniature sunset. How marvellous the rose and gold looked through the mass of trembling green leaves.

===Chinese===
弗勒里丝独自继续探看。她瞧见一只绚丽的蝴蝶朝她翩然驶来，黄的底子缀着黑纹。又跟来几只，它们用柔软的翅膀爱抚她周身，拿翅膀给她加冕。弗勒里丝想（伊佩西普正是这么告诉她的），这些就是化成蝴蝶的小仙子，好让她看得见她们。

她从灌丛间蛇行而过，小心翼翼不踩着叶子或干树枝，免得弄出声响，连一只老鼠也没有惊动。这时太阳开始西沉，把整个天空染成金色与玫瑰色。弗勒里丝悄悄钻进一架开金花的藤蔓（还没有那饱满的紫色果实，只有可爱的花朵），望着外面。看哪！每一片叶子都在颤动，光洁的叶面上映着又一幅小小的落日。透过那一片颤动的绿叶，玫瑰色与金色显得多么奇妙。

===Original===
Then Fleuriss squeezed her way out of the bush and began to explore again. Pushing northward in the dim, rosy light, she came to a smooth lawn of pale green moss. On the other side was a stretch of woods, then another lawn, of grass this time and smaller; and then there was a great row of massive pines and beyond them an opaline lake. And still the sun went down, and the mass of colour became smaller and brighter, and Fleuriss, who had never seen so much beauty in her little life, gazed and gazed. The colour faded slowly, slowly, as she watched, until only a deep flush was left, and it was then that Fleuriss thought she was in the heart of a giant rose. And—inconceivable—she looked, and she *was*. She was sure of it. She could even see the great curling petals around her. Right at the sun was a burning spot. That was the pollen of the great flower. And this tiny fire burned and burned until only one bright red spark was left. Then it too went out, and after it all the rose colour faded away.

Then Fleuriss turned to the lake, which also had held in its bright blue surface an image of the sunset. The sky was deep blue now. The pines looked even darker against it, and in the lake Fleuriss could see the reflection of the crescent moon setting. And then she ran down by the side of the lake, and very dark and strange it looked in the evening. Dipping her little hands into the clear, crystal water, she drank, for she was thirsty. But she was too tired to appreciate any more beauty just then, and so she crept back to her little nest of flowers to go to sleep. Then she heard a gurgle of sweet silvery music, and she listened spellbound, entranced. But it was no wicked witch, seeking to entice her by spells: it was the solitary wood-thrush, that superb singer of the dusk. And then Fleuriss dropped off to sleep.

===Chinese===
随后弗勒里丝从灌木里挤出来，又开始探看。她在幽暗的玫瑰色光线里一路向北，来到一片平整的、淡绿苔藓的草坪。草坪那边是一带树林，再过去又是一片草坪，这回是草的，也小些；然后是一大排粗壮的松树，树后一片乳色的湖。太阳还在往下落，那一团颜色越缩越小、越缩越亮，弗勒里丝——她小小的生平还从未见过这么多美——望啊，望啊。颜色慢慢地、慢慢地褪去，她眼看着，直到只剩一抹深红。就在那时，弗勒里丝觉得自己正待在一朵巨玫瑰的花心里。而且——不可思议——她看了看，*确实如此*。她敢肯定。她甚至看得见四周巨大的卷曲花瓣。正对着太阳的地方有一个燃着的点。那便是这朵巨花的花粉。这一点小火烧啊，烧啊，最后只剩一粒鲜红的火星。随后它也熄了，它一熄，所有的玫瑰色都褪尽了。

随后弗勒里丝转向湖去，那明蓝的湖面上也曾映着一幅落日。天空这时已是深蓝。松树衬着天色显得更黑了，弗勒里丝在湖里看得见下沉的新月的倒影。她顺着湖边跑去，暮色里的湖显得又暗又神秘。她把小手浸进清澈晶莹的水里掬水喝，因为她渴了。可她实在太累，一时再也受用不动更多的美，便悄悄爬回她那小花巢里去睡。这时她听见一段甜美的银铃似的乐声汩汩流出，她听得着了魔，入了迷。可那不是什么念咒诱人的恶巫婆：那是离群的一只棕林鸫，黄昏最出色的歌手。于是弗勒里丝沉沉睡去了。

===Original===
The next morning dawned fair, and she rose bewitched with what she had been through. The sunset and the silvery notes of the thrush all came back to her. She went down by the lake. It was very different now. Its blue was sparkling with the rays of the sun, whereas before it had looked very solitary—an icy cold blue. There was no beach—just a grassy bank—and in the shallow water she saw some little silvery fishes swimming and playing in shoals. And she watched them in their happy play for a long time, fascinated by the way they raced after each other around the shining stones and pebbles. Because they were so bright and gleaming, poor little Fleuriss thought that they were some rare and unheard-of fish, little dreaming that they were just common minnows.

Eepersip came back that day in a strange way. Fleuriss was looking down on the meadowy side of the hill, where the long green grass waved in the wind and butterflies were fluttering. And as she looked, suddenly—there was Eepersip standing in front of her. She had come from nowhere—she was just there without coming at all. Fleuriss was appalled. She remembered that strange dancing—was her sister about to melt into the air? Fleuriss stood stock-still.

Finally she raised her head and said, at first faintly, but with growing enthusiasm: "Oh, Eepersip, last night there was a great rose, and I was inside it—and I found a beautiful lake with fishes in it—oh, wonderful fishes of silver—and the beautiful birdie sang me to sleep in the flowers." And then, her voice sounding strangely timid: "O Eepersip—I want—Mother—to see it—so beautiful. I love—it here, but—I know Mother would like to see it, too. And I guess I can't get along without her. I guess I can't, Eepersip."

===Chinese===
第二天清晨天色晴好，她一觉醒来，还为自己经历的一切神魂颠倒。落日和鸫鸟银亮的歌声都回到她心头。她走到湖边。此刻的湖已大不相同。它的蓝色在阳光里闪闪发亮，而先前它显得那么孤寂——一种冰冷的蓝。这儿没有沙滩——只有青草的湖岸——她在浅水里看见几群银亮的小鱼游来游去、玩耍嬉戏。她久久看着它们快活的游玩，看它们绕着发亮的石子和卵石你追我赶，看得入了迷。它们那么亮、那么闪，可怜的小弗勒里丝只当是什么罕见的、闻所未闻的鱼儿，做梦也想不到那不过是普普通通的鲦鱼。

那天伊佩西普回来得也古怪。弗勒里丝正朝山丘草甸的那一侧望下去，长长的绿草在风里起伏，蝴蝶翻飞。她正望着，忽然——伊佩西普就站在了她面前。她不知打哪儿来——她就那么在了，压根儿没经过「来」。弗勒里丝吓坏了。她想起那种奇异的舞蹈——姐姐莫非要化进空气里去？弗勒里丝僵在原地，一动不动。

好半天她才抬起头说，起初细细的，后来越说越起劲：「哦，伊佩西普，昨儿晚上有一朵好大好大的玫瑰，我就在它里面——我还找到了一个美丽的湖，湖里有鱼——哦，银子一样奇妙的鱼——还有那只漂亮的小鸟，在花里唱着歌哄我睡觉。」接着，她的声音听起来怯得出奇：「哦，伊佩西普——我想——让妈妈——也看看——太美了。我爱——这儿，可是——我知道妈妈也会想看的。我想，没有她我不行的。我想我不行，伊佩西普。」

===Original===
Eepersip was brokenhearted. "But, Fleuriss," she said—and Fleuriss shuddered a very little as Eepersip took her hand—"Fleuriss, if your mother came, she would take you back home, and you would not be here any more. If she would come to see it, all right, but she would not—and so you would not see it either. Come on, show me the lake you found."

Fleuriss was happy in a flash. Laughing and dancing, she took her sister down to the lake and showed her the wondrous fishes. They went in bathing together, and Eepersip showed Fleuriss how to swim, as she had shown Toby. Fleuriss was wild with joy. Then they splashed each other and played tag in the water. Eepersip puzzled Fleuriss by swimming under water, and Fleuriss would scream with delight when she came up in a totally unexpected place. This new pastime kept them happy for several days.

But again Fleuriss began to grow miserable—and homesick.

---

===Chinese===
伊佩西普心都碎了。「可是，弗勒里丝，」她说——伊佩西普握住她的手时，弗勒里丝极轻微地打了个寒噤——「弗勒里丝，要是你妈妈来了，她会把你带回家里去，你就再也到不了这儿了。她要是只来看看，那敢情好，可她不会——那么你也见不着了。来吧，带我去看你找到的湖。」

弗勒里丝一下子又欢喜起来。她笑着、跳着，带姐姐下到湖边，把那些奇妙的鱼指给姐姐看。她们一起下水和嬉，伊佩西普教弗勒里丝游泳，就像当初教托比（Toby）那样。弗勒里丝乐得发了狂。随后她们互相泼水，在水里玩捉人游戏。伊佩西普会潜到水底下去，弄得弗勒里丝满心疑惑，等她从一个全然想不到的地方冒出来，弗勒里丝就快活地尖叫。这样新鲜的耍子让她们快活了好几天。

可弗勒里丝又渐渐愁苦起来——想家了。

---

===Original===
And again Eepersip resisted this feeling for a long time—two or three weeks of misery. But at the end of that time she began to think.

To begin with, she thought about where she had been on that little expedition of hers. She had been up toward those blue hills to see from nearer the snowy mountains. She had loved them more and wanted more than ever to go to them. She asked Fleuriss if she would not like to climb the high peaks with her. But Fleuriss replied, almost snappishly: "You know what I want, Eepersip."

Of course this misery weighed down Eepersip's mind frightfully; she was very uncomfortable. And then she began to think that after all she would want to be alone when she went amongst the mountains; Fleuriss would be all right if she were happy, perhaps, but a miserable companion would be unendurable. Perhaps she had made a mistake in taking Fleuriss away. Maybe it was true that they had to go in different directions—that she herself could not live at home, and that her little sister could not live elsewhere. And even in Eepersip's untamed heart there was a bit of pity. And she found that that pity kept growing. How badly the Eigleens must feel, after all! Once she smothered it with the thought, "No, she will be happy if she stays long enough, and they will forget her." But it only began to grow again.

===Chinese===
伊佩西普又一次同这感觉对抗了很久——愁苦了两三个星期。可到了最后，她开始想事情了。

起先，她回想自己那趟小小的远行都到了哪儿。她朝那列蓝色山丘去，为的是从近处望望雪山。她更爱它们了，比任何时候都更想去。她问弗勒里丝，愿不愿意同她一起去爬那些高峰。弗勒里丝却答道，口气几乎是冲的：「你知道我想要什么，伊佩西普。」

这份愁苦自然沉沉压在伊佩西普心上，压得她好难受；她浑身不自在。接着她开始想：她进山去，终究还是想一个人；弗勒里丝若是快快活活的，跟着倒也不要紧，可一个愁眉苦脸的同伴，实在叫人受不住。也许，带弗勒里丝出来本就是个错。也许她们注定要各走各的路——她自己没法住在家里，而她的小妹妹没法住在别处。就连伊佩西普那颗不驯的心里，也生出了一丝怜悯。而且她发现，这怜悯越长越大。伊格琳家心里该多么难受啊，说到底！有一回她用这样的念头把它掐灭：「不，她待得够久就会快活起来，他们会忘了她的。」可它偏偏又长了起来。

===Original===
Up to this unhappy time Fleuriss's flowers had not withered or drooped: in this they were like those of Eepersip. But now Eepersip noticed that for some peculiar reason hers only stayed fresh and sweet. And then she thought again about the mountains and about those poor wasted flowers, and the pity grew and grew.

And one happy, happy day for Fleuriss, Eepersip led her safely home again.

"Goodbye, Fleuriss," she said. "I'm sorry you wouldn't stay with me."

"Yes, I know, Eepersip, but I just couldn't. Why don't you come home—you've been away so long—and Mother cries for you still. Please come."

===Chinese===
直到这段不快活的日子为止，弗勒里丝的花从不曾枯萎或蔫垂：这一点上，它们同伊佩西普的花一样。可现在伊佩西普注意到，不知怎的，唯独她自己的花还保持着新鲜与甜香。于是她又想起那些山，想起那些可怜的、白白枯萎的花，怜悯便越长越大，越长越大。

于是在弗勒里丝顶顶快活的一天，伊佩西普又平平安安把她领回了家。

「再见吧，弗勒里丝，」她说，「你不肯陪着我，我很遗憾。」

「是啊，我知道，伊佩西普，可我实在没法子。你怎么不回家去——你走了那么久——妈妈还在为你哭呢。求求你回来吧。」

===Original===
"Oh, Fleuriss, I *couldn't*. If I were to go back home now, I should just die—even with you."

"Goodbye, then. Sometime I'm going to take Mother to see that beautiful hill."

"But not for a long time?"

"As soon as I can."

"Don't."

"Why?"

"You know. Please don't."

"Well, I'm not sure. I'm going to—pretty soon. Goodbye, Eepersip—Aren't you *ever* coming home?"

===Chinese===
「哦，弗勒里丝，我*不能*啊。这会儿要是回家里去，我会活不成的——哪怕有你在。」

「那么，再见了。往后我要带妈妈去看那座美丽的山丘。」

「可要等好久好久吧？」

「我一能去就去。」

「别去。」

「为什么？」

「你知道的。求你别去。」

「嗯，我说不好。我要去——快了。再见，伊佩西普——你*永远*不回家了吗？」

===Original===
"Oh, Fleuriss, no!"

"I wish you would."

"But I can't."

"Won't you let me take Mother and Daddy to live over there?"

"Well, after a while—if you want to. I shan't be there."

"Why, where are you going?"

"I'm going to the beautiful, beautiful white mountains. And then maybe the sea again, Fleuriss—the sea."

===Chinese===
「哦，弗勒里丝，不回去了！」

「我多希望你能回去呀。」

「可我不能。」

「那让妈妈和爸爸搬过去住，好不好？」

「行啊，过些时候——要是你想的话。我不会在那儿了。」

「咦，那你要上哪儿去呀？」

「我要去那些美丽的、美丽的白山。兴许以后再回海边去，弗勒里丝——海边。」

===Original===
"Oo!"

"Coming?"

"**No**! I'll ask Mother to take me to the sea. She will."

"Then—goodbye!"

And she decked Fleuriss's fern dress with beautiful flowers—a crown of them and a girdle. A sweet wind arose, carrying the scent of Eepersip's flowers to Fleuriss. A few butterflies were blown over to her. Eepersip stood on her tiptoes an instant: then, quick as a flash, she whirled about and bounded off, free—relieved of a gigantic burden.

---

===Chinese===
「噢！」

「来吗？」

「**不**去！我要叫妈妈带我去看海。她会的。」

「那么——再见了！」

她又用美丽的花装点弗勒里丝的蕨裙——编了一顶花冠，一条花带。一阵香风起来，把伊佩西普的花香送到弗勒里丝身边。几只蝴蝶也随风飘到她那里。伊佩西普踮起脚尖立了一瞬：随即快如闪电，她旋身一跃而去，自由了——卸下了一副千斤重担。

---

===Original===
She went up to the lovely hill and stayed there a few days, amid the dancing butterflies and the gorgeous roses. At the lake she would dream hour after hour and watch the little jewelled minnows playing about the white stones and shining pebbles. In the evening she crept into a great bed of thick vines with flowers of white and gold, and listened to the lapping of the waves and watched the twinkling fireflies. They were her favourites, those poor ignorant little insects. She loved them as well as the delicate, gauzy butterflies, the sweeping swallows with their slim white wings, or the great gold-and-black bees. She adored them all, but the tiny blue-black fireflies, with thin gauze wings and the spot of phosphorescence showing now and then, were perhaps the loveliest of all. How she liked to see them playing about at dusk, sparkling and gleaming—little stars of the trees, in golden waves across the sky.

Sometimes, when they began to come out, she would go forth and dance and skip with myriads of them clustered in her hair. Around each invisible fern and blossom in her dress would gather a row of the little insects, until finally one could have seen her entire form bordered with fireflies. And besides these which alighted on her dress, thousands gathered swarming about her, so that her head was entirely hidden in a maze of gold.

Sometimes she would sleep at night and in the daytime play with the butterflies, birds, and bees. But now she began to sleep more and more in the day and play about at night.

===Chinese===
她上那座可爱的山丘去，在翩翩的蝴蝶和绚烂的玫瑰间住了几天。在湖边，她一小时一小时地做梦，看那些宝石似的小鲦鱼绕着白石与发亮的卵石嬉戏。傍晚，她钻进一大架厚密的藤蔓——开着白花与金花——听波浪拍岸，看萤火虫明明灭灭。它们是她的心头好，那些可怜的、懵懂的小虫子。她爱它们，如同爱轻盈纱翅的蝴蝶、爱掠空而过、生着细长白翅的燕子、爱金黑相间的大蜜蜂。她样样都崇拜，可那些蓝黑色的小萤火虫，薄纱似的翅，一点磷光时时闪露，也许是一切里最可爱的。她多么爱看它们在黄昏四下玩耍，闪闪、灼灼——树间的小星星，一道道金浪掠过天空。

有时，它们刚出来，她便走出去，同簇拥在她发间的千百只萤火虫一起跳舞、蹦跳。她裙上每一茎看不见的蕨草、每一朵看不见的花，都会围上一小排小虫子，到后来，简直能看见她整个人形镶了一圈萤火。除了落在裙上的这些，还有成千上万只嗡嗡地聚在她四周，她的头整个儿隐没在一片金色的迷宫里。

有时她夜里睡觉，白天同蝴蝶、鸟儿和蜜蜂玩耍。可现在，她渐渐白天越睡越多，夜里起来四下游玩。

===Original===
One cool morning Eepersip went down the lovely hill that she and Fleuriss had found. She walked down and then out toward the pastured side of the hill. Here she stayed for a long time. She lived in the golden smell of steeple-bush, and instead of the wild strawberries that she had had on the hill she found great crops of blueberries. And in this pasture she had a sample of a new food—checkerberries. To be sure, she had eaten the leaves often enough, but to see the waxen white berries was quite new. These also she tasted and found greatly to her liking. She would lie and eat hundreds of those white berries which tasted of the woods. They were almost as good as the blueberries.

Now this pasture formed a steep hill, and one delicious morning when a soft, warm wind was blowing rather strongly, Eepersip climbed to the top of it. And oh, what a sight met her dark brown eyes! Far and near, far and near rose mountains, mountains, mountains! Stretching away, fold after fold, layer after layer, rose marvellous blue peaks, with the dazzling light of the sun brightening the white granite at some of their tops. Peak after peak rose up around her, lake after lake stretched out in the dim blue distance, with the sun striking them until they were a mass of gold, like great precious stones in that setting of purple mountains. She could make out three or four farmhouses, but no villages. She stood there entranced, watching.

===Chinese===
一个凉爽的早晨，伊佩西普下了她和弗勒里丝发现的那座可爱的山丘。她一路向下，又朝山丘放牧的一侧走出去。她在这儿盘桓了很久。她生活在尖塔绣线菊金色的香气里；山上原有的野草莓换成了成片成片的蓝莓。而在这片牧场上，她尝到了一种新的吃食——冬绿莓。不错，那叶子她是常吃的，可见到蜡白的浆果却头一遭。这她也尝了，觉得极合口味。她常常躺着，吃上百来颗那种带着林子味道的白浆果。它们几乎同蓝莓一样好。

这片牧场本身构成一座陡山。一个惬意的早晨，温软的风刮得正紧，伊佩西普爬上了它的顶。啊，怎样一幅景象映进她深棕色的眼睛！远远近近，远远近近，耸起的是山，山，山！一褶又一褶、一层又一层地铺展开去，耸起奇妙的蓝色峰峦，耀眼的阳光照亮了其中几座山顶上的白色花岗岩。一座又一座峰在她四周拔起，一片又一片湖在朦胧的蓝色远处铺开，阳光照着它们，把照成了一片金，像紫色群山这方托座上的巨大宝石。她辨认得出三四座农舍，却没有村庄。她站在那儿，出神地望着。

===Original===
Then down she dashed, through the tall grass sprinkled with buttercups and daisies. It seemed miles, but it also seemed no more than seconds. At last she found herself by the shore of a cobalt lake. It was almost perfectly round, with a group of tiny green islets sprinkled in it like a handful of emerald beads. No house could Eepersip see, for the lake was entirely surrounded with low green-blue hills. The shore was for the most part soft white sand, fine as pepper. With a cry of joy at the discovery of this beautiful little lake Eepersip dashed into it and swam in the cool of those waters from the mountains. And then she saw, playing up and down in the shallow water just off one of those many beaches, a shoal of slim fishes. They were all silver except one or two that were gold, and they had rather bulging red eyes.

For a long time Eepersip watched them. Then something caused her to look up. This something was the strange, shrill cry of a bird above her. She looked up suddenly and saw the bird. But she did not watch it, for the glint of something white—a strange whiteness which she had never seen before—caught her eye. She gazed long upon it, until, when her eyes became accustomed, she was able to make out the outline of a peak, going up sharp as a tooth, with bumps of smoother outline stretching away, away into the blue immensity of space on either side.

===Chinese===
随即她一冲而下，穿过洒满毛茛和雏菊的高草。仿佛走了好几英里，又仿佛不过几秒。最后她发觉自己已站在一座钴蓝色的湖边。那湖几乎是正圆的，湖里撒着一小群绿色的小岛，像一把翡翠珠子。伊佩西普看不见一所房子，因为这湖四面全被蓝绿色的低丘围拢。湖岸大半是软软的白沙，细得像胡椒面。发现了这座美丽的小湖，伊佩西普欢呼一声扎进水里，在那来自群山的沁凉湖水中游起泳来。随后她看见，就在那许多片湖滩之一旁边的浅水里，一群体形细长的鱼上下游动。它们全是银色的，只有一两条是金色，眼睛微微鼓凸，红红的。

伊佩西普久久看着它们。随后有什么引她抬起头来。那是头顶上一只鸟奇异的尖啸。她猛地抬头，看见了那鸟。可她没看它，因为一点白色的微光——一种她从未见过的、陌生的白——攫住了她的眼睛。她久久凝望，直到眼睛惯了，才辨认出一座山峰的轮廓，尖得像一颗牙直插上去，两侧延展着轮廓较圆缓的峰峦，一直、一直伸进蓝色的无垠太空里。

===Original===
"Oh," said Eepersip, "a dream! Oh, what a beautiful dream! But—I feel so wide awake."

She gazed and gazed, silent.

"Oh," she said again, after a while, "it cannot be a dream, it mustn't be a dream!"

She gazed and gazed again.

"Oh," she repeated, "I must go there at once! The snowy mountains!"

===Chinese===
「哦，」伊佩西普说，「一个梦！啊，多美的梦！可是——我觉得自己这么清醒。」

她望着，望着，一言不发。

「哦，」过了片刻她又说道，「它不会是梦，它不能是梦！」

她又望着，望着。

「哦，」她再说一遍，「我必须立刻到那儿去！雪山！」

===Original===
She plunged into the beautiful, icy lake and swam across it with never a thought of the beauty in the green depths around her. Her eyes were fixed upon that one thing only. Soon she reached the opposite shore, consisting only of thick woods. Her heart—that heart beautiful, yet with a certain sense of childlikeness in it which had never left her—was mad for a glimpse of those mountains. It was then that she felt as if there were a great bird in her, pulling her, hauling her forward, regardless of the thorns and nettles which tore her delicate dress of ferns and blossoms. At last she got through the forest and found herself in an open meadow, with the wondrous mountain before her and warm rain falling gently. She saw a farmhouse, and as she went along the simple peasant farmer saw her and muttered to his wife: "Look there, Mary." Mary looked, and then she said: "Ay, God hath taken this child into his care—ignorance demands mercy."

A moment of intense thought. She gazed and gazed, bewitched. Then she gave, or tried to give, a little laugh. It did not sound. "Oh," she tried to say, "how *queer* I feel! I believe I never felt so queer." And indeed she did feel queer. For she felt the feeling of speaking to her heart. She was talking, it seemed to her, loudly, but when, even in the midst of her talk, she listened, nothing sounded.

===Chinese===
她一头扎进那美丽冰冷的湖，游了过去，全然顾不上四周绿色深处之美。她的眼睛只盯住那一样东西。很快她到了对岸，岸上尽是密林。她的心——那颗美丽的心，心里还存着一种从未离开过的孩子气——发疯般想看一眼那些山。就在那时，她觉得自己体内仿佛有一只大鸟，拽着她、拖着她向前，任凭荆棘和荨麻撕扯她蕨草与花朵织成的柔嫩衣裙。她终于穿过森林，发现自己置身一片开阔的草甸，奇妙的山就在眼前，温热的雨轻轻落着。她望见一座农舍，她走过时，那朴实的庄稼汉瞧见了她，低声对妻子说：「瞧那儿，玛丽。」玛丽望了望，说道：「哎，上帝已把这孩子收在他看顾之下——无知者需要怜悯。」

一刹那深沉的思虑。她望着，望着，中了魔一般。随后她笑了——或者说，想笑一笑。却没笑出声。「哦，」她想说话，「我感觉多么*古怪*啊！我相信我从没这么古怪过。」她也确实觉得古怪。因为她感到了对自己的心说话的感觉。在她自己听来，她正大声说着话，可即使在说到半途侧耳去听，也什么声音都没有。

===Original===
After a few seconds, it seemed, she ran on, leaping through the wet. Raindrops gathered on the ferns and the flowers of her dress, outlining them with the pearly water. She looked like a rain-fairy. Hour after hour passed, and she went like the wind itself; yet she did not tire. At last she found herself near the foot of that wondrous mountain, shimmering with snowfields, cold white against a deepening night sky.

That night a bird of modest wood-colour, with speckled breast, sang of moonlight; and, rippling faintly, softly, came echoes from his silver-tongued mate.
