They sang, and they answered, and the moon-frost-tipped pines were quiet, and clouds floated near, snowy palaces of silence. Spellbound, Eepersip was borne away to fairy kingdoms where she danced and where birds sang the only melody in the world.

---

===Chinese===
仿佛过了几秒钟，她又跑起来，在湿漉漉的雨中一路跳跃。雨珠聚在她裙上的蕨草和花上，用珍珠似的水勾出它们的轮廓。她像一位雨中仙子。一小时又一小时过去，她走得像风本身一样；却不疲倦。最后，她发觉自己已临近那座奇妙的山脚下——雪原莹莹闪光，衬着渐深的夜空，一片冷白。

那天夜里，一只羽毛朴素的木色鸟儿——胸脯带斑点——唱起月光；随后，他的伴侣那银铃似的嗓音轻轻、柔柔地漾出回声。它们唱着，应答着，月霜缀尖的松树静静的，云朵浮在近旁，一座座白雪的寂静宫殿。伊佩西普着了魔似的，被带往仙人的国度，在那儿跳舞，在那儿听鸟儿唱世上唯一的旋律。

---

===Original===
The next morning the sun came out and shone through every raindrop in splendid crimsons and purple-greens. Eepersip looked about her and discovered a little plant with a peculiar flower of white and crimson. She found that its leaves were quite delicious, unlike anything that she had had in the meadow or by the shore of the sea. They were green—a strange pale green, delicately outlined and veined in marble white and pale gold. Eepersip loved their pleasant flavour, but could not bear to touch them, they were so beautiful.

Then she looked up and beheld the strange rough outline of the mountain, and far in the distance, almost on the top, was a great snowfield, on which the sun shone directly, covering it incredibly with brilliant tints and shades of gold. And, oh, the bright green foliage, shining in the dear golden light!

"Fairyland!" whispered Eepersip. "I loved the meadow, I loved the sea more, but even before I am really *in* the mountains, I love them the best of all." Then, after a pause, she added: "That snowfield of gold, these heavenly little flowers—oh, such beauty!"

After a few more moments of breathless gazing, gazing upon everything, she started up the mountain. The first few hundred yards she followed a faintly marked trail which led through dense woods, over great boulders covered with dark green moss. Occasionally a little rushing brook trickled across her path. For quite a way Eepersip kept climbing over the huge boulders, and the path was very mossy. After a while it began to grow fainter and harder to follow, and at last it was shut off entirely by the thick bushes and trees which surrounded it. Here she sat down to rest and to think a while.

===Chinese===
第二天早上，太阳出来了，穿过每一滴雨珠，映出绚烂的绯红与紫绿。伊佩西普环顾四周，发现一株小小的植物，开着一朵奇特的、白里透绯的花。她发现它的叶子十分可口，同她在草地上或海边吃过的任何东西都不一样。叶子是绿的——一种奇异的淡绿，精巧地镶着大理石白与浅金色的边和脉络。伊佩西普喜欢那可口的味道，却下不去手碰它们——它们太美了。

随后她抬头，望见那山粗粝奇崛的轮廓；远远地，几乎在山顶上，是一片巨大的雪原，太阳正照在上面，给盖上了难以置信的、一层层明亮的金色光彩。还有，啊，那鲜绿的叶子，在可爱的金光里闪闪发亮！

「仙境！」伊佩西普低声说。「我爱过草地，我更爱大海，可我还没真正*进到*山里，就已经最爱它们了。」顿了顿，她又说：「那金色的雪原，这些天堂似的小花——啊，这样的美！」

又屏息凝望了几眼——把一切都望进眼里——她便启程上山了。开头几百码，她顺着一条依稀的小径走，小径穿过密林，翻过覆着墨绿苔藓的巨砾。不时有一条湍急的小溪浅浅淌过路面。好长一段路，伊佩西普都在那些巨砾上攀爬，径上生满苔藓。过了一阵，小径渐渐模糊，越来越难认，最后被围拢的密树灌丛整个儿封死了。她在这儿坐下，歇一歇，想一想。

===Original===
She looked about and came upon a bubbling spring, at which she drank. No water she had ever found was like this. It tasted of the strong, delicious mountain air. She drank deeply, and, when she had quenched her thirst, continued her way. Here flowers which made her think of foam at sea—white, starlike, with silver-tipped petals—twined themselves among the trees mingled with wild roses—dawn-flowers of deep pink or sun-bright yellow. Strange orchids grew about, many of them pure white and fringed like fluffy clouds. One had green blossoms with long whitish spurs—mystic flowers on tall spikes with two smooth leaves. Yellow lady-slippers made her think of butterflies with folded wings, or of the sun peeping out from dark clouds. But the loveliest of all were pink orchids—hosts of them with more deeply tinted lips fringed like fairies' fingers: hosts of them on slender stems, each stem a dawn-sprite's wand.

"Like the dawn I saw once," she thought, "when snow-pink fringey flowers wreathed the sky. The sun was pleased and smiled. I danced for him, and the bobolinks and skylarks greeted him with song." There were tall flowers, too, pink silk beneath white tissue, with very dark and curious leaves up the stalks among the blossoms. Butterflies were playing like sun rays, winging softly from flower to flower. And as she went on she passed through forests of thick bushes and poisonous thorns, open pine-groves, and great pastures smelling of hay-scented ferns and budding steeple-bush. All the time the path, or rather the easiest way through the thick bushes, had been fairly level, but now it began to shoot up steeply, and it was all Eepersip could do to keep herself from sliding back in an avalanche of pebbles and stones. A bit of tough scrambling followed, and at last she broke out on a comparatively level piece of ground on one side of which was a deep ravine in which she heard a brook rushing and rippling. On the other side of the ravine was a peak of the mountain, crowned with snow and with the sun flashing upon it.

===Chinese===
她四下张望，撞见一汪汩汩的泉水，便喝了。她喝过的水从没有一样及得上这个。水里带着浓烈而可口的山的气息。她痛饮一番，解了渴，继续赶路。这一带有种种花儿叫她想起海上的泡沫——白的，星形的，瓣尖带银——缠绕在树间，同野玫瑰混在一处——深粉或阳亮黄色的黎明之花。四下长着奇异的兰花，许多纯白，流苏边像蓬松的云。有一种开绿花，拖着长长的白距——高高的花穗上的神秘花朵，配着两片光滑的叶。黄杓兰叫她想起合翅的蝴蝶，或从乌云里窥出来的太阳。可最可爱的还是粉红的兰花——成片成片，唇瓣着色更深，流苏像仙子的手指：成片成片立在细茎上，每一茎都是一位黎明精灵的魔杖。

「像我从前见过的一次黎明，」她想，「雪粉色的流苏花环满了天空。太阳欢喜，笑了。我为他跳舞，食米鸟和云雀唱歌迎他。」还有高高的花，白纱底下衬着粉红的绸，茎上花间生着极深色、极古怪的叶子。蝴蝶像阳光一样玩耍，柔柔地从一朵花飞向另一朵花。她一路走去，穿过厚密的灌丛与毒刺的林子、开阔的松林，和大片大片泛着草香蕨与初蕾尖塔绣线菊气味的牧场。这一路，小径——不如说是穿过密灌最容易走的道——一直相当平缓，可现在它陡然直插上去，伊佩西普使出浑身解数，才没在一阵卵石碎石的雪崩里滑下去。接着又艰难地攀爬了一阵，她终于闯出到一块比较平整的地面上；一侧是一道深壑，壑里她听得见一条溪奔涌、哗响。壑的那一边是山的一座峰，戴着雪冠，阳光在雪上灼灼闪耀。

===Original===
Eepersip longed to see the brook, which, by the sound, she judged to be quite large. She was not actually afraid to go down over those steep walls of dirt and sand, but she *was* rather afraid that, once being down, she would not be able to get up again. So on she went, and it grew so steep that, even by digging her feet into every crevice and clutching the roots of the trees, which were getting much scarcer and more stunted, she could just manage to cling on.

But at last a change came. She stood on that high peak, on which there were only bare rocks and a little snow, no roots or plants. On either side it went down, down, and it was getting late in the afternoon. She could see nothing to do. Still the highest peak was many miles ahead, and she knew that she could not make it in the remaining daylight. So she climbed warily down into a little crevice, where a few ferns and luscious mountain blueberries managed to grow. She ate a supper of these and of another hardy little berry which she found; then slept in peace till daybreak, her tired mind dreaming of strange things—of deep palaces at the bottom of the sea and snow palaces at the tops of the mountains; of fairies, nymphs, and elves.

---

===Chinese===
伊佩西普多想看看那条溪——听声音，她断定它相当大。从那些陡峭的土沙壁上下去，她倒不怕；她*怕的*是，一旦下去了，就再也爬不上来。于是她继续走，路陡到这地步：哪怕把脚插进每一道石缝、抓住树根——树也越来越稀、越来越矮——她也才勉强攀得住。

可终于有了变化。她站上了那座高峰，峰上只有光秃秃的岩石和少许积雪，没有根株，没有草木。两侧都是向下、向下，而时候已近傍晚。她看不出还有什么可做。最高的那座峰还在前面好多英里，她知道剩下的天光里赶不到了。于是她小心地爬下一条小小的岩缝，缝里居然长着几茎蕨草和甘美的高山蓝莓。她拿这些，加上又找到的一种顽强的浆果，当了晚饭；随后安安稳稳一觉睡到天亮，疲倦的头脑里做着种种怪梦——梦见海底深处的宫殿和山顶的雪宫；梦见小仙子、仙女和精灵。

---

===Original===
In the morning she breakfasted on the mountain blueberries again, and found, much to her delight, that they quenched her thirst almost as well as water. After her juicy breakfast she went on down for about a mile; then up, up again on sheer walls of rock, where there was not a sign of a plant of any kind. After a stretch of difficult climbing snow again began to appear, as the slope became more level. Eepersip went down through a snug hollow in the rocks, where it was thick with small, scrubby trees and where very little snow had managed to penetrate the thick branches.

Oh, but it was cold up here on these tremendous heights; the wind was keen and shrilly whistling. But, however cold, it was a mountain wind, an exhilarating mountain wind which made Eepersip leap into the air—leap and dance as on the meadow. Then, after she had rested a while under the welcome branches of the stunted firs and eaten tart mountain blueberries again, she went on, up out of the hollow and on to the solid rock covered with deep snow, into which she sank at every step. Another mile she trudged along, pulling herself through it. And still the mighty peak retreated before her, so that she could make no progress—or, at least, it seemed so. It seemed as far away and as faint in the snowy distance as from where she had been when the night had come on—a dreaming peak caressed with fingers of mist.

===Chinese===
早上她又拿高山蓝莓当了早饭，并且大为欢喜地发现，它们解渴几乎同水一样灵。吃完这顿多汁的早饭，她继续向下走了约一英里；随后又向上、向上，爬上光溜溜的石壁，那上面连一星植物的影子也没有。攀过一段艰难的路，随着坡势转平，雪又开始出现了。伊佩西普从岩石间一处安适的洼地里穿下去，洼地里密密生着矮小的树木，厚厚的枝条底下只渗进了很少的雪。

哦，可在这骇人的高处真冷啊；风尖利刺骨，呜呜地尖啸。然而不管多冷，这是山风，一阵叫人振奋的山风，逗得伊佩西普腾身跃起——又跳又舞，就像在草地上那样。随后，她在矮小的冷杉欢迎的枝条下歇了一会儿，又吃了些酸酸的高山蓝莓，便继续走，从洼地上去，踏上覆着厚雪的坚石，每一步都陷进雪里。她又跋涉了一英里，一路拽着自己穿过深雪。而那雄伟的巨峰仍在她面前退却，叫她寸步难进——至少看来如此。它在雪茫茫的远处，显得同夜里降临时她所在的地方望过去一样遥远、一样依稀——一座做梦的峰，被雾的手指轻轻爱抚。

===Original===
At last the ground went up abruptly again. However steep, Eepersip found it much easier, being there wasn't so much snow. It rose and rose, becoming more gradual, until she stood on another high peak, looking off over a tremendous range of mountains. Large flakes of snow were falling gently, so that she could not see much of these. She thought that she was now on the highest peak, and she sat down to wait for the snow to cease and give her a clear view. After a time it did; and then, and not until then, she saw another peak, the true summit of the mountain, going up, up, and up on the other side of a deep valley into which she would have to descend. After sucking a few handfuls of the pure mountain snow, she set off with a light heart and a happy spirit, her feet falling fast through the light drifts. After a while she got down into the valley; and here she came upon a brooklet full of icicles, winding through the long ravine and dashing over the green slimy rocks in great cascades of rattling icicles and foam. Eepersip drank deeply, and was refreshed.

===Chinese===
终于，地面又陡然上升。尽管陡，伊佩西普却觉得好走多了，因为雪没那么深了。路升啊升，渐渐平缓，直到她站上又一座高峰，俯瞰一片莽莽的山岭。大片大片的雪轻轻落下，叫她看不真切。她以为这已是最高峰，便坐下等雪停，好还她一个清朗的视野。过了一阵，雪果真停了；就在那时，也直到那时，她才望见另一座峰——山的真正极顶——在深谷的另一侧升啊，升啊，升上去；她还得到那谷底下去呢。吸了几捧纯净的山雪之后，她心情轻快、兴致勃勃地出发了，双脚飞快地踏过一层层浅雪。过了一阵，她下到谷底；在这里，她遇见一条挂满冰凌的小溪，在长长的峡谷里蜿蜒，翻过绿滑的岩石，泻下一挂挂咔啦作响的冰凌与泡沫的大瀑布。伊佩西普痛饮了一场，精神一爽。

===Original===
Then, after resting a few moments, she went on, up that steep wall of snow and rock which would take her to the longed-for summit. Eepersip counted sixteen brooklets rushing down over it, carrying hundreds of icicles with their currents, foaming and dashing with spray and myriads of shiny iridescent bubbles.

Across brook after brook she went, watching the colours change in the dazzling snowflakes. The sun was shining brilliantly now, making everything unimaginably beautiful in magnificent shades of ruby, copper, silver-gold, emerald, and sapphire. Each snowflake seemed covered with an almost invisible layer of tiny sparkling gems. And once, when Eepersip sat down in a deep snowbank to watch and to rest, the sun happened to strike directly on one of the many brooklets that went dashing down the mountainside, making it a blinding ribbon of silver and gold. Occasionally Eepersip saw the blossoms of the beautiful talatuna, with ruby-red leaves and blossoms of pale green and changing white. She thought that the leaves were *all* red, but when the wind flipped one over she saw that their backs were moon-white, pale but glistening.

On she went, through the incredible beauty of the fairyland about her. "Oh," she murmured to herself, "how marvellous it is! Oh, fairies, fairies." She whirled happily around. She had felt a few delicate touches on her shoulders, and at once the air was a-flock with glistening snowflakes. Each fern in her dress was bordered with a row of the fairy things, and her autumn hair was crowned white.

===Chinese===
随后，歇了几分钟，她继续走，爬上那道雪与岩的陡壁——它将带她登上渴望已久的极顶。伊佩西普数了数，有十六条小溪从壁上奔泻而下，水流卷着成百的冰凌，带着水沫与千千万万亮闪闪的虹彩泡泡，翻腾飞溅。

她跨过一条又一条溪，看那耀眼的雪花里变幻的颜色。此刻阳光灿烂，把万物照得美得难以想象——一片片华彩的红宝石色、古铜色、金银色、翡翠色和蓝宝石色。每一片雪花仿佛都覆着一层几不可见的、闪烁的小宝石。有一回，伊佩西普坐在一个深雪堆里观望歇息，太阳恰好直照在山坡上奔泻而下的许多小溪中的一条上，把它照成一条晃眼的金银缎带。伊佩西普不时看见美丽的塔拉图纳（talatuna）的花——叶子宝石红，花淡绿与变幻的白。她原以为叶子*通体*是红的，可风掀翻一片时，她看见叶背是月光白，淡，却莹莹有光。

她继续走，穿过四周仙境里那难以置信的美。「哦，」她喃喃自语，「这多么奇妙啊！哦，小仙子，小仙子。」她快乐地旋了个身。她觉出肩上有几下轻柔的触碰，随即，空气里便落满亮晶晶的雪花。她裙上的每一茎蕨草都镶了一排仙物，她秋色的头发顶上了一顶白冠。

===Original===
After a while a slight breeze sprang up and the big flakes whirled faster. The breeze rose and rose until it was a strong, cold wind, and she could not see a foot before her. The only thing to do was to wait for clear weather. But in that she was disappointed, for it was growing darker and darker, and at last she realized that night was coming on. So she lay down and ate a supper of snow, as it fell and fell.

---

===Chinese===
过了一阵，起了微微的风，大片的雪花旋得更快了。风越刮越大，直到成为一阵劲厉的寒风，她连脚前一尺也看不见了。唯一的办法只有等天放晴。可这一点她失望了，因为天越来越暗，她终于明白，夜来了。于是她躺下，就着纷纷、纷纷落下的雪，吃了一顿雪的晚饭。

---

===Original===
All night the snow whirled and whirled, and in the morning Eepersip was completely buried. It was a long, hard task to find her way out, or rather to push her way out, for almost as fast as the snow fell it froze into ice, so that there was on top of Eepersip a thick layer of ice. But just before she decided to give up and wait for warmer weather, she broke through. Out into the bright sunlight she came; and lo and behold! all the ferns on her dress, and the dainty blossoms, together with her hair, were covered with a layer of ice which shimmered and sparkled in the sun like jewels set in something brighter than the brightest gold.

But as soon as she came out into the sun the ice began to melt and run off in all directions, and as she skipped and jumped about she was almost hidden in the shower of water-drops which flew from her as she ran.

And how beautiful, how fairy-like, she was! Each fern was covered with a thin layer of the melting ice, and the crown of pink blossoms around her curly hair was frozen likewise, their fair colour persisting through the ice. Once in a while, when the sun touched her, she was a blaze of colour—of silver and gold, with here and there a splotch of brilliant red as the sun struck a red flower.

===Chinese===
整整一夜，雪旋啊旋啊，到早上，伊佩西普被埋了个严严实实。找路出去——不如说是闯出去——是一场漫长而艰苦的活计，因为雪几乎一下来就冻成了冰，伊佩西普身上压了厚厚一层冰壳。可就在她决定放弃、等天气转暖之前，她破壳而出了。她来到明亮的阳光里；看哪！她裙上所有的蕨草、娇嫩的花朵，连同她的头发，都覆着一层冰，在太阳里莹莹闪闪，像嵌在比最亮的金子还亮的东西上的珠宝。

可她一来到太阳底下，冰便开始融化，向四面八方淌下来；她蹦啊跳啊，一路飞出的水珠几乎把她整个人罩没了。

而她多么美，多么像仙子啊！每一茎蕨草都覆着一层薄薄的、正融化的冰，她鬈发四周那圈粉色花冠也同样冻着，娇美的颜色透过冰层依然留存。时不时，太阳照到她时，她便燃起一片颜色的光焰——银与金，这儿一块、那儿一块鲜亮的红，那是太阳照上了一朵红花。

===Original===
After she had found that there was nothing to be eaten except snow, she sucked a few handfuls, flavoured with the petals of the flowers which she wore. Then she went on, through paradises of silver, gold, and red, through deep hollows of shining green. Everything was something besides white, and the world that was in Eepersip's range of vision was fairyland.

But, as she went on, clouds began to float in—little white clouds. They grew thicker and thicker, until, before she had come near the highest peak, there was nothing but pearly mist—scudding grey mist, curling into fantastic shapes as it rose. She could see nothing, and she sat down in the snow to wait. That night a gale came up, whistling and howling around the peaks, reminding Eepersip of that storm at sea. What an awesome sound it made! It sleeted, too, and when she awoke the next morning the snow was covered with a crust. The mist had partly cleared, and she pushed on again. She went through icy hollows and up on shimmering peaks, until, finally, she saw near her that long-sought summit, and, with a shout of joy, she dashed up. Fast she went, but when she really reached it at last, the mist had closed in again, the wind was up, and it was sleeting furiously. It was only through a break in the mist that she had made the summit at all.

---

===Chinese===
她发现除了雪再没什么可吃的，便吸了几捧，带着她身上花瓣的香味。随后她继续走，穿过银、金与红的乐园，穿过绿光闪闪的深洼。样样东西都不是单纯的白，伊佩西普视野里的世界，就是仙境。

可她走着走着，云开始飘进来——小小的白云。云越积越厚，直到她还没挨近最高峰，天地间只剩了珍珠色的雾——疾飞的灰雾，升起时卷成种种奇形。她什么也看不见，便在雪里坐下等。那天夜里起了大风，绕着峰群呼啸、怒号，叫伊佩西普想起海上那场风暴。那声音多么骇人！还下了雨夹雪；第二天早上她醒来时，雪面上结了一层冰壳。雾散了一部分，她又奋力向前。她穿过冰冷的洼地，登上莹莹的雪峰，终于，她望见那寻访已久的极顶已近在身旁，便欢呼一声，直冲上去。她跑得飞快，可等她真正到达时，雾又合拢了，风又起了，雨夹雪下得正狂。她好歹是从雾的一线裂隙里，登上了这极顶。

---

===Original===
The next morning it was still misty, but not nearly so thick. There was even a faint purple glow over on the eastern horizon where the sun was rising. Occasionally the mist would break open above, and she would see glimpses of blue sky—the deep, deep blue of that day in the meadow with Fleuriss. And lying all around on the boulders were frost-feathers. When Eepersip first saw them she thought that she was dreaming. But no, they were really there, delicate ferns and feathers with scalloped edges—ferns and feathers of frost.

"Oh, mountain-fairies—fairies have left them here," she said quietly. Some were as long as her forearm, and others tiny—oh, so tiny; some were almost round like the inner feathers of a bird, and others long and narrow like the outer plumes. Down in a hollow were some stunted firs, laden with snow and covered with those fronds of ivory chiselled by wind-sprites, lovelier than anything Eepersip had ever seen, lovelier than anything ever made by Nature. No, Nature could never have carved them, Eepersip thought. The fairies—fairies!

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，雾还没散，却稀薄多了。东方地平线上、太阳升起的地方，甚至泛着一层淡淡的紫光。雾偶尔在上头裂开，她便瞥见几眼蓝天——同那天在草地上同弗勒里丝一起看见的一样深深的蓝。而四周的圆石上，躺满了霜羽。伊佩西普初见它们时，只当自己在做梦。可不，它们真在那儿——精巧的蕨叶和羽毛，边缘呈扇贝形——霜的蕨叶与霜的羽毛。

「哦，山中的小仙子——是小仙子们把它们留在这儿的。」她轻轻地说。有的同她的小臂一样长，有的极小——啊，那么小；有的几乎浑圆，像鸟身上贴身的绒羽，有的细长，像外围的翎羽。洼地里立着几株矮小的冷杉，压满了雪，覆着那些风之精灵凿出的象牙色叶蕨——比伊佩西普见过的一切都可爱，比大自然造出的一切都可爱。不，大自然雕不出它们来，伊佩西普想。是小仙子——小仙子！

===Original===
Once she found a hollowed rock entirely lined with them, like a fairy's crystal palace with strange shadowy recesses. They crowded everywhere they could find room, and sometimes, when there was no other place, rippled on the snow. They overlapped on the rocks, and hung from windward crags, pointing into the wind. And behold! Eepersip's dress and her head were covered with small ones, like a diadem—a fairy crown and fairy ornaments. Moving gently, so as not to disturb them from where they rested, she wandered from one cluster to another, looking carefully at each one, noting each special pattern, each magic tracery. All day she followed the winding rabbit-trails amid the feathery firs.

The sun, too, had been pushing out. Now the mist opened in one direction, and Eepersip caught a fleeting glimpse of snowy peaks; but it closed again. It opened a trifle longer in another direction, and Eepersip saw, 'way down below, first low blue-green foothills and lakes golden with the sun, then higher purple hills, melting into range after range of billowing mountains, and valley after valley filled with white clouds rapidly lifting. The mist shut in. Another direction opened in the same way, with hills fading into mountains; and far off on the horizon was another range of snow mountains, lying just under great white clouds. There were clouds hanging over the valleys too, and they cast strange shadows on the sunlit trees far below. When the mist shut in, the golden lakes seemed to stay the longest, and after the mountains had entirely disappeared they could be seen as if hanging in midair, limpid pools of gold. And more sides opened, and more, the waits growing shorter in between, until, on a gust of mountain wind, the last of the mist went scudding away, banished, and the sun broke out into the blue sky. The snow sparkled, the mountains sparkled, the lakes and rivers sparkled, the frost-feathers sparkled, the air itself sparkled. And the mountains of the range that Eepersip was in, crowned with snow, gleamed like gold. Down on one side of the snowy peak dashed a great river, green and swirling, covered with clots of foam. Sometimes it would cascade over the rocks throwing up a fountain of spray, and sometimes it would slip over a smooth slide, then, whirling round and round in a rock basin, thunder down another great cliff in a shower of bubbles, rattling icicles, and foam. It cut its way through a green hollow in the snow, and where it tunnelled under the snow-banks it was overhung with long, gleaming icicles.

===Chinese===
有一回，她发现一块凹进去的岩石，里里外外全镶满了霜羽，像一座小仙子的水晶宫，带着一个个幽影重重的深龛。它们挤满了一切挤得下的地方，有时实在没有别处，便在雪上漾出波纹。它们在岩石上层层相叠，从迎风的崖壁上垂下，指着风来的方向。看哪！伊佩西普的裙子和她头上也覆满了小小的霜羽，像一顶宝冠——小仙子的冠冕和小仙子的饰物。她轻轻地走，生怕惊动它们栖歇的地方，从一堆踱到另一堆，细细端详每一片，记下每一种独特的花样、每一道魔法的纹路。一整天，她沿着羽状冷杉间蜿蜒的兔子小径走。

太阳也在往外挤。雾先朝一个方向敞开，伊佩西普飞快地瞥见一眼雪峰；随即又合上了。它朝另一个方向敞得稍久些，伊佩西普望见老远老远的下方，先是蓝绿色的低丘和被太阳染金的湖，再是更高的紫色山丘，融进一浪又一浪翻涌的山岭，还有一道又一道山谷，盛满正迅速升起的白云。雾合拢了。又一个方向同样敞开，丘陵淡入高山；遥远的地平线上，另有一列雪山，正卧在大朵白云的下边。山谷上也悬着云，往远远下方阳光里的树上投下奇异的影子。雾合拢时，金色的湖仿佛留得最久，群山全然隐没之后，它们还看得见，像悬在半空——一泓泓澄澈的金。敞开的方向越来越多，越来越多，间隔的等待越来越短，直到一阵山风刮过，最后一片雾被吹得疾飞而去，放逐了，太阳冲进了蓝天。雪在闪，山在闪，湖与河在闪，霜羽在闪，空气本身也在闪。伊佩西普所在的那道山岭，戴着雪冠，灿灿如金。雪峰一侧的下方，一条大河奔泻，绿而湍旋，覆着团团白沫。它时而翻过岩石泻成瀑布，抛起一座水沫的喷泉；时而滑过一道平滑的石滑槽，随后在石盆里转啊转地打旋，再带着一阵泡泡、咔啦作响的冰凌和泡沫，雷一般轰下另一道大崖。它在雪中一道绿色的洼地里劈出通路；凡是钻过雪堆底下的地方，都垂着长长的、莹亮的冰凌。

===Original===
Eepersip danced in the snow, among the frost-feathers, all that day—danced like a mountain sprite, leaping high, then running gracefully in a shower of water-drops which flew from her as the frost-feathers melted in the warmth of the sunlight. She danced down to the river and played there a while—played with the white foam.

At sunset she was again at the peak of her mountain. The sky was flushed with magic; a great cloud in the west became brilliantly fringed with gold and red-gold, the east was all submerged in a lilac sea, and a delicate laciness of pink trailed across the zenith. Sunset fairies alighted on the snow-peaks: they were fiery for a moment, and all the great snowfields were flaming. Then the colour faded to pink on the summits. But in the sky Nature still flung about her colours wildly—fire was in the zenith, the long bank of clouds was vividly fringed with red-gold, and there to the south it changed to caverns of shadowed pink and strange violet. Seas and bays and cloud islands formed out of it—seas of a strange greenish rose. Then one thrill and flame of gold spread about the whole earth; the snow at her feet was shadowy gold, and a pathway of it danced upon the air 'way to the horizon. It played upon each frost-feather; the eastern mountains were flushed with this soft gold.

And then, dizzy with the colour and the beauty, Eepersip fell asleep, her fingers clutching the rosy snow.

---

===Chinese===
那一整天，伊佩西普都在雪里、在霜羽间跳舞——像一位山中的精灵，高高跃起，又在一片水珠的雨里优雅地奔跑——霜羽在阳光的暖意里融化，水珠便从她身上飞散。她一路舞到河边，在那儿玩了一会儿——同白色的泡沫玩耍。

日落时分，她又回到她的山的峰顶。天空浸透了魔法般的红晕；西天一大朵云镶起灿烂的金与红金的边，东方整个沉入一片丁香色的海，一缕精巧的粉色蕾丝曳过天顶。落日的小仙子们栖落在雪峰上：有一刻它们灼灼如火，所有巨大的雪原都燃烧起来。随后峰顶的颜色褪成了粉。可是在天上，大自然还在狂放地挥洒她的颜色——天顶燃着火，长长的云堤镶着鲜明的红金边，在南边，它变成了一个个阴影粉与奇异紫色的洞窟。海上、湾上、云的岛屿，都从这里面生出来——一片片奇异的带绿的玫瑰色的海。随后一阵战栗、一道金焰传遍整个大地；她脚边的雪成了影影绰绰的金，一道金光在空气里舞着，一直舞到天边。它抚过每一片霜羽；东边的群山也泛起这柔金的红晕。

随后，颜色与美令她晕眩，伊佩西普睡着了，手指还攥着玫瑰色的雪。

---

===Original===
The next morning the frost-feathers had almost disappeared underneath a new snowfall. The air was full of its fresh scent, as it came down gently in tremendous flakes. Here and there Eepersip saw one of the lovely blossoms of the talatuna, with those same ruby-red leaves. How beautiful they were, growing in great clusters, just peeping through the snow! Once in a while a pale cream-coloured mountain moth would flit before her. Occasional gusts brought swarms of tiny bottle-green, white-winged snow-beetles, and the air was abuzz with them. Sometimes a blue or white insect like a firefly would hover past, a strange red light gleaming about its transparent body.

On and on Eepersip explored, seeing nothing but the wonderland about her—the fairy palaces of snow, the fluttering, hovering insects, and the beautiful mountain flowers. Following the icy river down, she came sometimes to a great cascade of the green water—a cascade coming over one of those great cliffs, washing down the snow, throwing up fountains and clouds of spray in its furious descent. Sometimes it cut under the banks, making a green cave hung with icicles gleaming strangely. One of these had been made when the river was in flood; now it was large enough for Eepersip to stand in, and, wading in water about up to her knees, she went back into its innermost recesses, where the roar of the stream was muffled. There were fish there—trout playing in the whirlpools and riding swiftly with the current. She found some odd bright stones and gleaming pebbles in this mysterious place, silent save for the deadened rush of water.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，霜羽几乎全隐没在一场新雪底下了。空气里满是新雪清新的气息，大朵大朵的雪花轻轻落下。伊佩西普这儿那儿地看见一朵朵可爱的塔拉图纳的花，还是那种宝石红的叶子。它们成大簇地长着，恰好从雪里探出头来，多美啊！时不时有一只奶油色的山蛾从她面前掠过。阵阵狂风送来一群群瓶绿色的、白翅的雪甲虫，空气里嗡嗡全是它们。有时，一只萤火虫似的蓝色或白色的虫子会悬飞而过，透明的身体周围闪着一圈奇异的红光。

伊佩西普一直往前探看，眼里只有四周的奇境——雪的仙宫、翻飞悬停的虫子，和美丽的高山花。她顺着冰冷的河往下走，时时来到一挂绿水的巨瀑前——瀑布从那样的大崖上翻下来，冲开积雪，在狂怒的跌落里抛起一座座水沫的喷泉和云。有时河水切进岸底，掏出一座绿洞，挂着的冰凌奇异地闪光。其中一处是河水涨时冲出来的；如今已大得够伊佩西普站进去。她蹚着约齐膝深的水，一直走到它最里面的深处，那里水声闷闷的。里面有鱼——鳟鱼在漩涡里嬉戏，又乘着水流疾驰。在这神秘的地方，她找到些古怪的亮石头和莹莹的卵石；这里静悄悄的，只有那被隔闷了的奔流声。

===Original===
Sometimes, again, the rushing brook took such steep course that Eepersip was forced to make a detour into the woods for a little way, through clumps of the firs, now growing less stunted, but hung with icicles which clicked together in the wind, sounding to Eepersip like fairy castanets. Even at this high altitude, she saw occasionally a white pine, each cluster of pale green needles laden with snow—tufts of snow which seemed to make little faces peering out from the tree. Bursts of happiness would overwhelm her now and then, and she would leap high and dart like some frightened deer or mountain nymph.

Once she found beautiful little violet-shaped pink flowers with bowed heads and feathery leaves—snow-pinks blooming there, thrusting their buds from the snow itself. She tucked a spray of them into her dress of fluttering ferns.

And then she would return to the river and follow it again. When the moon came, dappling the foamy water with silver, she watched it as it dipped down its forehead in the stream and touched the treetops with magic. Then she would go on again through the moonlit night. Once she came to a place where the brook separated, and she had a difficulty choosing which branch to follow.

===Chinese===
有时，奔涌的溪流又取了那样陡的道，逼得伊佩西普绕进林子里走一小段，穿过一丛丛冷杉——它们如今不那么矮了，却挂着冰凌，风一吹便咔嗒相击，在伊佩西普听来像小仙子的响板。即便在这样的高处，她也不时看见一棵白松，每一束淡绿的针叶上都驮着雪——一撮撮雪仿佛成了从树里窥望出来的小脸蛋。阵阵幸福会不时将她淹没，她便高高跃起，窜出去，像一头受惊的小鹿，或一位山中的仙女。

有一回，她发现一种美丽的小花，堇菜的模样、粉色、垂着头，叶子羽毛似的——雪石竹正在那儿开放，把花蕾从雪里本身顶出来。她折了一小枝，插进她那飘飘扬扬的蕨裙里。

随后她又回到河边，继续顺流而下。月亮来了，给带沫的水面点上银斑，她望着月亮把额头浸进溪流，用魔法抚过树梢。然后她再穿过月夜往前走。有一回，她来到一处溪水分岔的地方，竟难于决定该跟哪一条岔流。

===Original===
And when the russet dawn reappeared, tipping the mountains with apple-blossom and fire, she had followed it to its goal in the very meadow from where she had started—a pool hitherto unseen by her. About a hundred feet across it was, beached with clean white pebbles. In it bloomed water-lilies, fragrant and white, with centres of gold; strange red flowers, too, she saw on the bottom, growing between the pebbles. Dragonflies with crackling wings swept over it in circles. She saw, too, a shoal of tiny fishes of a brownish colour, striped with yellow. They would suddenly dart forward as if something had frightened them, and then poise themselves stock-still, mimicking so many sticks in the shadows of the abundant lily-pads.

She was wading about in the pool when suddenly—where there had been ground for her foot to rest on, nothing was there. The bottom of the pool under her foot had slid forward and collapsed! Suddenly "*Clug-glug, clug-glug, chugarum, glug!*" reached her, as a big bullfrog's nose appeared by the side of a lily-pad. A second later the frog diked up on the lily-pad and stared at Eepersip with his goggly eyes. She burst out laughing, he looked so ridiculous staring at her like that.

She stayed in the meadow, playing gaily among the leaves and flowers. Butterflies of all the colours of the rainbow swept over it in great flocks. Flowers bloomed so thickly that there was hardly any grass—white ones with waxen petals, striped and bordered with heavy golden bands; red ones with centres of dark green-gold; great blossoms of pink and purple, whose petals fluttered about in the breeze like butterflies.

===Chinese===
等到赤褐色的黎明再现，给群山点上苹果花与火，她已跟着溪水到了它的尽头——正是她当初出发的那片草地——那儿有一泓她从未见过的水池。池约莫一百英尺宽，岸边铺着干净的白卵石。池里开着睡莲，芬芳雪白，中心一点金；池底她还看见古怪的红花，生在卵石之间。翅膀脆响的蜻蜓一圈圈掠过池面。她还看见一小群褐色的、带黄条纹的小鱼。它们会忽然向前一窜，像被什么惊着了，随即定住不动，装成一根根小棍，藏在密密层层的莲叶影子里。

她正在池里蹚水，忽然——原本有地面容她落脚的地方，什么也没有了。她脚下的池底向前滑塌了下去！忽然，「*咕嘟噜、咕嘟噜、恰加勒姆、咕嘟！*」传进她耳朵，一只大牛蛙的鼻子从一片莲叶旁冒了出来。一秒钟后，青蛙蹦上莲叶，鼓着眼睛瞪伊佩西普。她放声大笑起来——它那样瞪着她的样子，实在太滑稽了。

她留在草地里，在叶丛花间快活地玩耍。彩虹各色的蝴蝶成大群成大群地掠过草地。花开得那么密，几乎不见草——白的，蜡质的花瓣，描着粗重的金色条纹与镶边；红的，中心是深绿金色；粉紫的大花，花瓣在微风里扑闪，像一只只蝴蝶。

===Original===
One morning she was awakened early by "*Peep, peep, twitter-itter‑ee‑e‑e‑e‑e‑e, twit chirup, twitter‑ee‑e‑e, twit!*" She looked up and saw a great flock of snow-white birds with long narrow wings. They were flying northward. The flock was much more gigantic than Eepersip had supposed, for it kept on until she began to think that it was going round and round. But no: after ten or fifteen minutes the sky cleared, and she heard faintly in the distance: "*Twitter-itter‑ee‑e‑e‑e‑e, **ee‑e‑per‑s‑sip!** e‑e‑p, e‑ep, chirup.*"

Day after day she danced here, playing, as on the first meadow, the butterflies, flowers, and swallows. And now, as she danced, she seemed to float through the air, her feet almost motionless. Sometimes she would leap high and come down—*float* down—quite slowly. She seemed to have no weight at all, and a breeze would almost lift her off the ground and hold her up in the air. Indeed, when she ran with the wind behind her she would be *blown* along—blown like a leaf just above the flowers.

---

===Chinese===
一天早晨，她被早早吵醒了——「*啾，啾，啁啁-啾啾-咿-咿-咿-咿-咿，啾啾唧啾，啁啁-咿-咿-咿，啾！*」她抬头一看，望见一大群雪白的鸟，翅膀细长。它们正往北飞。那鸟群比伊佩西普以为的大得多得多，因为它一直过、一直过，过得她开始以为它在绕圈儿。可不：过了十或十五分钟，天空净了，她隐隐听得远处传来：「*啁啁-啾啾-咿-咿-咿-咿，**咿-咿-佩-尔-西普！**咿-咿普，咿-咿普，唧啾。*」

她日复一日在这儿跳舞，同第一片草地时一样，同蝴蝶、花朵和燕子玩耍。如今她舞着的时候，仿佛浮在空气里穿行，双脚几乎不动。有时她高高跃起，落下——*飘*落——下来，慢慢的。她仿佛全无重量，一阵微风几乎就能把她托离地面，擎在空中。真的，当风从背后吹着她跑，她简直是被*吹*着走的——像一片叶子，恰恰飘在花的上方。

---

===Original===
One day she was dancing there—dancing and leaping in the long grass, amid the blossoms. Butterflies drifted over the sunny field—butterflies of red and yellow, blue and green, black and white, orange and purple. How gracefully they flew; how delicately they alighted on the flowers; how fairy-like they were, hovering for an instant over some blossom, then dipping their wings and starting off again! Eepersip felt as though—as though she were going to be one of them; as though she were so happy that she must fly about with them, sip the honey from the flowers with them.

As she was thinking happily she heard a few faint peeps, which became louder as she danced toward a certain part of the field. Then there was a desperate twitter right at her feet, and, looking down she saw a yellow fledgling hopping towards her. She picked him up carefully and saw that he had broken his left wing. She worked a moment with her hands and pulled the bone into place. Then she made him a comfortable nest of grass and set out to see where he had come from. Looking up, she saw a nest from which a bird was peering about anxiously. Straightway she took the little one from the nest *she* had made, and climbed the tree with it to its own nest; upon which the mother-bird gave a twitter of joy.

After doing this Eepersip descended the tree and continued her happy dance with the butterflies until evening. Then they all found shelters under the leaves, and the stars came out, one by one. Presently Eepersip spied a flicker in the meadow—then another and still another, until the fireflies were out in full play. They gathered around Eepersip in one flaming mass, kissing her with their feathery wings. Making her way over to the pool, she saw her reflection, a shimmer of gold.

===Chinese===
一天，她正在那儿跳舞——在长草里、花丛间又舞又跳。蝴蝶在阳光照耀的原野上漂移——红的黄的、蓝的绿的、黑的白的、橙的紫的蝴蝶。它们飞得多么优雅；落在花上多么轻柔；它们多么像小仙子，在某朵花上悬停一瞬，随即一沉翅膀，又飞走了！伊佩西普觉得仿佛——仿佛自己就要成为它们中的一员；仿佛她幸福得必须要同它们一起飞舞，同它们一起从花里啜饮蜜。

她正想得高兴，听见几声细细的啁鸣，随着她朝田野的某个角落舞过去，那声音越来越响。随后就在她脚边，响起一阵拼命似的啾鸣；低头一看，她看见一只黄色的小雏鸟朝她跳来。她小心地捧起它，发现它的左翅折了。她用手摆弄了一阵，把骨头接回了位。随后她用草给它做了一个舒舒服服的窝，动身去看它是从哪儿来的。抬头一望，她看见一个鸟巢，一只鸟正焦急地四下张望。她立刻把小家伙从*她*做的窝里捧出来，抱着它爬上树，送回它自己的巢；鸟妈妈当即欢快地啾鸣了一声。

做完了这件事，伊佩西普下了树，同蝴蝶继续她快活的舞蹈，直到天黑。随后它们都在叶子底下找好了栖处，星星一颗一颗出来了。不多时，伊佩西普瞥见草地里一点微光——又一点，又一点，直到萤火虫全都出来玩了。它们聚在伊佩西普四周，聚成一大团火，用羽毛般的翅膀吻她。她一路走到水池边，看见自己的倒影，一片闪烁的金。

===Original===
A light darted out toward her from the woods; then another and yet another, until there were hundreds of lights flickering and blinking at her from all corners of the great field—the lights of elves and gnomes, little fairies of the field. And she danced happily among them—danced until the dawn appeared on the horizon, sending away the darkness and making the stars fade into space. It flushed the whole sky with rose, sent arms of it even as far as the west; arms and streamers of colour which paled toward their tips. Little white clouds grew pink, too, and the colour was reflected on the distant mountaintops. Again the snowfield seemed to become fire—fire which was soon quenched by the coolness of the snow. As the sun sent its first golden beams above the horizon, the colour faded, turned to yellow, and soon entirely disappeared. Then the sky was blue—deep, quivering blue, with the fluffy clouds like pearls in an azure setting.

Suddenly Eepersip saw that she was dressed in a flouncy array of spring crocuses and maidenhair ferns. Lovely flowers of pink and yellow were entwined in her hair, and butterflies fluttered around her. She danced happily and leaped high in the air. How free and light she felt in the lovely dress that had been given her!

That day Eepersip was even happier than usual. She floated about, visiting each flower, each bush and tree. She played games with the butterflies, the games she had played on the old meadow, that first summer of her life in the House without Windows. When she rested, she sat on top of a laurel-bush, and not a twig bent beneath her. The slightest breeze blew her about, changed the direction of her dance. Butterfly after butterfly flew to her, flock after flock, as if they had some message to tell her; and after each visit she was happier than before. Yes, they were messengers, these happy creatures; messengers who came to whisper her a secret—a secret from Nature, a secret of the beautiful meadow, a secret from the fairies.

===Chinese===
一点光从林间向她射来；又一点，又一点，直到千百点光从大田野的四角朝她闪烁眨眼——那是精灵和地精的光，田野的小仙子们。她在它们中间快活地跳着舞——一直跳到黎明出现在地平线上，赶走黑暗，让星星淡进太空。黎明把整个天空染成玫瑰色，把一枝枝玫瑰色一直送到遥远的西方；一臂臂、一缕缕的颜色，向尖端渐淡。小小的白云也泛起粉来，颜色还映在远处的山顶上。雪原又一次仿佛变成了火——很快便被雪的清凉浇熄的火。当太阳把最初的金光送上地平线，颜色褪了，转成黄，很快全然消隐。随后天空是蓝的——深深的、颤动的蓝，绒绒的云像宝蓝底座上的珍珠。

忽然，伊佩西普看见自己穿上了一身蓬蓬的新衣——春番红花和铁线蕨。可爱的粉花黄花缠绕在她发间，蝴蝶绕着她翻飞。她快活地跳着舞，高高跃入空中。穿着这身赠她的可爱衣裙，她觉得多么自在、多么轻盈啊！

那一天，伊佩西普比平日还要快活。她飘飘地四处游走，拜访每一朵花、每一丛灌木、每一棵树。她同蝴蝶做游戏——就是她在老草地上玩过的那些，她在无窗之屋度过的头一个夏天里玩过的。她歇息时，便坐到一丛山月桂的顶上，没有一根枝条在她身下弯折。最轻的微风也吹得她四处飘动，改换她舞蹈的方向。一只又一只蝴蝶向她飞来，一群接着一群，仿佛有什么消息要告诉她；每来一回，她都比先前更快活。是的，它们是信使，这些快活的生灵；来向她低语一个秘密的信使——一个来自大自然的秘密，一个美丽草地的秘密，一个来自小仙子们的秘密。

===Original===
And, when the sun again tinged the sky with colour, a flock of these butterflies, of purple and gold and green, came swooping and alighted on her head in a circle, the largest in front. Others came in myriads and covered her dress with delicate wing-touches. Eepersip held out her arms a moment. A gold-and-black one alighted on each wrist. And then—she rose into the air, and, hovering an instant over a great laurel-bush, vanished.

She was a fairy—a wood-nymph. She would be invisible forever to all mortals, save those few who have minds to believe, eyes to see. To these she is ever present, the spirit of Nature—a sprite of the meadow, a naiad of lakes, a nymph of the woods.

===Chinese===
当太阳又一次把天空染上颜色，一群这样的蝴蝶——紫的、金的、绿的——飞掠而来，在她头上围成一圈落下，最大的在前头。其余的千千万万只跟来，用纤巧的翅膀轻触，覆满她的衣裙。伊佩西普把手臂伸出片刻。一只金黑相间的蝴蝶落在她一边腕上。随后——她升入了空中，在一大丛山月桂上悬停了一瞬，消逝了。

她是一位小仙子——一位林中仙女。她将对一切凡人永远隐形，唯有那寥寥几个心里肯信、眼里能见的人除外。对这些人，她无处不在——她是大自然之魂——草地的精灵，湖上的水仙，林间的仙女。

## II The Sea / II 大海

===Original===
> Your flashing waves hold out their arms to me—

 I entangle myself in their silver hair,

 And ride with them to catch the wind.

 The sun trails bright jewels in the water,

 And laughs because I cannot touch them.

===Chinese===
> 你闪烁的波浪向我伸出臂膀——

 我把自己缠进它们银色的长发里，

 骑着它们去追逐风。

 太阳在水里拖曳出明亮的珠宝，

 笑我碰不到它们。

===Original===
As Eepersip journeyed on, the meadow grew dimmer in her mind, and the memory of how the sea had looked grew brighter. She couldn’t see it now, for she was in a valley; but she knew that she was going in the right direction. The spring breeze was blowing; it was not cold, and the breeze stirred the air gently, so that it wasn’t hot. Occasional whiffs from the meadow came to Eepersip with that breeze; but when she had gone about two miles this fragrance ceased.

That afternoon she came into a great forest where strange, mysterious shadows passed back and forth in a frightening way. She hurried on as fast as she could, but she had to spend a night in it—one alarming, terrible night. The next day she came out, torn and bedraggled with fighting her way through the dense thickets. Several times she had to cross rivers—some of them without a bridge, though luckily none of these was over her depth. Another day had passed. Nightfall found her wearily climbing a very high hill. The reflection of the moon showed her where the ocean was. It seemed hardly any nearer than before!

The third morning she descended into a rich and fertile valley. A small brook was winding down it, and where the weeping willows dipped into the current it bubbled and sang. This valley was the broadest that Eepersip had yet gone through. But after a long time she came out of it against a high, precipitous cliff. Up the side of this she climbed, digging her toes into the cracks between the rocks. At last she got to the top; and a long, weary climb it had been. She was now on a grassy hill where bloomed daisies shining like stars, and little buttercups of gold. There were butterflies, too, with brilliant wings, and they hovered and fluttered over the flowers. And lo! there was the ocean, nearer now, with the sun shining on it; and Eepersip could see the surf rolling and foaming. Shrill cries pierced the air—the cries of birds, of seagulls swooping inland in wide circles. And as she went on through the waving grass she could smell the delicious salt air of the sea.

But, alas, she met with a hindrance. Between her and the coast there was a valley extending for miles, and poor Eepersip would have to clamber down a precipitous cliff, through the valley, and up another cliff. Down she went, rather unwillingly but knowing that she would get there sometime. At last she came to the bottom. It wasn’t so bad down there—there was a lovely lakelet at which she refreshed herself with a drink; it was grassy, and there were flowers. But it was stiflingly hot. There was a patch of pine woods here and there, but it was hot even in the shade of the great trees.

===Chinese===
伊佩西普（Eepersip）一路前行，草地在她心里渐渐淡去，大海模样的记忆却越来越亮。此刻她看不见海，因为她正在一道山谷里；但她知道自己走的方向没错。春风吹拂着，不算冷，微风轻轻搅动着空气，便也不热。草地上的气息偶尔随风送到伊佩西普身边；可是走出大约两英里之后，这香气便断了。

那天下午，她走进一座大森林，林中奇异而神秘的影子来来去去，样子吓人。她拼命加快脚步，却还是不得不在林中过夜——一个叫人心惊胆战的、可怕的夜晚。第二天她走出来时，已因在密密的树丛里开路而衣衫褴褛、狼狈不堪。有好几次她不得不蹚水过河——有几条河上没有桥，所幸都没有没过她的头顶。又一天过去了。暮色降临时，她正疲惫地爬一座很高的山。月亮的倒影给她指出海洋的所在。它看上去几乎不比先前更近！

第三天早晨，她下到一道富饶肥沃的山谷。一条小溪在谷中蜿蜒而下，垂柳浸入水流的地方，溪水汩汩歌唱。这是伊佩西普迄今走过的最宽的一道山谷。可是走了许久，她来到谷口，迎面是一堵高峻陡峭的崖壁。她顺着崖壁往上爬，脚趾抠进岩石间的裂缝。终于爬到了顶——那是好长好累人的一程攀爬。此刻她站在一座青草葱茏的山上，雏菊盛开，亮得像星星，还有金黄金黄的小毛茛。蝴蝶也来了，翅膀绚烂，在花间悬停、翻飞。看哪！海洋就在那里，近多了，阳光照在上面；伊佩西普看得见浪头翻滚、白沫飞涌。尖利的鸣声刺破天空——是鸟儿的叫声，是海鸥兜着大圈向内陆俯冲。她拨着起伏的草浪往前走，闻得到大海那沁人的咸涩气息。

可是，唉，她遇到了阻碍。在她与海岸之间横着一道山谷，绵延数英里，可怜的伊佩西普得爬下一面陡崖，穿过山谷，再爬上另一面崖壁。她下去了，心里老大不情愿，却知道总归会到的。终于到了谷底。底下倒也不坏——有一个可爱的小湖，她喝了口水，提了提神；遍地青草，还开着花。只是闷热难当。这里那里有一片片松林，可即便在大树的荫凉里也照样热。

===Original===
She stumbled on in the almost blinding heat, clambering up the other great precipice—the wall of the valley. From the top she looked down, and, seeing again that ponderous cliff, she wondered how she could ever have got up it. Then she lay down on the grass, and in a moment was asleep.

When she awoke, the strong wind was blowing again. It made her almost fly through the cold, salty air. Before her was the long-sought ocean, with the waves rolling and the gulls swooping, diving and screaming. She flew; her feet could not stay still. She was tired no longer—she didn’t feel the smallest effects of her wearisome journey.

Suddenly she heard a sound—the magical sound of the waves as they crashed on the rocks. In they would come, pounding, roaring, breaking upon the shore. The foam and spume would fly back and leap up into the air. Everything sounded strange—stranger than anything Eepersip had ever heard. No words can describe what she imagined. She never had had such a lot of emotions in her head at the same time. She tried to describe them to herself, but soon gave it up as useless. She thought: “Here I am; I see it; you don’t need to tell me about it!” And then she realized that she was alone, knowing in her own mind what it was like, yet unable to stop wishing that she could describe the hollow, ringing sound. Was she becoming homesick? No! it was sheer delight.

For a moment she paused. Then she bounded through the yellow sand, and, ever going faster and faster, she came to the edge of her sea. Her longing had been fulfilled.

===Chinese===
她在几乎叫人发昏的暑热里跌跌撞撞往前走，攀上另一座大峭壁——山谷的墙。从顶上往下望，又看见那面笨重的崖壁，她纳闷自己究竟是怎么爬上来的。随后她往草地上一躺，转眼便睡着了。

她醒来时，大风又刮了起来。风载着她，几乎是在冷冽咸涩的空气里飞。眼前就是那寻了许久的大海，波浪翻滚，海鸥俯冲、扎水、尖叫。她飞奔起来；双脚怎么也停不住。她再也不累了——那一路风尘的劳顿，连一丝一毫也感觉不到了。

忽然她听见一种声音——浪头砸在岩石上的那种有魔力的声响。它们滚滚而来，捶击着，咆哮着，在岸边碎开。浪花水沫向后飞溅，跃上半空。一切都听起来那样新奇——比伊佩西普听过的任何东西都新奇。她想象中的情景，没有言语能够描摹。她还从来没有同时怀有过这么多情绪。她想给自己一一描述它们，但很快便放弃了，觉得无用。她想：「我在这儿呢；我看见了；用不着谁来告诉我！」随后她意识到自己是独自一人，心里明白那是怎么回事，却止不住盼望能把那空洞而铿锵的声响说给别人听。她是想家了吗？不！这纯粹是欢喜。

她驻足了一瞬。随后她蹦跳着穿过黄沙，越跑越快，终于来到她的海边。她的渴望实现了。

===Original===
This beach was almost overhung at one end by a great shelf of rock. The sand was glistening with shells of all colours and bordered with seaweeds washed up. Tiny sandpipers’ tracks ran all over it. Eepersip stayed there a long time, gazing into the waves, gazing at everything.

The rock-ledge at one end of the beach had been catching her eye for some time. She watched how fearlessly the gulls plunged on quivering wings, down, down, then rose again, covered with silvery drops, to fly here and there. Then she would look back at the little precipice. She thought: “*I* cannot fly! *They* do it from the air, but I cannot. I can do it from the precipice! Why not?” Then, aloud: “I *will* be a bird—I *will* do it!”

She walked back to the point where the cliff towered from the beach. She climbed up. She selected, in the water so far below, a place that was free from the treacherous-looking rocks. Then, swaying her arms a moment and plucking up high courage, she gave a flying leap and landed in the deep water.

Another miracle! She had never had a chance to swim before, but somehow she did it naturally now. It was an instinct in her to kick with her legs and throw out her arms in the right way. Fortunately she had landed in the place without rocks. Shaking herself in imitation of the gulls, so that silvery drops flew from her in all directions, she began to swim about. She played in the water for a time, entranced, singing as she had never done before, even in the meadow. After a while she came out, all shining, laughing and dancing. But it was then too late in the day to play any more; so she lay down on the sand, well out of reach of the tide, and slept, with the murmuring of the sea in her ears all night.

===Chinese===
这片沙滩的一端几乎被一面巨大的岩架压在头顶上。沙地被五颜六色的贝壳映得闪闪发亮，边上镶着冲上岸来的海草。小小的矶鹬足迹布满沙滩。伊佩西普在那里待了很久，凝望浪头，凝望一切。

沙滩一端的石梁引了她好一阵子的注意。她看着海鸥怎样无所畏惧地振着颤动的翅膀俯冲下去，向下，再向下，然后又浑身披着银亮的水珠飞起来，四处翱翔。这时她便回头望那座小小的峭壁。她想：「*我*不会飞！*它们*是从空中飞的，我不会。我可以从峭壁上飞呀！为什么不行？」随后她出了声：「我*一定*要当一只鸟——我*一定*要飞起来！」

她走回峭壁从沙滩上拔地而起的地方，爬了上去。她在下面极深的水里选定一处地方，那里没有那些看着阴险的岩石。然后，她把双臂摆了几摆，鼓足大大的勇气，腾身一跃，落进了深水里。

又一个奇迹！她从前从来没有机会游泳，可这会儿不知怎么竟无师自通。用腿蹬水、把双臂按着正确的姿势划出去，仿佛是她与生俱来的天性。幸亏她落在了没有岩石的地方。她学海鸥的样子抖了抖身子，银亮的水珠便朝四面八方飞散，随后她游动起来。她在水里玩了一阵，如痴如醉，唱着歌，连在草地上时也不曾这样唱过。过了一会儿她上了岸，浑身晶亮，又笑又跳。可这时天色已晚，玩不成什么了；于是她在沙滩上躺下——离潮水远远的——睡熟了，耳边整夜是大海的絮语。

---

===Original===
It had been high tide; but the tide was now going out, and near the beach the tops of the great rocks were appearing. To Eepersip, who had never before been near the ocean, these things which happened every day were strange and delightful, and she could not look at them enough. Each wave was pure blue, topped and trimmed with spray. As the waters drew back Eepersip had to retreat; for the low tide revealed more and more rocks, and the spray that hit upon them flew back farther and farther. Gradually they were left bare and dry, and Eepersip arranged seaweeds and sea-plants in the little pools left in their hollows. When, at last, high tide came in, she sorrowed to watch them become part of the sea again. But she knew, of course, that when the tide went out other pools would be left—perhaps more than there had been before.

Among the rocks at the back of the beach Eepersip found a pool made by leaping spray from a storm. She trimmed it with seaweeds of brown and green. She took some of the dried low-tide snails from the rocks around it and cast them into the sea. With her hands she caught some sluggish yet pretty little fishes and put them into her pool. As she was doing this she noticed how the tide was coming in—she had been so intent upon her task that she hadn’t seen it. It was now almost up to her. She stopped what she was doing and watched it anxiously, afraid that it was going to reach her pool. But, to her great joy, it didn’t. The waves lapped as if they wanted it very much, but they couldn’t quite touch it; and Eepersip, worried no longer, continued her happy playing.

In this way the days passed, with something new all the time. But she did not forget her little pool. She tended it, putting in fresh plants and rocks, and replacing a fish if it died.

She slept in a crevice in the rocks at the end of the beach. There was a tunnel under the rocks that the water had cut; if she crept to the farther end, no tide could reach her. There was a spring in the pasture in back of the beach, about a hundred yards away, and there Eepersip got her supply of fresh water. It made a merry brooklet which ran bubbling down a small hill and into the sea. When it was stormy she had a habit of merely snuggling under the rocks as far as she could go, to watch the glistening whitecaps and listen to the crashing surf. But before she had seen many storms she stayed out when they weren’t too severe, and sometimes played about in the waves—and she liked to be ducked.

===Chinese===
刚才还是满潮；此刻潮水退了下去，近岸的大岩石顶渐渐露了出来。对从没到过海边的伊佩西普来说，这些每天都要发生的事既新奇又可爱，怎么看也看不够。每一道浪都是纯净的蓝，顶上镶着浪花的白边。海水退下去时，伊佩西普只好跟着后撤；因为落潮露出的岩石越来越多，砸在岩石上的浪花也越飞越远。渐渐地，岩石被晾得又干又裸，伊佩西普便在岩石凹处留下的小水洼里布置起海草和海藻来。等到满潮终于涌回，她难过地看着它们重新变回大海的一部分。不过她当然知道，潮水退去之后还会留下别的水洼——说不定比先前还多。

在沙滩后面的岩石间，伊佩西普发现了一个由风暴的飞沫砸成的水洼。她用褐色和绿色的海草给它镶了边。她从周围岩石上拾起几只退潮晒干的螺，把它们抛回大海。她又用手捉了几条懒洋洋却很好看的小鱼，放进自己的水洼。正忙着，她注意到潮水涨上来了——她太专心于手头的活计，竟没有看见。潮水这会儿几乎要漫到她跟前了。她停下手里的事，不安地望着，生怕它淹到她的水洼。可是，令她大喜过望的是，没有。浪头舔啊舔的，仿佛万分想要，却怎么也够不着；伊佩西普不再担心，继续她快活的玩耍。

日子就这样过去，时时都有新鲜事。可她没有忘记自己的小水洼。她照料着它，添上新的海草和石子，哪条鱼死了，就再补上一条。

她睡在沙滩尽头岩石间的一道缝隙里。岩石底下有一条被水凿出的隧道；只要爬到最里头，再大的潮也够不着她。沙滩后面的牧场上有一眼泉，离着大约一百码，伊佩西普的淡水便取自那里。泉水果成一条欢快的小溪，汩汩地淌下一座小丘，流进大海。起风暴的时候，她习惯尽量往岩石深处一缩，看闪闪发亮的白浪头，听轰然砸碎的浪涛。可没见过几场风暴之后，只要风暴不算太猛，她就待在外头，有时还在浪里玩耍——她喜欢被浪头按进水里。

---

===Original===
In her explorations along the shore one day Eepersip found a great raft, made from interlacing twigs and plastered over with clay and pitch. Here and there great water-soaked ropes bound it firmly. It had been washed up on the shore, and from a long period in the sea, had become terribly slimy and waterlogged. Eepersip hauled it to the water to see if it would hold her weight, but it sank immediately. So she let it dry off in the sun for a long time; and at last, when it had become quite dry, she tried again. This time it held her. It started drifting off to sea with her on it, but she quickly slipped off and took it to shore again. A few days afterward Eepersip found a board, about three feet long and broad enough to serve perfectly as a paddle.

That was what she had wanted. She hauled the raft out to her depth, climbed on to it, took the paddle, and pushed off merrily.

Under strong strokes the water whirled and rushed, and the raft pushed through it. Sometimes she came to a sand-flat, and again to such a deep place that when she looked down all she could see was menacing shadows. Once the raft came into a shoal of carmine-coloured fishes with very long pointed fins. Of course, they scattered in all directions as she came amongst them.

When she had started it was dawn. By midday, with the help of a favourable wind, she was out of sight of land. Then she saw that, if she were going to get back to the beach by evening, she must hurry and use her remaining daylight in that direction. So she turned about, with great difficulty because of the wind, and then she started homeward. But everything that had before been favourable was now against her; with her clumsy craft she could make no headway, and the waves were rising higher all the time. So she gave up, thinking that possibly the wind would soon change or calm down altogether. But this did not happen.

She was dashed about wildly, ever going farther from land, and seeing nothing save the unlimited expanse of rough water. Yet, even in her fright, she enjoyed it. She was not hurt at all, and she had only to cling tight to the raft. The sensation of being so dashed about and of riding up and down on the waves was glorious.

All the same, when it began to grow darker, darker, the wind remaining steady, she began to wish she had not ventured forth, but had stayed in shelter and safety at her little beach. She had always had great fun watching the storms, the high spray, the wind-tossed gulls; but now she saw that she had wished for rather too much.

It became steadily blacker, and still she was borne on, making no resistance now, for she saw how useless it was. By the faint remaining light from where the sun had set, she saw ahead of her a dark pointed object rising out of the water. She knew that it was a rock; and, afraid of being dashed against it, she began resisting with her paddle. Extreme fright made her strokes powerful, and she actually managed to slow up the raft a little. She came gently against the edge of the rock, fastened her raft to it by means of one of the ropes, and climbed up to its peak. From there, the sea, with its wild waves, was like the sky, full of weird cloud-caves, fringed with light from a hidden moon.

She looked for a long time; she looked steadily. And then, not far off, she saw a dark mass which, outlined against the deep blue of the night sky, appeared to be land—blessed land! She realized that the waves were going straight toward it. With a cry of joy, she unfastened her raft, leaped upon it, gave a useless push with her paddle, and went on.

Soon she came to the shore—a smooth beach. She pulled up her raft, well out of reach of the advancing tide, and started for the bushes to find a place to sleep. For the first time since night had fallen, she noticed the wondrous beauty of the moon, almost full, and the stars that showed faintly their silvery faces. She crawled in among the bushes, and, watching all these lovely things and listening to the soft murmuring of the waves, which were now calm again, she fell into a deep, delicious sleep.

===Chinese===
一天，沿着海岸探察的伊佩西普发现了一只大木筏，用交错的树枝扎成，外面糊着黏土和沥青。几根浸透了水的大缆绳东一道西一道，把它捆得结结实实。它是被冲上岸的，在海水里泡得太久，已经滑腻不堪、灌满了水。伊佩西普把它拖下水，看它可载得住她的分量，它却立刻沉了。于是她让它在太阳底下晒了很久；到它彻底干透，她又试了一回。这一回它载住她了。筏子载着她开始往海里漂，她赶紧滑下来，把它又弄回岸上。几天之后，伊佩西普找到一块木板，约三英尺长，宽度正好做一只桨。

这正是她想要的东西。她把木筏拖到没顶的深水处，爬上去，拿起桨，欢欢喜喜地划了出去。

她用力划着，水打着旋、涌着流，木筏破水而行。有时她来到一片浅沙洲，有时又来到深得吓人的地方，朝下一望，满眼只是森然可怖的影子。有一回，木筏闯进一群洋红色小鱼中间，它们的鳍又长又尖。当然，她一闯进鱼群，它们便朝四面八方散开了。

出发时是黎明。到晌午，借着顺风，她已望不见陆地。这时她明白，若要在天黑前赶回那片沙滩，就得抓紧，把剩下的日光都用在这件事上。于是她掉转方向，逆着风好不容易才转过来，往回划去。可是先前帮着她的一切，如今都跟她作对；驾着这只笨拙的筏子，她寸步难行，浪头还一浪高过一浪。她只得由它去了，心想风也许很快就会转向，或者干脆平息下来。可是并没有。

她被疯狂地抛来掷去，离陆地越来越远，除了无边无际的滔滔浊浪什么也看不见。然而，纵然心惊，她也觉得受用。她一点儿也没受伤，只消紧紧扒住木筏。这样被抛来掷去、在浪头上升起降下的感觉，真是痛快极了。

尽管如此，天色开始越来越黑、越来越黑，风却一丝不减，她开始后悔不该冒险出来，而该安然躲在自己那片小沙滩上。看风暴、看高高的浪花、看被风抛来掷去的海鸥，向来是她的一大乐事；可现在她明白了，自己指望得有点过头了。

天越来越黑，她仍被载着往前去，如今已不作抵抗，因为她看出抵抗毫无用处。借着落日处残余的微光，她看见前方水面上耸起一个尖尖的黑影。她知道那是一块岩石；她怕被撞上去，便用桨划水抵抗。极度的惊恐使她的桨有了力气，竟真的让木筏慢下来一点。她轻轻蹭上岩石的边缘，用筏上的一根缆绳把木筏系住，爬上了岩顶。从那里望去，波涛汹涌的大海宛如天空，满是奇形怪状的云洞，镶着藏起来的月亮洒下的一线光。

她望了很久；一动不动地望着。忽然，不远处，她看见一团黑黢黢的东西，映在夜空深蓝的底子上，像是陆地——有福的陆地！她看出浪头正径直朝它涌去。她欢喜地叫了一声，解开木筏，跳上去，用桨徒劳地一撑，继续前行。

很快她到了岸——一片平顺的沙滩。她把木筏拖到高处，让涨上来的潮水怎么也够不着，然后朝灌木丛走去，找个睡觉的地方。入夜以来，她第一次注意到那轮近圆的月亮妙不可言的美，和星星们隐约露出的银色脸庞。她爬进灌木丛，望着这一切可爱的事物，听着此刻重归平静的浪头的轻柔絮语，坠入了深沉而甜美的睡眠。

---

===Original===
The next day the sea was absolutely calm. The sun was shining brilliantly on the water, making it dance and sparkle. Even Eepersip, who was so accustomed to waking in a different place from where she had been yesterday, was surprised to find herself where she was, and she had to rub her eyes hard to make sure that she was not dreaming. Then the whole adventure came back to her—the raft, the windy night, the raging sea, and the happy landing on this shore. There was her raft, lying on the beach just where she had left it.

She got up and started to explore along the beach, but suddenly she stopped short in her tracks; for there, covered with climbing vines and bordered with bright little flowers, was a cottage—a little cottage in the midst of its forest of green leaves and bushes. Beautiful though it was, for a moment tears came to Eepersip’s eyes. Exactly so had her own cottage looked; through all these years she had remembered it—just how it was in every detail. But this recollection soon passed away, in the dismay of realizing that she had come to an inhabited place. It was all so beautiful—she had wanted to stay and explore—and her hopes were crushed!

She stood stock-still for a long time, looking at the cottage. Nothing stirred within. Everything was quiet—oh! so quiet. Stealthily as a mouse Eepersip crept toward it, opened the door, and went in. A house, a detested house!—one of those houses that she had run away from. Everything came back to her—those foolish coverings on the floors which they called carpets, at the windows those useless decorations called curtains. To think of it! when there was a carpet so much lovelier of green grass or of white sand—and no windows to be curtained!

It was a delightful little room, all the same; with a brownish woolly carpet, a small fireplace, and little blue curtains of a delicate material. It was quite deserted, so she decided not to let it bother her.

A small back door opened into the lovely woods at the back of the house. Quickly Eepersip made her way out into the open; and everything looked twice as lovely as before. How light it was, with all the world a window, instead of those silly little peepholes fringed about! How much more glowing everything was! Oh, nothing in a house could compare with the world of light that Eepersip lived in!

===Chinese===
第二天，大海平静极了。阳光灿烂地照在水面上，照得它舞动、闪光。连伊佩西普——她早已惯于在一个不同于昨日的地方醒来——也惊讶自己竟到了这里，不得不使劲揉揉眼睛，确认自己不是在做梦。随后整场历险回到了她心头——木筏、刮风的夜、狂怒的大海，还有在这片海岸的快活登陆。她的木筏就在沙滩上躺着，正落在她搁下它的地方。

她起身沿着沙滩探察，忽然却猛地收住脚步；因为就在那里，爬满藤蔓、镶着明亮小花的，是一座小屋——一座藏在绿叶与灌木的森林当中的小屋。它虽然美，伊佩西普的眼里却霎时涌上泪水。她自己家的小屋，从前正是这副模样；这些年来她一直记得它——每一处细节都记得清清楚楚。可这回忆很快消散了，因为她惊觉自己来到了一个有人居住的地方，一阵沮丧涌上心头。这一切多么美啊——她本想留下来探个究竟——而她的希望破灭了！

她一动不动地站了很久，望着那座小屋。屋里毫无动静。一切都静悄悄——哦！那么静。伊佩西普像老鼠一样蹑手蹑脚地挨过去，推开门，走了进去。一座房子，一座可恨的房子！——正是她逃掉的那些房子中的一座。一切都回到她记忆里——地上那些他们叫做地毯的愚蠢的覆盖物，窗上那些叫做帘子的无用的装饰。想想吧！明明有绿草或白沙织成的可爱得多的地毯——而且根本没有窗户要挂帘子！

话虽如此，那还是一间讨人喜欢的小屋子；棕色的绒地毯，小小的壁炉，还有质地轻柔的蓝色小窗帘。屋里空无一人，于是她决定不为它烦心。

一扇小小的后门通往屋后可爱的树林。伊佩西普快步走到露天里；一切看上去比先前美了一倍。多亮堂啊——整个世界都是窗，而不是那些镶了边的小小的傻窥孔！一切都鲜亮了多少啊！哦，房子里的任何东西，都比不上伊佩西普栖身的这个光明的世界！

===Original===
Out here, the sunbeams made shadows wherever they struck; the birds twittered; the ripples lapped the shore caressingly. Otherwise all was still. But she was not thinking of the sea: she had decided to explore the woodland, for she felt, in a way, that it was her home. Following a little winding path, she came through a grove of white pines carpeted with needles and dotted with gnome-like toadstools of red and yellow, looking very bright and mysterious in that shady place. They were, to Eepersip, like the traces of some elfin revel, perhaps thrones of precious mineral. There were great boulders, too, covered with grey-green lichens, some bearing aloft tiny cup-like blossoms of pearly grey—the cups from which the feasters had drunk their flower-wine. Seeing a lighter place ahead, she knew that she was coming out of the pine grove. A flood of pale green radiance greeted her, as she stepped out of the dimness of the woods into a meadow. White and yellow butterflies were fluttering over it in great flocks, with wings shining. Eepersip could hear birds chirping and singing. She passed on through the meadow and came again into woodlands, so thick now that hardly a sunbeam could penetrate the dense canopy of leaves.

After a while she emerged into a clearing. In the middle of it there was a pool, almost entirely surrounded with dark green moss, very soft, overhung by a boulder. It, too, had a covering of moss. A tiny stream flowed silently and mysteriously into the pool, which was so dark that Eepersip could just see its floor of dark sand. On the bottom grew strange star-leaved plants, and small fishes were nibbling them. It was all very strange and magical, it was so silent.

Eepersip stayed looking at this pool for a long time, and then she decided to follow the little brook which was trickling into it and see where it came from. She followed it through deep woodland for about three miles. All this way it was sluggish. Then the land changed abruptly; and Eepersip realized that she was climbing a steep and rugged hill. She went up and up on a rough path. It was very hard climbing, and she was becoming tired. At last she got to the top, and her happy eye looked back upon the way she had come.

She saw from that high perch the pool, into which she knew the little brook was trickling; the blotches which were clumps and patches of dark forest; the field, a mass of sparkling green light, a brilliant illumination to the gloomy pine forests around it; the cottage, a tiny brown speck in the distance—and the sea, the billowing sea, with the spots of foam, the towering waves, and that green colour which the waves show when they are agitated. She could even see the gulls, no bigger than flies to her, swooping about; but she was too far away to hear their shrill, excited screams. Long and steadily she looked. And then—the strangest thought Eepersip had ever experienced came to her happy mind. “Forgetfulness!” she whispered to herself. “Oh, I loved it so! and then, when it happened that I came to the woodlands again, why—I forgot it. I must go back instantly. But I am *so* tired!”

===Chinese===
在这外头，阳光照到哪里，就在哪里投下影子；鸟儿啁啾；细浪爱抚地舔着岸边。除此之外万籁俱寂。但她心里想的不是海：她已决定去探察树林，因为不知怎么，她觉得那就是她的家。沿一条蜿蜒的小径，她穿过一片白松林，地上铺着松针，点缀着红红黄黄、地精模样的毒菌，在那荫翳之处显得又鲜亮又神秘。在伊佩西普眼里，它们像是一场精灵欢宴的痕迹，说不定还是贵重矿石雕成的宝座。还有巨大的圆石，覆着灰绿的地衣，有的高高擎起珍珠灰的杯状小花——那正是宴客们饮花酒的杯子。看见前面亮起来，她知道自己快走出松林了。一泓淡绿的清辉迎着她，她从林中的幽暗里踏进一片草地。白蝴蝶黄蝴蝶成群地在草地上翻飞，翅膀闪着光。伊佩西普听得见鸟儿鸣啭歌唱。她穿过草地，又走进树林；这时的林子密得几乎没有一缕阳光能穿透浓密的叶幕。

过了一会儿，她来到一片林间空地。空地中央有一个水潭，四周几乎全围着墨绿的软苔，头顶探着一块巨石。石头上也盖着一层苔。一道细细的水流悄然地、神秘地淌进潭里；潭水黑沉沉的，伊佩西普勉强看得见潭底的黑沙。底上长着叶子像星星的怪植物，小鱼正一点点啃着它们。一切都那样古怪、那样有魔力，又是那样安静。

伊佩西普守着这个水潭看了很久，然后决定沿着那条涓涓淌入的小溪逆流而上，看它是从哪里来的。她跟着它穿过深林，走了约莫三英里。这一路溪水都懒洋洋的。随后地势陡然一变；伊佩西普发觉自己在爬一座陡峭崎岖的山。她顺着一条坎坷的路向上、再向上。爬得非常吃力，她也渐渐累了。终于到了山顶，她那双快活的眼睛回望来路。

从那高高的栖处，她望见那个水潭——她知道小溪正涓涓注入其间；望见一块块深色森林的团簇；望见那片田野，一团闪闪烁烁的绿光，把四周阴郁的松林照得透亮；望见那座小屋，远处一粒褐色的小点——还有大海，波涛滚滚的大海，带着点点白沫、耸立的浪头，还有波浪激动时才泛出的那种绿色。她甚至看得见海鸥，在她眼里不比苍蝇大，四处翻飞；可她离得太远，听不见它们尖利而兴奋的鸣叫。她久久地、一动不动地望着。随后——伊佩西普平生最奇怪的一个念头，落进她快活的脑袋里。「忘性！」她悄悄对自己说。「哦，我从前多爱它呀！可后来，等我碰巧又回到树林里来，你瞧——我就把它忘了。我得马上回去。可是我*太*累了！」

===Original===
Each wave seemed to bring a pain to Eepersip’s heart, as she watched the sea, like emerald, stretching away until it seemed to meet the blue sky. Suddenly she sprang to her feet and started down like a wild deer. Tearing through the woodlands, through the dense thickets and the brambles, she came out at last by the pool. But she had no eye for all its beauties; she had no mind but for the sea. She rested a second; then she was on her feet again, plunging, rearing, fighting her way through the woods. She came again, in the depths of exhaustion, into that pool of light, the meadow. Unable to move, she sank down in the delicious soft grass and watched the butterflies, like winged jewels, swooping above. Then she fell into a deep, heavy slumber.

She was awakened by shrill cries which pierced the air. Looking up, she saw a flock of gulls with their long, narrow wings, the colour of foam, winging their way toward the sea. Then she remembered that she, too, was supposed to be winging her way toward the sea, and she cried: “O happy birds, I would I were among you, to go with such flashing speed!” It seemed to her that the sea was in her care, and that she, through foolish forgetfulness, had wandered off from it—wandered off from her guarding, leaving it to the mercy of the beasts. Of course, if she had thought a moment she would have seen how out of proportion this was, but she could do nothing but blame herself, and fancy a terrible monster who would come and drink it all up in her absence. And she began fighting and struggling against her tiredness, until, with one desperate effort, she managed to start running again. Then there was no stopping! Her old strength seemed to come back, the strength which she had had before starting her woodland explorations—the result, as she thought now, of a foolish desire. Once she had started running again, her feet winged with a great longing, she sped along the ground.

Soon she passed the cottage; and then—there was her sea again, just as she had left it, with the waves beating the sandy shore. The gulls were screaming and diving; everything was excited and trembling. With a cry of ecstasy, Eepersip sprang into the waves.

===Chinese===
她望着大海像翡翠一样伸展出去，直到仿佛与蓝天相接，每一道浪仿佛都往伊佩西普心里送去一阵疼痛。突然她一跃而起，像一头小鹿般向下奔去。她闯过树林，闯过密密的树丛和荆棘，终于来到水潭边。可潭子的种种美，她一眼也无心去看；她心里只有大海。她歇了一秒；随即又上了路，跌撞着、腾跃着，一路在树林里杀出一条道。等她再一次来到那光之潭——草地时，已是筋疲力尽。她一动也不能动，倒进柔软可爱的草里，望着蝴蝶像生了翅膀的珠宝在头顶翻飞。随后她坠入深沉酣沉的睡眠。

尖利的鸣声刺破天空，把她惊醒。抬头望去，她看见一群海鸥，长长的窄翅膀是浪花的颜色，正振翅飞向大海。这时她想起，自己也正该飞向大海去，便喊道：「哦幸福的鸟儿，我真愿与你们同行，用这样闪光的速度飞去！」在她看来，大海是在她的照看之下的，而她由于愚蠢的忘性，竟从它身边走开了——离开了她的看守，把它丢给野兽们发落。当然，只要她细想一分钟，就会看出这念头多么不成比例；可她只会责怪自己，还会幻想出一个可怕的怪物，趁她不在便来把大海喝个精光。于是她开始与疲乏搏斗、挣扎，直到凭着一股拼死的劲头，重新跑了起来。这一来就再也停不下了！她旧日的力气仿佛回来了——那是在她开始探察树林之前曾有过的力气，如今想来，那不过是一场愚蠢念头的产物。一旦重新跑起来，她的双脚便怀着巨大的渴望生出翅膀，贴着地面飞掠。

很快她掠过了那座小屋；随后——她的大海又在那里了，正如她离开时的模样，浪头捶打着沙滩。海鸥尖叫着、扎着水；一切都兴奋得发颤。伊佩西普欢叫一声，跃进了浪里。

---

===Original===
Many happy days Eepersip spent here, living in the vicinity of the hated little cottage. Since she had come from the sea she had worn a mermaid dress of seaweeds, fastened at the neck by a white shell with a hole through it. Her favourite play was with the waves. She could swim now, even under water, with a speed that surprised herself, and she dived gracefully from all the rocks that she came upon. But it was watching the sea that fascinated her more than anything else. She would sit for hours at a time on the rocks and listen to the waves bellowing beneath her. Sometimes, when they were very high, she would go down on the low rocks and shout with delight when the white spray rushed along and whirled itself up into her face. The waves would wash her over and over and play with her in their salty hands, and, though they seemed rough and wild, something always guided her away from the treacherous rocks which they headed for.

But she was born to wander, and it was not long before she was off on her explorations again. One sparkling day when the sun danced and glimmered on the little ripples, Eepersip started to explore the shoreline. Every sun-sparkle made her feel happier and happier, and every breath of salty air lighter, until at last she thought she must rise up into the air on strong wings. After exploring quite a while and finding nothing unusual, she sat down on a rock. Her auburn curls goldened in the sunlight, and her brown eyes sparkled.

After she had rested a while, watching the swooping seagulls, she decided to collect shells. She went along the beach some way, picking up shells and pebbles. But soon she tired of this and, feeling very hot, flung herself into the sea and played a while in the shallow water. Soon she thought that she would like to take a long swim, and she started out rapidly.

The waves came in higher and higher and brought with them great flocks of gulls sweeping around in wide graceful circles and uttering strange wild cries. Eepersip went on a long way until she saw a great rock ahead, draped with seaweeds of a dark green which were floating up and down with the motion of the waves. There were many crabs and snails caught in them. She was borne forward to the rock in a mighty wave, and, clinging to it hard, she waited until the wave drew back before climbing up. After she had rested some time she noticed a shoal of shining little fishes down in the water. Some were gold, some silver, and some had bands of dark blue. They all had ruby eyes. She watched them for a long time, lying on her stomach on the rock. She observed how they nosed down and fed on the oozy sea-plants on the bottom, which were covered with silver oxygen-bubbles. Also she could see, ’way down there, lovely bright corals of all colours. The water was rather muddy, but there was a current coming in underneath, and before long it was perfectly clear. The rock was tremendous, spreading out beneath the surface and going down, down, all covered with slime and seaweeds. Eepersip was fascinated watching those little fishes: she cared for nothing else. How long might she have watched them if the tide had not been rising and rising? Now it was touching her dress when a ripple larger than the others came in. And now—a flash of lightning down there in the shadows! Eepersip could not realize what had happened. Then she thought: a great brownish-green fish had shot into the middle of the shoal, seized one of them, and carried it off. It was so quick that Eepersip could not think, until some time after it was all over, what had really happened.

===Chinese===
伊佩西普在这里度过了许多快活的日子，住在那座可恨的小屋附近。自从来到海边，她便穿着一件海草织成的美人鱼裙，领口用一只带孔的白贝壳扣着。她最爱的游戏是同浪头玩耍。如今她会游泳了，连在水底也能游，速度快得叫她自己都吃惊；无论遇上哪块岩石，她都能优雅地跳水。但最令她着迷的，还是看海。她能一连几个钟头坐在岩石上，听浪在脚下吼啸。有时浪头很高，她便走到低处的岩石上，等白色的浪花奔涌而来、打着旋扑上她的脸时，快活得大喊大叫。浪头把她一遍又一遍地冲刷，用咸咸的手掌同她玩耍；它们虽然显得又粗暴又狂野，却总有一样什么东西，引着她避开它们冲向的那些暗藏杀机的岩石。

可她生来就要流浪，没过多久，她又出发探察去了。一个闪闪发亮的日子，太阳在小小的涟漪上跳舞、闪光，伊佩西普动身去探察海岸线。每一粒阳光的碎金都让她越发快活，每一口咸涩的空气都让她越发轻快，到最后她觉得自己准要凭着强壮的翅膀飞上天去了。探察了好一阵，没发现什么特别的，她便在一块岩石上坐下。她赤褐色的鬈发在阳光里镀成了金色，棕色的眼睛闪闪发亮。

她歇了一会儿，望着俯冲的海鸥，随后决定去捡贝壳。她沿着沙滩走出一段路，一路拾着贝壳和卵石。可不久她就腻了，觉得燥热，便一头扎进海里，在浅水里玩了一会儿。很快她又想好好游一趟远水，便飞快地游了出去。

浪头一浪高过一浪地涌来，还带来了一大群海鸥，兜着又宽又优雅的圈子盘旋，发出野性的怪叫。伊佩西普游出去很远，直到看见前方一块巨岩，披挂着墨绿的海草，随着浪的起伏上下漂摆，缠着许多螃蟹和螺。一道大浪把她送到岩石跟前，她紧紧攀住，等浪退了才爬上去。歇了些时候，她注意到水下有一群闪闪发亮的小鱼。有的金，有的银，有的带着深蓝的条纹，全都长着红宝石的眼睛。她趴在岩石上看了它们很久。她看它们怎样把头扎下去，啃食水底软泥里的海藻，藻上覆着银色的氧气珠。她还看得见老深老深的地方，那些可爱又鲜亮的珊瑚，什么颜色都有。水相当浑，可底下有一股水流涌进来，不久便清亮极了。那块岩石大极了，在水面下铺展开去，向下、再向下，全长满了黏泥和海草。伊佩西普看那些小鱼看得入了迷：别的什么都顾不上了。若不是潮水一涨再涨，她还要看多久呢？这会儿，浪已经舔到她的裙子了，一道比别的都大的涟漪涌了进来。紧接着——阴影里一道闪电！伊佩西普一时明白不过来出了什么事。随后她想起来了：一条棕绿色的大鱼射进鱼群中央，叼住其中一条，带走了。一切快得叫伊佩西普来不及想，直到事情过去好一阵，才明白究竟发生了什么。

===Original===
She swam to the shore, but, to her surprise, it was quite a different shore from where she had started. She wondered where she was. She landed on a beach of white sand, so fine that it was impossible to hold. It was covered with shells of all colours. These interested her for a long time, and she piled up the whole beach with heaps of them that she had collected, and had a beautiful time playing with herself until—

She saw some footprints! *Footprints!* They came down on the beach and apparently into the water, then out again, and disappeared in the woods on a narrow path which Eepersip had not noticed before.

But she was not interested in where they went to or where they came from. Her only thought was to get away—away. It was then too late to go out in the sea again—that is, far from shore. The sun was about to set. She would spend the night there, and then she would wander again. So she lay down and went to sleep.

===Chinese===
她朝岸边游去，可叫她吃惊的是，这海岸跟她出发的地方全然不同。她不知道自己到了哪里。她登上了一片白沙滩，沙细得根本攥不住。滩上覆满五颜六色的贝壳。这些东西让她着了许久的迷，她把捡来的贝壳一堆堆垒起来，摆满了整片沙滩，独自玩得好不开心，直到——

她看见了一些脚印！*脚印！*它们从沙滩上下来，显然下到水里，又从水里出来，随后顺着一条伊佩西普先前没留意的小径，消失在树林里。

可它们通向哪里、来自哪里，她全不关心。她唯一的念头就是走开——快走开。这时再出海已经太晚了——指的是远离海岸。太阳快落了。她要在那里过一夜，然后再去流浪。于是她躺下睡着了。

===Original===
The next morning when she woke up she was not alone. A little golden-haired boy with sky-blue eyes was looking at her. They looked at each other for a long time.

“Who are you?” he ventured at last.

Here was a puzzler. “Eepersip Eigleen,” she answered. “I mean,” she added doubtfully, “I *was*.”

“Who are you now, then?”

“I don’t know exactly.”

“Why don’t you?”

“I haven’t any name now. I’m just somebody. Have *you* any name?”

“Yes—Toby—Toby Carrenda.”

===Chinese===
第二天早晨她醒来时，身边不是独自一人。一个金头发、天蓝色眼睛的小男孩正望着她。他们彼此对望了很久。

「你是谁？」他终于壮着胆子问。

这可是个难题。「伊佩西普·伊格琳（Eepersip Eigleen）。」她答道。「我是说，」她迟疑地补了一句，「我*从前*是。」

「那你现在是谁？」

「我也说不准。」

「怎么会不知道呢？」

「我现在没有名字了。我就是一个人罢了。*你*有名字吗？」

「有——托比（Toby）——托比·卡伦达（Carrenda）。」

===Original===
“Do you live here in the woods?”

“Yes.”

“In a house?”

He looked at her curiously a moment; then he said: “Yes, of course—don’t you?”

“**No**!”

“How funny!”

“Yes, it is.” With a little reluctance—“Will you play with me?”

===Chinese===
「你住在这树林里吗？」

「是。」

「住房子里？」

他好奇地看了她一会儿；然后说：「是啊，当然——难道你不吗？」

「**不**！」

「多奇怪呀！」

「是啊，是奇怪。」她有点迟疑——「你愿意跟我玩吗？」

===Original===
Strange: here was Eepersip, who detested people, asking a little boy to play with her! It was simply that she, not having seen any children for a long time, was fascinated by this small boy who seemed so unafraid of her and so natural.

They wandered together on the beach and picked up shells. Then Eepersip asked the little boy if he liked to swim.

“Yes,” he said. “But do you think I’d better?”

“Yes—why not?”

“All right.”

So he took off all his clothes and went in with her, and they splashed each other and had a lovely time. Eepersip wanted to make him a mermaid dress, but there was no seaweed right there, and she didn’t want to leave him. So they went into the woods to find some ferns to make him a nymph dress. She found a beautiful ferny glade, and sat down and began to weave ferns together, talking to him at the same time. When it was all done he was delighted.

===Chinese===
奇怪：讨厌人类的伊佩西普，竟主动请一个小男孩跟她玩！其实不过是因为，她许久没见过孩子了，而这个小小的男孩似乎全然不怕她、又那样自然，让她着了迷。

他们一起在沙滩上溜达、捡贝壳。随后伊佩西普问小男孩喜不喜欢游泳。

「喜欢，」他说。「可你觉得我去合适吗？」

「合适——为什么不去？」

「那好吧。」

于是他脱光了衣裳，跟她下了水，两人互相泼水，玩得快活极了。伊佩西普想给他也做一件美人鱼裙，可手边没有海草，她又不愿离开他。于是他们走进树林，去找些蕨叶，好给他做一件宁芙裙。她找到一片长满蕨草的漂亮林间空地，坐下来动手把蕨叶编到一起，一面同他说话。裙子做好时，他欢喜极了。

===Original===
“But, please,” he said, “can’t I have a shell, too?”

He touched the shell strung up on her seaweed dress. They looked all over the beach, and at last they found another shell with a hole all the way through. Then he was entirely content.

They went into the woods together and picked flowers, and Eepersip showed him how to make fern dresses and how to weave wreaths of flowers. They went into a grove of sunlit white pines and danced there together. Finally the little boy said: “I’m hungry, Eeserpip.”

“It’s *Eepersip*,” she said, “but it doesn’t matter much. I’ll find you something to eat.” After a while they found some flame-coloured berries, and then Eepersip dug up some white roots of which she was fond.

The boy said: “This is jolly, it is. Is this the way you get your food?”

“Always,” she said.

They played a while longer, and then someone called.

===Chinese===
「可是，求求你，」他说，「我也能有一只贝壳吗？」

他碰了碰她海草裙上系着的那只贝壳。他们找遍了整个沙滩，终于又找到一只带通孔的贝壳。这下他完全心满意足了。

他们一同走进树林采花，伊佩西普教他怎样做蕨叶裙、怎样编花环。他们走进一片阳光朗照的白松林，在那里一起跳舞。最后小男孩说：「我饿了，伊瑟西普。」

「是*伊佩西普*。」她说，「不过也差不多啦。我去给你找点吃的。」过了一阵，他们找到一些火红色的浆果，随后伊佩西普又挖了几条她爱吃的白根。

男孩说：「真带劲，太带劲了。你平日就是这样弄到吃的吗？」

「一向如此。」她说。

他们又玩了一会儿，随后有人喊他了。

===Original===
Eepersip had a strange feeling at that moment. She could not help feeling a certain reluctance when she had first played with him; then she had decided that he could not have anything to do with the civilized people she hated so. He must be separate from them, perhaps even a wild thing like herself. She felt a sensation of horror when the strange voice sounded. Then he was not alone—then he lived in a house with other people!

Startled, she cried: “Who’s that?”

“My mother,” he answered.

“Then you don’t live here all by yourself?” She had a bitter feeling of disappointment.

“Oh, no.”

“I *wish* you did.” This escaped her before she could think. Strange, that some magic power in this child had already made her say as much as she had said.

“I must go now,” he said sorrowfully. “But I’ll be out this afternoon—I guess.”

===Chinese===
那一刻，伊佩西普心里生出一种奇怪的感觉。起初同他玩时，她不免有几分不情愿；后来她认定，他不可能同她如此憎恨的文明人有什么干系。他必定超然于他们之外，说不定还是同她一样的野孩子。可那陌生的嗓音响起时，她感到一阵恐怖。这么说，他不是独自一人——这么说，他同一群人住在房子里！

她吓了一跳，喊道：「那是谁？」

「我妈妈。」他答道。

「那你不是自己一个人住在这儿？」她心里涌起一阵苦涩的失望。

「哦，不是。」

「我*真希望*你是。」这话没等她想就溜出了口。奇怪，这孩子身上竟有一种魔力，已经让她说出了这么多她本不会说的话。

「我现在得走了。」他难过地说。「不过今天下午我会出来——大概吧。」

===Original===
Eepersip fell on her knees in front of him and said entreatingly: “Will you do something for me?”

“I will—maybe.”

“Don’t tell *anybody* about me.”

“Why?”

“Never mind why, but don’t, will you?”

“I want to.”

“Then I won’t play with you any more.”

“All right, Eepersip. I won’t.” She looked at him doubtfully. “I *promise* you I won’t. Goodbye. I like you.”

===Chinese===
伊佩西普在他面前跪下来，恳求道：「你能替我做一件事吗？」

「我愿意——也许。」

「别把我的事告诉*任何人*。」

「为什么？」

「别管为什么，就是别告诉，好吗？」

「我想说嘛。」

「那我就再也不跟你玩了。」

「好吧，伊佩西普。我不说。」她将信将疑地望着他。「我*向你保证*，我不说。再见。我喜欢你。」

---

===Original===
Eepersip was delighted with her little friend. She waited anxiously for him to come out. Presently he came.

“Eepersip,” he said, “will you swim with me again?”

They went in again, and this time Eepersip showed him how to swim, by holding him up while he kicked with his arms and legs. After a long time he could swim a little bit by himself; and then Eepersip took him to some rather high rocks and showed him how to jump in. At first he wouldn’t do it alone; she took his hand and they jumped in together. After that he did it alone, and screamed with laughter when he came up. Then Eepersip showed him how to go in head first, and he had so much faith in her that he tried it right off. Although he went rather flat, he liked it very much. The next time Eepersip bent him ’way over before he went in, and he straightened out and hit the water clean as an arrow. That was much better, he said.

===Chinese===
伊佩西普喜欢她的小朋友喜欢得不得了。她焦急地等他出来。不多时他来了。

「伊佩西普，」他说，「你再同我一起游泳好吗？」

他们又下了水。这一回，伊佩西普教他游泳：她托着他，让他的胳膊和腿踢打起来。练了好久，他能自己游上一小段了；随后伊佩西普带他到几块相当高的岩石上，教他怎样跳下去。起初他不肯独自跳；她牵住他的手，两人一起跳了进去。那以后他就敢自己跳了，冒出头时笑得直叫。接着伊佩西普又教他头朝下入水；他对她信得很，马上就试了。虽然入水时身子平了些，他还是非常喜欢。第二次，伊佩西普在他入水前把他弯得低低的，他一挺身，像箭一样干净利落地切入水里。他说，这回好多了。

===Original===
Eepersip asked him what his mother had said about the fern dress, for he had gone in so quickly that he had forgotten his own clothes. He said that she had asked him about it, and he had said that he found it. Eepersip thanked him for not telling about her.

But she was discovered in spite of her caution. One day when they were playing in the woods, Mrs. Carrenda came out and found them. Eepersip dashed for the waves immediately, in spite of the fact that Toby’s mother called: “Don’t run away, little girl; I won’t hurt you!”

But Toby began to cry bitterly. “Why did you send her away, Mother?”

“I didn’t, Toby. She ran as soon as I came. Who is she?”

===Chinese===
伊佩西普问他，妈妈对那条蕨叶裙说了什么——因为他进屋太快，把自己的衣裳都忘在了外头。他说妈妈问过他，他回答说是自己捡到的。伊佩西普谢他没有把她供出来。

可尽管她百般小心，还是被发现了。一天，他们正在树林里玩，卡伦达太太（Mrs. Carrenda）走了出来，撞见了他们。伊佩西普立刻朝浪头奔去，任凭托比的妈妈在身后喊：「别跑呀，小姑娘；我不会伤害你的！」

可托比伤心地哭了起来。「妈妈，你为什么把她赶走？」

「我没有赶她，托比。我一到她就跑了。她是谁呀？」

===Original===
That Toby did not answer. There were two instincts equally strong struggling within him—one to obey his mother, and the other to do what the strange girl asked him to with the threat of refusing to play with him if he did not.

“I can’t tell you, Mother,” he said courageously. It would have been as true if he had said “I don’t know,” for he knew nothing but her name, after all. However, he never stopped to think that knowing her name was not all there was to knowing *her*.

Mrs. Carrenda wisely pursued the matter no further; but she determined to keep watch.

Eepersip was much more cautious after this. She was always on the lookout. Several times Toby asked her why she didn’t want to be seen. But she would not answer him. She was, however, very kind in all other respects. Several times Mrs. Carrenda found Toby playing with her, but never spoke or let him know. She saw that Eepersip played nicely with him and that they liked each other much; so she did not interfere. Once, however, she put her hands suddenly on Eepersip’s shoulders from behind and said kindly: “Little girl, don’t be afraid of me.”

===Chinese===
这个问题托比没有回答。他心里有两种同样强烈的本能正在打架——一种是要听妈妈的话，另一种是要照那陌生女孩的嘱咐去做，因为她威胁过，不照办就不跟他玩了。

「我不能告诉你，妈妈。」他勇敢地说。其实他若说「我不知道」，也一样是真话，因为说到底，除了名字，他对她一无所知。不过他从未想过：知道了她的名字，并不等于认识*她*。

卡伦达太太识趣地没有再追问；但她打定了主意要留心观察。

打这以后，伊佩西普小心多了。她时刻提防着。托比好几次问她，为什么不愿被人看见。可她不肯回答。不过在其他方面，她待他好得很。有几次卡伦达太太撞见托比同她玩，却从不搭话，也不让他知道。她看得出伊佩西普同他玩得很好，两人彼此喜欢得很；所以她不干预。只有一回，她突然从背后把手搭上伊佩西普的肩膀，和蔼地说：「小姑娘，别怕我。」

===Original===
Eepersip sprang to her feet, stared wildly a moment, and then dashed off straight to the sea. But for fear of making Toby very unhappy, Mrs. Carrenda never questioned him about her.

She and her husband had many anxious conferences together. Her husband thought that it was exceedingly risky to let Toby play so unwatched with Eepersip, but Toby’s mother did not feel that way at all. Then they talked over the matter of who she was.

One day Eepersip was peeping into the house to see if she could find Toby, for he had not been out to play with her. Looking into the dining-room, she saw him there, eating luncheon with Mr. and Mrs. Carrenda. They were talking anxiously, and she was curious, and listened.

“I have it,” said Mr. Carrenda suddenly. “Don’t you remember those people—the Eeglines, or Eigleens—that came over to the hill near Mount Varcrobis where we lived before we came here? who wanted to know if we had seen a strange little girl, dressed all in ferns? She is the Eigleens’ lost little girl.”

===Chinese===
伊佩西普一跃而起，惶乱地瞪了一瞬，随即径直朝大海奔去。只是怕托比太难过，卡伦达太太从此不再向他盘问她的事。

她和丈夫一起忧心忡忡地商量过许多回。丈夫认为，让托比这样无人看管地同伊佩西普玩，风险极大；可托比的妈妈全然不这么想。随后他们又讨论起她究竟是谁的问题。

一天，伊佩西普正朝屋里张望，想看看能不能找到托比，因为他没有出来跟她玩。往餐室里一望，她看见他正同卡伦达先生（Mr. Carrenda）和卡伦达太太一起吃午饭。他们谈得忧心忡忡，她好奇，便听着。

「我想起来了。」卡伦达先生忽然说。「你还记得那家人吗——伊格林家，还是叫伊格琳家（Eigleen）——就是搬到我们搬来这儿之前住的那座山、瓦克罗比斯山（Mount Varcrobis）附近来的那家？他们打听我们见没见过一个浑身穿蕨叶的怪小姑娘？她就是伊格琳家走失的小女儿。」

===Original===
Mrs. Carrenda looked puzzled.

“They told us, you know, that they had given up all hope of having Ee—ee—serpip” (Toby started violently) “back again—”

“Oh yes, I remember now.”

“—When Fleuriss came, and—”

“Oh yes, it all comes back to me now. They were making a great effort to find her and entice her back home by telling her about her baby sister.”

“Yes.”

“Why, father,” said Toby, “Eepersip—” He suddenly saw her in his mind, kneeling in front of him, begging him not to tell—and he said no more. Nobody noticed his remark.

===Chinese===
卡伦达太太露出困惑的神色。

「你知道，他们跟我们说过，他们对把伊——伊——瑟西普（托比猛地一颤）找回来，已经不抱任何希望了——」

「哦对了，我想起来了。」

「——后来弗勒里丝（Fleuriss）出生了，再——」

「哦对了，这下全想起来了。他们正千方百计找她，想用她小妹妹的事把她诱回家去。」

「是啊。」

「咦，爸爸，」托比说，「伊佩西普——」他脑海里忽然浮现出她跪在他面前、求他别说出去的样子——他便不再往下说了。谁也没留意他的话。

===Original===
A moment Mrs. Carrenda gazed at her husband astounded. Then she said: “I believe it is so. Let us send word to them right off.”
